Preface

Lao She (1899~1966), originally named Shu Qingchun, courtesy name Sheyu, was a native of Beijing. He lost his father at a young age and came from a poor family, influenced by the lower class of old Beijing's lower class society, as well as their ideological views and aesthetic tastes. In 1924, he went to England to teach and began literary creation, attracting attention with his humorous style. After returning to China in 1930, he created works such as 'Rickshaw Boy' and 'Divorce,' establishing himself as the first author of urban popular literature in modern literary history. During the War of Resistance, he presided over the work of the All-China Association of Literary and Art Circles to Resist the Enemy and wrote the million-word novel "Four Generations Under One Roof." After the founding of New China, he produced other powerful plays such as 'Longxu Ditch' and 'Teahouse,' earning the title of 'People's Artist.'

The novel tells the tragic story of a rickshaw puller named Xiangzi in old Beiping.

Xiangzi drifted from the countryside to the city and became a rickshaw puller. He was young and strong, with no bad habits, full of confidence about the future. His only wish was to buy a rickshaw for himself and become an "independent" and "free" rickshaw driver. However, the three ups and downs of buying a car shattered his life ideals and plunged him into despair.

In terms of relationships, Xiangzi lost control. The daughter of Liu Siye, the rickshaw factory owner, was fond of Xiangzi. Although Xiangzi disliked her old age and ugliness, he couldn't resist her temptation and married her. After marriage, Xiangzi used Huniu's private savings to buy an old car belonging to Erqiangzi. But not long after, Huniu died in childbirth, and Xiangzi had to sell his carriage to pay for her burial. This family ruin and loss left Xiangzi deeply grieved. After Huniu's death, Xiaofuzi's attentive care made him truly fall in love with this ill-fated girl. He seemed to have the courage to go on again, but fate still liked to play tricks on him. Forced by life, Xiao Fuzi became a prostitute, and before Xiangzi could "settle down," he hanged himself. This shattered Xiangzi's last hope.

From then on, Xiangzi completely collapsed and fell into ruin. He indulged in drinking, gambling, prostitution, cheating, and even betraying lives, until he ultimately became a social scoundrel, a walking corpse, awaiting death.

"Rickshaw Boy" uses Beijing vernacular and dialect expressions, along with descriptions of old Beijing customs and customs, making it warm and natural and catchy to read. It is a classic work of modern vernacular Chinese novels.

1

What we are talking about is Xiangzi, not the camel; "camel" is just one of Xiangzi's nicknames. So, let's start with Xiangzi. As for the connection between the "Camel" and Xiangzi, I'll just casually mention it here.

There are many types of Western-style coachmen in Beiping: young, strong, agile, particular about renting beautiful rickshaws, hauling "all day," and having the freedom to send and collect whenever they want; Pull out the car and place it at a fixed "entrance"[1]

 Or when the gate is left open, waiting specifically for the master of the express train; If you do it, you might end up with a piece or two at once; By chance, maybe the whole day was wasted, and I couldn't even get a 'car share,' but I didn't care. This group of guys probably has two hopes: either charter a car; Or you can buy your own car, have your own, then buy a monthly package or a free seat—it's not a big deal, since the car is yours anyway.

Those slightly older than this group, or those who run a bit less due to health issues, or those who dare not waste a day due to family constraints, mostly drive about 80% new cars; Both the person and the car are quite attractive, so when asking for a price, it still maintains a certain level of dignity. This group's coachman might pull "all day," or maybe "half a day." In the latter case, because they still have considerable spirit, they always "pull Wan'er" regardless of winter or summer[2]

 。 At night, of course, it requires more attention and skill than during the day; Naturally, he earned more money.

Those over forty but under twenty probably find it hard to hold a position among the first two schools. Their cart was broken and they didn't dare to "pull late," so they could only leave early, hoping to move from early morning until three or four in the afternoon to pull out "the cart" and their own chewed grain. Their cars are broken and slow to run, so they walk more and spend less. Whether at the melon market, fruit market, vegetable market, or hauling goods, it was always them; Money is low, but there's no need to run fast.

Here, those under twenty—some have been in this profession since eleven or twelve—rarely become beautiful coachmen after twenty, because injuries in childhood make it hard for them to grow strong. They may have spent their whole lives pulling foreign rickshaws, but never even pulled them in the spotlight. Those over forty years old had been pulling carts for ten or eight years. Their weakened muscles made them willing to live in life, and they gradually realized that sooner or later they would stumble and die on the road. Their way of pulling carts, their quick thinking during bargaining, and their walking style—all of these evoke memories of their past glory, while they slap the newcomers with their noses. But this glory did nothing to lessen the darkness ahead, and they themselves often sighed quietly while wiping sweat. However, compared to other coachmen around forty years old, it seemed they hadn't suffered so much as home. These were things he never imagined would get involved with foreign cars, and when the boundary between life and death became unclear, he picked up the handlebars. Patrol officers or school constables who have been dismissed, vendors who have spent all their capital, or unemployed craftsmen, when there is nothing left to sell or nothing to offer, grit their teeth and hold back tears, and embark on this path of death. These people have already sold their prime days, and now they drip the sweat and blood of cornbread onto the streets. Without strength, experience, or friends, even among peers, you won't be well received. They pull the most beat-up truck, and the belt may deflate several times a day; While holding onto the person, he had to beg for forgiveness, even though fifteen large copper coins was already a sweet deal.

Moreover, due to the unique environment and knowledge, some coachmen formed separate factions. Those born in Xiyuan Haidian are naturally more convenient to travel to Xishan, Yanjing, and Tsinghua; Similarly, outside Anding Gate are the clear river and the northern garden; Outside Yongding Gate, Zou Nanyuan...... This is a long trip, unwilling to take a zero-seat drive; Because one trip is a trip, and they disdain spending three or five copper coins to squeeze in. But they were not as energetic as the rickshaw pullers in Dongjiaominxiang, who specialized in foreign trade and traded all the way from Jiaomin Alley to Yuquan Mountain, Summer Palace, or Xishan. Qi's long breath was a minor issue. The main reason most drivers couldn't compete for this business was because these foreigners had some unique knowledge—they spoke foreign languages. British soldiers, French soldiers, the so-called Wanshou Mountain, Yonghe Palace, the "Eight Great Hutongs"—they all knew about it. They have their own set of foreign languages and do not teach them to others. Their running style is also unique: four or six steps at a moderate pace, head down, eyes not to look aside, walking close to the roadside, exuding an air of being uncontented and possessing their own expertise. Because they were pulling foreigners, they could skip the hierarchy and instead wore long-sleeved white jackets, white or black pants, with especially thick pant legs and thin straps tied around their ankles; On his feet were wide blue cloth shoes with layered soles and thousand-layer soles; Clean, efficient, and spirited. Seeing such attire, other coachmen no longer compete for seats or race cars; they seem to belong to a different profession.

With this simple analysis, let's talk about Xiangzi's status again, as accurately as saying—we hope—some kind of nail on a machine. Before Xiangzi got involved with the nickname "Camel," he was a relatively free-spirited rickshaw puller. In other words, he was young, strong, and owned his own rickshaw: his own carriage and his own life, all in his own hands, a high-class rickshaw puller. This is by no means an easy task. One year, two years, at least three or four years; One drop of sweat, two drops of sweat—who knows how many tens of thousands of drops of sweat it took to earn that car. Gritting my teeth through wind and rain, suffering through self-suffering in daily life, I earned that car. That car was the sum of all his struggles and hardships, the reward and reward, like the badge of a battle-hardened warrior. When he rented a rickshaw, he worked from morning till night, from east to west, south to north, like a spinning top being pulled by someone; He has no oneself. But in this spinning, his eyes were not clouded, his mind undisturbed. He kept thinking about a distant car that could make him free, independent, like his own hands and feet. With his own car, he no longer had to endure the anger of those tied up, nor did he have to brush off others; With your own strength and a foreign car, you can open your eyes and have food to eat.

He was not afraid of hardship, nor did he have the usual bad habits that most rickshaw pullers could forgive but found hard to imitate. His intelligence and hard work were enough to make his aspirations a reality. If his environment had been a bit better, or he had received more education, he definitely wouldn't have fallen into the 'rubber group,' and no matter what he did, he would never let his opportunity slip by. Unfortunately, he had to pull a foreign cart; Well, in this business, he also proved his ability and intelligence. It was as if he could be a good ghost even in hell. Growing up in the countryside, having lost his parents and a few acres of poor farmland, he came to the city at eighteen. With the strength and honesty of a country boy, he had done almost anything that could earn a living by working hard. But soon he realized that pulling a rickshaw was an easier way to make money; Doing other hard labor brings limited income; Pulling a cart brings more changes and opportunities; you never know when or where you might encounter rewards higher than you hoped for. Naturally, he also understood that such opportunities were not entirely accidental; both the person and the car had to be spirited and spirited, with goods to sell to meet those who recognized them. After thinking it over, he believed he was qualified: he was strong and young; The only thing missing is that he hasn't driven before, and doesn't dare to pull a beautiful car right away. But this was not an insurmountable difficulty. With his body and strength as a foundation, he only needed to test for ten days or half a month, and he would definitely run like this. Then he would rent a new rickshaw, and maybe soon he could hire a chartered vehicle. After a year or two, even if it was three or four years, he would definitely be able to drive a car himself—a beautiful one! Looking at his strong muscles, he thought it was only a matter of time—a goal and ambition he could definitely achieve, definitely not a dream!

His stature and muscles have developed to the point of being older; In his twenties, he was already very tall and tall. Although his body hadn't yet developed a certain pattern with time, he already looked like an adult—an adult whose face and body exuded an innocent and mischievous demeanor. Looking at the superior coachman, he planned how to strike at his waist, to further highlight his iron-fan-like chest and straight, hard back; Turning my head to look at my shoulders, how broad, so dignified! After slimming her waist, she put on white pants with fat legs, tying the cuffs with chicken intestine straps to show off those big, "outgoing" feet! Yes, he was undoubtedly the best coachman; Like a fool, he laughed to himself. He doesn't look very good; what makes him endearing is the spirit on his face. His head wasn't very big, with round eyes, a chubby nose, and two short and thick eyebrows, always shaved shiny. There was no extra flesh on his cheeks, and his neck was almost as thick as one side of his head; Her face was always flushed, and the most bright part was a sizable scar between her cheekbone and right ear—when she was little, she slept under a tree,

A donkey bit into it. He didn't pay much attention to his appearance; he loved his face just as much as he loved his body—so strong and hard; He counted his face as if it were his limbs, as long as he was hard. Yes, after arriving in the city, he could still stand head-down for half a day. Standing like this, he felt like a tree, with no part of it loose.

He truly resembled a tree—strong, silent, yet full of vitality. He had his own plans and some scheming, but it was not appropriate to discuss them with others. Among rickshaw drivers, personal grievances and difficulties are the public's feed. At the "carriage entrance," in small teahouses, and in the crowded courtyard, everyone reports or argues about their own affairs, which then become everyone's property, passed from place to place like folk songs. Xiangzi was a country man, not as eloquent as city dwellers; If eloquence is the result of genius, he is naturally reluctant to speak much, so he also refuses to imitate the sharp tongues of city folks. He knew about his own affairs and didn't like to discuss them with others. Because his mouth was often idle, he had time to think; it was as if his eyes were always watching his own heart. Once he made up his mind, he followed the path he set in his heart; If it didn't work, he could go a day or two without making a sound, gritting his teeth as if biting his own heart! He decided to pull a rickshaw, so he went to pull one. He rented a beat-up cart and started by practicing his legs. On the first day, I didn't make much money. Business was good the next day, but after lying down for two days, his ankles swelled like two gourds, and he couldn't lift them anymore. He endured it, no matter how much pain it was. He knew this was inevitable, a hurdle that the cart had to pass. Once he got past this hurdle, he couldn't run boldly.

After his foot healed, he dared to run. This made him feel very satisfied, because there was nothing else to fear: he was very familiar with place names, and even if he took a detour sometimes, it didn't matter—fortunately, he had the strength. The method of pulling the cart, based on his experience in pushing, pulling, carrying, and carrying, wasn't too difficult to grasp. Besides, he had his own ideas: be more careful, compete less, and probably nothing will go wrong. As for bargaining and seat fighting, he's slow to talk and arrogant, unable to outdo those seasoned veterans. Knowing this shortcoming, he simply didn't go too far to get up to the "car entrance"; Wherever there was no car, he put it there. In this secluded spot, he could negotiate calmly, and sometimes refused to ask, simply saying, "Take a seat, I'll pay as you see fit!" He looked so honest, his face so simple and cute that people seemed to have no choice but to trust him, not daring to imagine that this big fool would be good at hitting people. Even if people were suspicious, they could only suspect he was a country bumpkin newly arrived in the city, probably unfamiliar with the way, so they couldn't offer a price. Then people asked, "Do you know him?" He smiled as if pretending to be foolish, yet also playfully, leaving people at a loss for what to do.

After two or three weeks, he had slipped his legs out. He knew his running style was very impressive. Running style is proof of the driver's ability and qualifications. The one with his feet spreading, fanning the ground like a pair of palm fans, was undoubtedly a newcomer who had just come up from the countryside. The heads were very low, feet scraping the ground, running and walking at about the same speed, and the ones who showed a bit of running were the elderly men over fifty. Those who are experienced but not very strong have another method: the chest faces inward and the prescription is very deep; Legs held high; One walk, one peeks out; This way, they appear to be running with great effort, but in reality, they are not at all faster than others; They rely on their "style" to maintain their dignity. Of course, Xiangzi would never adopt these postures. His legs were long and his steps big, his waist very stable. He ran without much noise, his steps stretched and contracted a bit, and the handlebars didn't move, making the seat feel safe and comfortable. Say stop—no matter how fast you run, just lightly rub your foot on the ground and you'll stop; His strength seemed to reach every part of the car. He leaned his back slightly, loosely gripping the handlebars with both hands; he moved swiftly, efficiently, and precisely; You don't notice the rush but the runner runs fast, fast but without danger. Even among chartered vehicles, this one is considered quite precious.

He got a new car. From the day he changed cars, he found out that the car he rented—soft bow, copper tunnel, raincloth curtain, dual lights, slim-neck large copper speakers—was worth just over a hundred; If the painter and copper workers have a bit of iron, a hundred yuan is enough to cover it. To put it roughly, as long as he had 100 yuan, he could get a car. Suddenly, he thought, if I could have one dime left in a day, then 100 yuan would be a thousand days, a thousand days! Stacking a thousand days together, he could hardly calculate how far that must be. But he made up his mind: even if it was a thousand or ten thousand days, he had to buy a car! The first step he should take is to make up his mind and go to hire a chartered car. When he had many social gatherings and dinners, with an average of ten or so dinners a month, he could easily spend two or three yuan on the bus and meals. Plus, he saves a piece of eight anise every month—maybe three heads and five yuan—and that's enough to leave fifty or sixty yuan in a year! This way, his hope was much greater. He doesn't smoke, drink, gamble, has no hobbies, and isn't burdened by his family. As long as he's willing to grit his teeth, nothing will go wrong. He swore to himself: within a year and a half, he—Xiangzi—would make his own chariot! It's freshly made, don't see new on an old car.

He really pulled in the monthly contract. However, the facts do not fully help with hope. Indeed, he gritted his teeth, but after a year and a half, he still hadn't fulfilled that vow. The chartered car was indeed loaded, and he watched things with great caution; Unfortunately, things in the world are not one-sided. He was careful with him, and the owner didn't refuse to let him go; It might be three or two months, or even ten days or so—it's all hyped! He had to look for something else. Naturally, he had to stir up trouble while also pulling the group apart; Riding horses to find horses, he couldn't just sit idle. During this season, he often made mistakes. He also tries to stay alert, not just chewing grain for a day [3]

 , and he also had to continue saving money to buy a car. But forcing oneself to stay alert is never a good thing: when pulling the cart, he can't focus on running, as if he's always thinking about something, and the more he thinks, the more scared and angry he becomes. If things keep going like this, when will you be able to buy a car? Why is that? Could I really be considered a weak opponent? In such wild thoughts, he forgot his usual caution. The wheels were loaded with scrap copper magnetic disks and fired off the cannons; So they had to take the cart. Even more seriously, sometimes they hit pedestrians, and once, in a hurry to squeeze through, they even lost the axle cover. If he were driving a chartered car, these mistakes would never have happened; Once he put it aside, he felt unhappy and started kowtowing a bit. If you damage the car, you naturally have to pay for it; This made him even more restless and added fuel to the fire; Afraid of causing even bigger trouble, he sometimes slept in all day. When he opened his eyes, the whole day had passed in vain, and he regretted it and hated himself. Also, during such times, the more anxious he was, the more he suffered for himself, and the less he ate and drank; He thought he was made of iron, but it turned out he could be sick too. When sick, he was reluctant to spend money on medicine and endured it himself; As a result, his illness worsened; not only did he have to buy medicine, but he also had to rest for several days at once. These difficulties made him grit his teeth and work harder, but the money for the car was never enough to be scraped together. For a full three years, he managed to scrape together a hundred yuan!

He couldn't wait any longer. My original plan was to buy the most complete, latest, and most attractive car, but now I have to settle for 100 yuan. and could not wait any longer; What if something goes wrong and you lose a few more yuan! Coincidentally, a freshly ordered car (custom-made but unable to afford pickup) was much the same as the car he had hoped for; It was worth over a hundred yuan, but because of the deposit, they gave up, and the shop was willing to ask for a bit less. Xiangzi's face flushed red, his hands trembling, and he struck up ninety-six yuan: "I want this car!" The shop owner planned to squeeze in a whole number, saying countless things, pulling his car out and back in, setting up the shed, putting it down, honking, every move accompanied by a long string of best adjectives; Finally, he kicked the steel wheel bar twice. "Listen to the sound, it's like a bell!" Take it away, you're basically breaking the car. If even one steel bar gets soft, take it back and throw it in my face! One hundred yuan, every cent less, we'll brag! Xiangzi counted the money again: "I want this car, ninety-six!" The shopkeeper knew he had met a shrewd person. Looking at the money and Xiangzi, he sighed, "Make friends, and the car will be yours;" Six months warranty: unless you break the big box, I'll repair it for free; Policy, take it! ”

Xiangzi's hands trembled even more as he tucked his insurance card into his pocket and pulled the cart, almost in tears. Pull it to a quiet spot, carefully examine your car, and try to take a look at your face on the paintboard! The more I look, the cuter it gets. Even the parts that don't quite match my ideal can be forgiven, because it's already my car. Looking at the car, it seemed like it could take a break for a while. He sat on the new footrest of the water winnowing basket, looking at the shiny brass horn on the handlebars. He suddenly remembered—he was twenty-two years old this year. Because his parents died early, he forgot what day his birthday was. Since coming to the city, he hasn't celebrated a single birthday. Alright, I bought a new car today. Even if it's my birthday, the person belongs to the car, which is easy to remember. Plus, the car is my own effort, so there's nothing wrong with combining people and cars.

How do you spend this "double longevity"? Xiangzi had an idea: the first business must involve someone dressed well, absolutely not a woman. It's best to pull it to Qianmen, followed by Dong'an Market. If he arrived, he should have a meal at the best food stall, like hot flatbread with stir-fried lamb. After eating, if there's a good deal, I'll pull one or two more; If not, just buy the car; It's a birthday!

Since getting this car, his life has become more and more vibrant. Whether it's a monthly package or a scattered seat, he doesn't have to worry every day about the "car quota"; no matter how much he pays, it's all his own. When your heart feels comfortable, you become kinder to others and business goes more smoothly. After half a year of driving, his hope grew: if this continued, he could work for two years, at most two years, and then buy another car—one, two...... He could run a car factory too!

However, most hopes were dashed, and Xiangzi's was no exception.

[1]

 The car entrance is the parking area.

[2]

 Pulling Wan'er means leaving after 4 p.m. and driving until dawn.

[3]

 Chewing grain means eating it.

2

Since buying the car, Xiangzi has been running even faster. His hands were more active, and his courage had grown a bit. But he was always especially cautious with his own car. Yet when he looked at himself and then at his car, he felt something was off, especially if he didn't run fast.

Since coming to the city, Xiangzi had grown more than an inch taller. He himself could tell it was as if he still had to climb higher. Indeed, his skin and appearance were harder and more refined, and he already had a small beard on his upper lip; But he thought he should grow taller. When he reached the door of a small house or street gate and had to bow deeply to enter, although he said nothing, he secretly liked it because he was already so tall and still growing. He seemed both an adult and a child, very interesting.

Such a grown man, pulling such a beautiful cart, his own cart, the bow trembling softly, even the handlebars twitching slightly; The carriage is so bright, the mats are so white, the horns are so loud; If you don't run fast, how can you be worthy of yourself, how can you face that car? This is not vanity, but rather a sense of responsibility. Without fast running and speeding, one cannot fully unleash their power and the car's elegance. That car was truly adorable. After half a year of driving, it seemed to have feelings and emotions everywhere. Every twist of Xiangzi's waist, squat, or back stretching was immediately responded, giving Xiangzi the smoothest help. There was no awkwardness or distance between them. When he reached a flat area with few people, Xiangzi could hold the handle with one hand. The faintly humming of the leather wheel would urge him to run like a sharp breeze, fast and steady. When they arrived at the location, Xiangzi's clothes were wrung out of sweat, making a splash, as if he had just been pulled out of a basin. He felt tired, but it was exhilarating and proud—a kind of exhaustion, like riding a famous horse for dozens of miles. If courage is just carelessness, then Xiangzi was not careless when he ran boldly. If you don't run fast, you feel guilty toward others; if you run fast and hurt your car, you are letting yourself down. The car is his life, and he knows how to be careful. By balancing caution and boldness, he became more confident, firmly believing that both he and the car were made of iron.

Therefore, he not only dared to run boldly but also didn't worry much about when to set out. He felt that pulling a cart hard to earn a living was the most prideful thing in the world; He was willing to go out, and no one could stop him. He didn't pay much attention to the rumors outside—stories like soldiers had arrived at Xiyuan again, battles at Changxindian, cases were being pulled outside Xizhimen, and Qihuamen had been closed for half a day. He didn't pay much attention to them. Naturally, the shops on the street had already opened their doors, and the streets were filled with armed police and security teams. He didn't want to deliberately make things uncomfortable, so he hurriedly collected the car like everyone else. But he didn't believe the rumors. He knew how to be cautious, especially since the car was his own, but after all, he was a country man, unlike city dwellers who could hear the wind and rain. Moreover, his health made him believe that even if he unfortunately arrived at the "point," he would have a way and wouldn't suffer a major loss; He's not easy to bully—so tall, with broad shoulders!

News and rumors of war almost every year grow together with the spring wheat; the ears of wheat and bayonets can be seen as symbols of hope and fear for northerners. When Xiangzi's new car was just half a year old, it was the season when wheat needed spring rain. Spring rain does not always fall according to the people's hopes, but war will come, whether or not people hope for it. Rumors, or facts, Xiangzi seems to have forgotten he once worked in the fields; He didn't care much about how war destroyed the fields, nor did he pay much attention to the presence or absence of spring rain. He only cared about his chariot, which produced pancakes and all kinds of food. It was an all-powerful field, obediently following him—a living land, a treasure land. Because of lack of rain and news of war, grain prices have risen; Xiangzi knew this. But like city dwellers, he only complains about expensive grain and has no ideas at all; Grain is expensive, expensive, right? Who can make it cheap? This attitude made him focus only on his own life, putting all disasters and disasters behind him.

If the people in the city were powerless about everything, would they spread rumors—sometimes fabricating things out of thin air, sometimes making a bit of truth a matter ten parts—to show they were not foolish or inactive. They were like little fish, and when idle, they would put their mouths on the water's edge and spit out a few useless bubbles, which was quite proud. Among the rumors, the most interesting ones are about war. Other kinds of rumors are often just rumors, like talking about ghosts or foxes—you never really see a ghost in the middle of the story. Regarding war, precisely because there is no accurate information, rumors can have immediate effects. The small show may differ greatly from true events, but when it comes to whether war exists or not, it is almost certainly correct. "War is coming!" Once this sentence is spoken, war is bound to break out sooner or later; As for who fought whom, and how to fight, that's a matter of personal interpretation. Xiangzi was well aware of this. However, those who did hard labor—including cart pullers—may not welcome war, but encountering it doesn't necessarily mean bad luck. Whenever war breaks out, the wealthy are the most anxious. As soon as they heard the bad wind, they quickly wanted to escape; Money makes them come and go quickly. They themselves wouldn't run, because their legs were weighed down by money. They had to hire many people to be their legs, had people carry the boxes, and had carts to pull them; At this moment, the hands and feet of the guys specializing in hand and foot sales all start to get expensive: "Qianmen, East Station!" "Where?" "East—train—stop!" "Oh, why not just give one yuan and forty cents! No need to reject it, chaos and chaos! ”

It was under these circumstances that Xiangzi pulled the cart out of the city. The rumors have been circulating for over ten days, and prices have risen, but the war seems far off, and it won't reach Beiping anytime soon. Xiangzi continued pulling the cart as usual, not slacking off because of rumors. One day, when he arrived at Xicheng, he noticed some cracks. At the west entrance of Huguosi Street and the entrance of Xinjie, there was no greeting: "Where is Xiyuan?" Tsinghua University? ". He wandered around the area near Xinjiekou for a while. I heard that no cars dared leave the city; outside Xizhimen, they were arresting cars—big and small cars, mules, and foreign carts all at once. If he wants a bowl of tea, he drives south; The calmness at the entrance revealed real danger. He was quite brave, but it wasn't appropriate to deliberately take a dead end. At that critical moment, two cars arrived from the south, and the people inside seemed to be students. The cart puller walked along the way[1]

 He shouted, "Is anyone going to Tsinghua University?" Hey, Tsinghua! ”

No one answered the cars at the entrance; some looked at the two cars with faint smiles, others sat with their cigarette pipes in their mouths, not even looking up. The two cars kept shouting, "Are you mute?" Tsinghua! ”

"Two yuan, I'll go!" A young, bald short man saw that the others remained silent and jokingly agreed. "Bring him over!" Find another one! "Those two cars stopped.

The young bald man was stunned for a moment, seemingly unsure what to do. Everyone else remained still. Xiangzi could tell that leaving the city was dangerous; otherwise, why would two yuan for Tsinghua (a tsinghua cost)—usually just two or three dimes—be snatched away? He didn't want to go either. But the bald young man seemed to have made up his mind: if someone accompanied him on a trip, he would go all out; He immediately took a liking to Xiangzi: "Big guy, what about you?" ”

The words "big guy" made Xiangzi laugh; it was a kind of praise. His mind turned to a spin: with such praise, it seemed he should also give that short and bold bald head a favor; Besides, two yuan is just two yuan—this isn't something you encounter every day. Danger? Could it really be that coincidental? Moreover, just a couple of days ago, someone said the Temple of Heaven was full of soldiers; What he saw with his own eyes was not even a single soldier's hair. Thinking this, he pulled the car over.

When we reached Xizhimen, there were hardly any pedestrians in the city tunnel. Xiangzi's heart sank a little. The bald man sensed trouble but still smiled and said, "Give it your attention[2]

 , buddy! A blessing, not a curse; today is today[3]

 Alright! "Xiangzi knew things were going to get bad, but after years on the streets, he couldn't hold back or throw tantrums!

Outside Xizhimen, I really didn't encounter a single car; Xiangzi lowered his head, not daring to look at the left or right side of the road. His heart seemed to press straight against his ribs. When he reached Gaoliang Bridge, he glanced around—there wasn't a single soldier there. He felt a bit more at ease. Two yuan was really two yuan, he was calculating. Without some courage, how could he find such a clever opportunity? He usually didn't like to talk, but this time he was willing to say a few words to the bald shorty guy. The street was eerily quiet. "Let's take the dirt path and go?" On the road—" "Of course," the short man guessed what he meant, "As soon as we get on the road, we'll have a bit of a foothold!" ”

Before they even reached the side road, Xiangzi and the bald dwarf, along with their cart and men, were captured by a dozen or so soldiers!

Although it was already the season for temple opening and offering incense at Miaofeng Mountain, the nighttime chill was still not something a single robe could withstand. Xiangzi had no burdens on him, except for a gray uniform and blue cloth trousers, both soaked with sweat and smelling foul—this had been there since before he was on him. From this tattered military uniform, he recalled the white cloth jacket he used to wear and the Yindanshilin blue jacket with a padded pants; How clean and dignified that is! Yes, there are many things in the world more respectable than Yindansilin blue, but Xiangzi knew how hard it was for him to get so clean and efficient. Smelling the stench of sweat on his body, he regarded his past struggles and successes as even more glorious, magnifying them tenfold compared to before. The more he thought about the past, the more he hated those soldiers. His clothes, shoes, hats, carriages, even the cloth belt tied around his waist were all taken by them; All he left was bruises and bruises, and his feet covered in blisters! However, clothes are nothing; The wounds on his body would heal soon. His car, the one earned through years of hard work, is gone! Ever since it was brought into the camp, it disappeared! All the hardships and difficulties of the past can be forgotten in the blink of an eye, but he can't forget this car!

He was not afraid of hardship; But getting another car isn't something you can just say; At least a few more years to go! All his past successes were for nothing; he had to start over and start anew! Xiangzi shed tears! He not only hated those soldiers, but also hated everything in the world. Why should he be bullied to such an extent? Why? "Why should I?" He shouted out. That shout—though somewhat satisfying—immediately reminded him of danger. Leave everything else aside for now; escaping is what matters!

Where is he? He himself couldn't answer correctly. These days, he had been running with the soldiers, sweat streaming down from his head to his heels. When walking, they had to carry, pull, or push the soldiers' belongings; Stop right there, he had to fetch water and toil the fire to feed the livestock. All day long, he only knew how to put his last bit of strength into his hands and feet, leaving his heart blank. At night, as soon as his head touched the ground, it was as if he had died, and never opening his eyes was not necessarily a bad thing.

At first, he seemed to remember the soldiers retreating toward the Miaofeng Mountain area. When he reached the back mountain, he was so focused on climbing that he could only focus on climbing, constantly thinking that he might fall into the ravine and have his flesh and blood eaten by the wild eagles, ignoring everything else. After circling the mountains for many days, one day the mountain paths became fewer and fewer. When the sun was behind him, he saw the flat ground from afar. The bugle for dinner called the soldiers back from camp, and a few armed men brought over camels.

Camel! Xiangzi's heart stirred; suddenly he could think, like a lost person suddenly finding a familiar marker and quickly recalling everything. Camels can't cross mountains; he must have come to the plains. From his knowledge, he knew that in western Beijing, places like Bali Village, Huangcun, Beixin'an, Moshikou, Wulitun, and Sanjiadian, all kept camels. Could it be that after all the detours, you finally reached the millstone's edge? What kind of strategy is this—if these soldiers who only know how to run and loot have a strategy too—he doesn't know. But he knew that if this really was a grindstone pass, the soldiers wouldn't be able to get out of the mountain and would instead look for a way down to survive. Moshikou is a good place; heading northeast leads back to Xishan; Heading south, one can head to Changxindian or Fengtai; Heading west from the exit is also a way out. By calculating this for the soldiers, he drew a path for himself: now it was time for him to escape. If the soldiers retreated back into the chaotic mountains, even if he escaped from the soldiers' hands, he still risked starvation. If you want to escape, you have to seize this opportunity. From here, he believed he could run back to Haidian in one step! Even though there are so many places in between, he knows them all; As soon as he closed his eyes, he had a map: this was the Millstone's Pass—heavens, this must be the Millstone's Pass! —He turned northeast, passed Jinding Mountain, and Li Wang's Tomb, which is Badachu; From Sipingtai heading east to Xingzikou, you arrive at Nanxinzhuang. To keep some cover, he still walked along the mountain, passing through Beixin Village and heading north past Weijia Village; Heading north, it passes Nanhe Beach; Further north, you reach Hongshantou, the Jie Prince's Mansion; Jingyi Garden! Once he found Jingyi Garden, he could reach Haidian with his eyes closed! His heart was about to leap out! These days, it seems all his blood has flowed to his limbs; At this moment, it feels as if everything comes to the heart; Feeling a warmth in the heart, but the limbs actually feel cold; The desire made him tremble all over!

He couldn't close his eyes until midnight. Hope made him happy, fear made him anxious, he wanted to sleep, but couldn't, his limbs scattered and scattered on some dry grass. There was no sound, only the stars in the sky accompanied his heartbeat. Suddenly, the camel let out two mournful cries and was not far from him. He liked this sound, like suddenly hearing a rooster crowing at night, evoking sadness yet finding some comfort.

In the distance, there was the sound of cannon fire—far away, but the sound of the cannons was clear. He didn't dare move, but the camp immediately erupted into chaos. He held his breath—his chance had come! He must know that the soldiers will have to retreat again, and they will definitely head into the mountains. His experiences from these days taught him that these soldiers fought like bees trapped in a house—they would just run around everywhere. With the sound of cannons, the soldiers must run; So, he should be more alert himself. He slowly, holding his breath, crawled on the ground, aiming to find those camels. He knew well that the camels wouldn't help him, but since he and they were both captives, it seemed he had to show some sympathy. The camp grew even more chaotic. He found camels—a few mounds lying in the darkness, with no movement except for heavy breathing, as if the world was peaceful. This is to teach him to muster some courage. He crouched beside the camel, like a soldier hiding behind a sandbag. Quickly coming up with an explanation: the artillery fire came from the south, and even if it wasn't a genuine battle, it was at least a warning that 'this road is blocked.' then these soldiers would have to flee back into the mountains. If they really went up the mountain, they couldn't bring camels. Thus, the camel's fate is also his destiny. If they hadn't given up these animals, he would have followed suit; They forget the camel, and he can escape. He pressed his ear to the ground, listening for footsteps, his heart racing.

No one knew how long they waited, but no one came to pull the camel. He mustered his courage and sat up, peering between the camel's humps, but saw nothing—everything was pitch black. Run! Whether auspicious or inauspicious, escape!

Xiangzi had only run a few dozen steps before stopping. It turned out Xiangzi couldn't bear to part with those camels, because now he had only one life. Even a hemp rope on the ground, he was happy to pick up. Even if it wasn't useful, it could comfort him a little. At least he had a rope in his hand, not completely empty. Escaping is important, but what use is a bare life? He had to take these animals with him. Although he hadn't yet recalled what use camels might have, they were still a few items, and they were quite a few pieces.

He pulled the camel up. He didn't know much about how to deal with camels, but he wasn't afraid of them, because he came from the countryside and dared to approach the livestock. The camels stood up very slowly. He didn't bother to check if they were all tied together. When he felt he could pull them along, he would take a step, whether pulling one or all the 'handles.'

With every step forward, he regretted it. Camels—used to carrying loads in their mouths—can't walk fast. Not only must you walk slowly, but you must also walk very carefully—camels fear slipping; A pool of water or a patch of mud can teach them to split their legs or twist their knees. The value of a camel lies entirely in its four legs; Once the legs are done, all done! And Xiangzi was trying to escape!

But he refused to let them go anymore. Everything is left to heaven; a camel gained for free cannot be let go!

Having grown accustomed to pulling carts, Xiangzi had a good sense of direction. Even so, he was feeling a bit unsettled right now. When he found the camels, his heart seemed to be entirely on them; When he pulled them up, he couldn't tell where they were. The sky was so dark, his heart so anxious. Even if he could look at the stars and adjust his direction, he didn't dare to act calmly; The stars—in his eyes—seemed even more anxious than he was, moving wildly in the dark sky, touching each other. Xiangzi dared not look up at the sky any longer. He lowered his head, anxious but afraid to walk forward. He remembered this: since he was leading a camel, he had to follow the main road, not just the hillside. From Moshikou—if this is Moshikou—to Huangcun is a straight road. This is not only the main road of the camel, but also not a detour. "Not taking a detour" holds great value in the heart of a rickshaw puller. However, there is no concealment on this road! What if they run into soldiers again? Even if he didn't encounter a soldier, could anyone believe he was just a camel puller with his tattered military uniform, mud on his face, and a head full of long hair? No, definitely not like someone pulling a camel! He looked a lot like a deserter! Deserters being taken by the authorities are a minor issue; Teach the villagers to catch them, at least bury them alive! Thinking of this, he shivered, and the soft sound of camel's hooves behind him startled him. If he wanted to escape, he had to give up these burdens. But in the end, he refused to let go of the rope on the camel's nose. Let's go, let's go, wherever we go, we say whatever we encounter; If you survive, you earn a few livestock; If you're dead, accept your fate!

But he took off his uniform and tore off his collar; The copper knobs that were still willing to take responsibility were also pulled off and thrown into the darkness, not making a sound. Then, he draped the collarless and buttonless robe over his body, tying two sleeves into a knot at his chest, like a backpack. He thought this would reduce the suspicion of losing his troops; His pants were rolled up a bit higher. He knew this didn't look exactly like a camel puller, but at least he didn't quite look like a deserter anymore. With the mud on his face and sweat on his body, he was probably a "coal hater" [1]

 That's it. His thinking is slow, but he thinks thoroughly, and as soon as he remembers, he immediately puts it into action. In the dark night, no one sees him; He originally didn't need to do it immediately; But he couldn't wait. He didn't know the time; maybe dawn would come suddenly. He neither followed the mountain path nor had any chance to hide during the day; If he planned to travel during the day, he had to make people believe he was a "coal blacksmith." Thinking of this, he immediately acted accordingly. He felt somewhat relieved, as if the danger had passed, and now Beiping was right before him. He had to make it to the city steadily, because he had no money or any food left, and couldn't afford to waste any more time. Thinking of this, he wanted to ride a camel to save some strength and endure hunger a little longer. But he dared not ride it; even if it was steady, he had to teach the camel to kneel before he could climb up; Time is valuable; it can't be any more troublesome. Moreover, if he climbed that high, it would be even harder to see beneath his feet. If a camel fell, he would have to accompany him. No, let's just leave like this.

Perhaps he felt he was following the main road; The direction and location were somewhat confused. Late at night, days of exhaustion and the fear of fleeing left him physically and mentally uncomfortable. When he finally walked a bit of the path, his steps were so steady and slow that he gradually seemed to grow sleepy. The night was still dark, and there was a damp, cold mist in the air, making my heart feel even more distant. Looking hard at the ground, it seemed like there were posts on the ground, but when I put my foot down, it was flat. This caution and being deceived made him even more restless, almost irritable. So he simply ignored the ground, looked at Pingli, and walked with his feet wiped the floor. Nothing could be seen outside, as if all the darkness in the world was waiting for him—stepping through the darkness and back into it; Behind him followed silently, the camel made a sound.

The darkness outside gradually grew accustoming to it, and his heart seemed to stop moving. His eyes involuntarily closed. Whether he was moving forward or having stopped, his heart felt waves of waves of waves, like a turbulent black sea. Darkness and heart merged into one, both indistinct, rising and falling, all vague. Suddenly, a thought struck his heart, as if he remembered something, or perhaps heard some noises he couldn't describe; But then he opened his eyes again. He was indeed moving forward, forgetting what he had just remembered, and there was no movement anywhere. My heart beat for a while, then gradually calmed down again. He reminded himself not to close his eyes again, nor to let his thoughts wander anymore; Getting to the city quickly was the first priority. But when he was preoccupied with thoughts, his eyes easily closed again. He had to miss something, and had to stay awake. He knew that if he collapsed, he could sleep for three days straight. What are you thinking? His head felt dizzy, his body damp and uncomfortable, his hair itched, his legs ached, and his mouth was dry and astringent. He couldn't think of anything else, only wanted to pity himself. But he couldn't even think about his own matters in detail. His head felt so hollow and dizzy, as if he had just remembered himself and then forgotten him again, like a candle about to go out, unable even to light himself up until it turned white. Combined with the surrounding darkness, he felt as if he were drifting in a cloud of black mist. Although he knew he still existed and was moving forward, there was nothing else to prove he was heading somewhere. It was like drifting alone in a desolate sea, unable to believe himself. He had never experienced such uncertainty and sadness, and absolute gloom. Usually, although he didn't like making friends, being alone under sunlight, with sunlight shining on his limbs and all sorts of things before him, he wasn't afraid. Now, he wasn't afraid yet, just couldn't be sure of everything, which made him unbearable. If the camels were as mischievous as mules and horses, perhaps he could have been taught to pay attention to them, but the camels were so docile that they made him impatient; At the most distracted moment, he suddenly wondered if the camel was still behind him, startling him; He seemed to believe that these big beasts would quietly slip into the dark fork, and he had no idea that they could gradually melt away like pulling on a block of ice.

At some point, he sat down. If he died just like that, even if he had consciousness after death, he wouldn't remember how he sat down or why. He sat for five minutes, maybe an hour, he didn't know. He didn't know whether he sat down first and then fell asleep, or if he slept first and then sat down. He probably fell asleep first and then sat down, because his exhaustion was enough to fall asleep standing upright.

He suddenly woke up. Not the kind of natural awakening from sleep, but a sudden startle, like jumping from one world to another, all in the blink of an eye. What he saw was still darkness, but he clearly heard a rooster crow—so distinct, as if something hard had grazed his mind. He was fully awake. What about camels? He couldn't think about anything else. The rope was still in his hand, and the camel was still beside him. He felt quiet inside. Too lazy to get up. He felt sore and lazy, didn't want to get up, but didn't dare sleep anymore. He had to think carefully, carefully think about it—a good idea. It was at this moment that he remembered his car and shouted, "Why?" ”

"Why should I?" But empty shouting is completely useless. He went to touch the camels, but he still didn't know how many he had brought along. Got a clear—there were three horses in total. He doesn't feel whether it's too much or too little; He focused his thoughts on these three horses. Although he hadn't yet figured out what to do, he vaguely realized that his future depended entirely on these three beasts.

"Why not sell them and buy another car?" He was about to jump! But he didn't move, as if he felt ashamed because he hadn't thought of such a natural and convenient method before. Joy outweighed shame, and he made up his mind: didn't he just hear the rooster crow? Even though the rooster sometimes crows as early as one or two in the night, it's not far from dawn. Where there is a rooster's crow, there must be a village; perhaps it is Beixin'an? There were camels there, so he had to leave quickly. If he could arrive at dawn and sell the camels, he could buy a cart as soon as he entered the city. During times of turmoil, cars are always cheaper; He was only focused on buying a car, as if selling camels was no problem at all.

Thinking of the relationship between camels and foreign carts, his spirits lifted, as if he had never felt uncomfortable anywhere. If he thought that using these three camels could buy a hundred mu of land, or exchange them for a few pearls, he wouldn't be so happy. He quickly stood up, grabbed the camel, and left. He didn't know what camels were on the market nowadays, only hearing that in old age, before trains, a camel was worth a treasure [2]

 Because camels are stronger, they eat even less than mules and horses. He didn't want to have three big kids, only hoped to get one for a hundred or eighty years, just enough to buy a car. The more I walked, the brighter the sky became; That's right, the bright spot is ahead; he really is heading east. Even if he took the wrong path, the direction was still good; The mountain is to the west, the city is to the east—he knows this. Everywhere, the uniform pitch-black gradually reveals shades of darkness. Although the colors are still unrecognizable, the fields and distant trees have all taken shape amid the widespread grayness. The stars grew sparse, and the sky was shrouded in a gray haze that looked both cloudy and foggy—dim but much higher than before. Xiangzi seemed to dare to lift his head. He also began to smell the grass by the roadside and hear a few birdsongs; Because he saw the faint shape, his ears, eyes, mouth, and nose seemed to function as they should. He could see everything on himself; though it was so shabby and disheveled, it allowed him to believe he was still alive; It was as if waking from a nightmare, feeling how adorable life is. After looking at himself, he turned to look at the camel—just as ugly as he was, but equally adorable. It was the time for animals to shed their feathers. The camels already had their grayish-red skins, with only scattered strands of long, weak, and easily shed hair that could be shed at any time, like a huge beast of beggars. The most pitiful thing is that long, hairless neck—so long, so bald, curved, clumsy, stretching far ahead, like a frustrated thin dragon. But Xiangzi never disliked them; no matter how unrespectable they were, they were still living creatures. He admitted he was the luckiest person in the world, and that heaven had given him three treasures worth trading for a foreign car; This isn't something you encounter every day. He couldn't help but laugh.

The gray sky shows some red, and the ground and distant trees become noticeably darker; The red gradually blended with the gray; some places turned gray-purple, others especially red, and most of the sky was grape gray. After a while, the red glows with a bright golden hue, and all the colors glow; Suddenly, everything became very clear. Then, the morning glow in the east turned a deep crimson, and the sky above turned blue. The red clouds shattered, golden rays shot out in streams—horizontal, horizontal, straight, light—weaving a magnificent spiderweb: green fields, trees, and wild grass all changed from dark green to glowing jade. The old pine's trunk was tinged with golden, the wings of birds gleamed with golden light, and everything was filled with a smile. Xiangzi shouted a few times toward the red light. Ever since he was dragged away by the soldiers, he seemed never to have seen the sun. He kept cursing in his heart, his head bowed, forgetting the sun and moon, forgetting the heavens. Now, he walked freely, growing brighter and brighter. The sun gave the dew on the grass leaves a touch of golden light, illuminating Xiangzi's eyebrows and warming his heart. He forgot all hardship, all danger, all pain; No matter how ragged and filthy his body was, the sun's light and heat did not exclude him; he lived in a universe filled with light and heat; He was happy, he wanted to cheer!

Looking at the tattered clothes on his body, then at the three bald camels behind him, he smiled. With just four such undecent people and livestock, he thought, to have escaped danger and still walk toward the sun—how strange! He didn't need to think about who was right or wrong anymore; it was all fate, he thought. He felt relieved and walked slowly. As long as heaven blessed him, there was nothing to fear. Where have you gone? I don't want to ask anymore, even though men and women have already come to work in the fields. Let's go—even if we can't sell camels for now, it doesn't seem like a big deal; He wanted to get to the city first. He longed to see the city again. Although there were no parents or relatives there, and no property, it was still his home. The whole city was his home, and once he arrived, he had a way. In the distance was a village, quite a small one. Outside the village, the willow trees stood like a row of tall, green guards, looking down at the low houses with smoke rising from the roofs. From afar, you can hear the village dogs barking—it's very pleasant. He kept rushing toward the village, not hoping to encounter any pleasant events, as if to show he feared nothing, that he was a good man, and certainly not afraid of the good villagers; Now everyone is bathed in the bright and peaceful sunshine. If possible, he wants a little water to drink; Even if you can't get water, that's okay; Since he didn't die in the mountains, what difference is a little more thirst for?

The village dog barked at him, but he didn't pay much attention; The stares of women and children made him feel uneasy. He must be a strange camel puller, he thought: Otherwise, why would everyone just stare at him in a daze? He felt extremely embarrassed: the soldiers didn't treat him as a person, and now that he had come to the village, everyone saw him as a monster! He didn't know what to do. His stature and strength had always made him proud and respectful, but in recent days, he had suffered countless grievances and hardships for no apparent reason. He looked over from the roof ridge of a house and saw the bright sun again, but it didn't seem as lovely as before!

On the village's only main road, pig urine and sewage converge into several foul-smelling little lakes. To the north of the road is a rather wealthy house, with tiled houses behind it. The main gate is only blocked by a wooden fence, with no wooden gate or gatehouse. Xiangzi's heart stirred: Wafang—wealthy landlord; Wooden fences without gatehouses—the owner who keeps camels! Alright, he might as well rest here for a while. What if there's a good chance to send the camel off?

Everyone watches him, and he looks at everyone; He knew that only this would be enough to reduce the villagers' suspicions.

After sitting for a while, an old man came out of the courtyard. He wore a blue cloth jacket with his arms open, his face bright and bright—clearly a wealthy man from the countryside. Xiangzi made up his mind: "Old man, the water is ready, right?" Drink a bowl! ”

"Ah!" The old man's hand rubbed a mud roll on his chest, glanced at Xiangzi, and carefully examined the three camels. "There's water!" Where did it come from? ”

"West!" Xiangzi didn't dare mention the place name, because he wasn't allowed to know.

"Are there troops in the west?" The old man's eyes fixed on Xiangzi's military trousers.

"He wrapped up the soldier and just escaped."

"The soldiers have entered the mountains, and the roads are very safe."

Xiangzi followed him inside. When he arrived at the courtyard, he saw four camels. "Old man, can you keep my three horses and make up a handful?"

"Hmph! A handful? If you go back thirty years, I've had three rounds! Times have changed; who can afford to feed camels anymore! The old man stopped, staring blankly at the four animals. After staying for a while: "A few days ago, I wanted to team up with the neighbors to send them outside the mouth to let them grow green[3]"

 。 Troops are stirring up trouble in the east, and troops are stirring up trouble in the west—who dares to leave! "See, look, twenty or thirty yuan is really hard to say, but it's really hard to take it out; These days, there's no other way! ”

Xiangzi felt a bit cold inside. Twenty or thirty yuan? Still far from buying a car! But first, he wanted to finish it quickly; second, he didn't believe he could run into another buyer by such coincidence. "Old man, give me as much as you want!"

Xiangzi spoke the truth.

Xiangzi was at a loss. When it comes to money, he has never let go of any of them. However, during these days in the army, suddenly hearing the old man's sincere and heartfelt words, he felt embarrassed to argue further. Just three live camels, for example, can't be done, absolutely not, worth only thirty-five silver dollars! But what could be done! "The camel counts as yours, old man! I just want one more thing: find me a little jacket and some food! ”

"Alright then!"

Xiangzi took a sip of cold water, then took thirty-five shiny silver coins and two stick-sized flatbreads, wearing a tattered white jacket that covered his chest, ready to step into the city!
