4

Xiangzi lay in a small shop in Haidian for three days, his body alternating between cold and warmth, his mind dazed, a streak of purple blisters forming on his gums, wanting only water, not eating anything. After three days of hunger, the anger subsided, and her body was as soft as sugar peels. It was probably during these three days that his relationship with the three camels was overheard through sleep talk or nonsense. When he woke up, he was already a 'Camel Xiangzi.'

Ever since arriving in the city, he had been 'Xiangzi,' as if he had no surname at all; Now, with the "Camel" placed above "Xiangzi," no one cared about his surname anymore. Whether he had a surname or not, he himself didn't care. However, the three animals only exchanged a few yuan, while he had earned a nickname, which he felt was a bit unfair.

Just as he could struggle to stand, he wanted to go out and take a look. He hadn't expected his legs to be so effortless. When he reached the shop entrance, he went weak and collapsed to the ground, groggy for a long time, sweating from the cold on his forehead. After holding back for a while, he opened his eyes, his stomach rumbling for a while, and he felt a bit hungry. He slowly stood up and found a wonton picker. He ordered a bowl of wontons, but he still sat on the floor. She took a sip of soup, feeling nauseous, held it in her mouth for a long time, and forced herself to swallow; I don't want to drink anymore. But after a while, the hot soup ran like a strand all the way to his abdomen, causing two burps. He knew he was alive again.

With a bit of food in his stomach, he couldn't even care about looking at himself. He had lost a lot of weight, and those torn pants were dirtier than ever. He was too lazy to move, but he wanted to restore his neat and neat appearance immediately. He was unwilling to enter the city with such a twisted face. However, to stay neat and tidy, it costs money—getting a haircut, changing clothes, buying shoes and socks—all cost money. The 35 yuan in my hand should not have moved at all; even one is still far from the amount needed to buy a car! But he felt sorry for himself. Although the days taken away by the soldiers were short, looking back now, it all felt like a nightmare. This nightmare aged him a lot, as if he suddenly gained several years. Looking at his own extravagant gestures, clearly his, yet somehow suddenly found somewhere. He was deeply saddened. He didn't dare think about the grievances and dangers of the past. Though he didn't dwell on them, they still existed, just like on cloudy days, you know the sky is dark without looking at it. He felt his body was especially adorable and shouldn't be tormented too much anymore. He stood up, knowing he was still soft, but he was eager to dress up, as if just a shave and a change of clothes would instantly make him stronger.

After dressing up, the total cost was only 2.2 yuan. A set of coarse cloth pants and jackets similar to woven fabric cost one yuan, blue cloth shoes cost eighty cents, socks woven from thread shawls cost fifteen yuan, and a straw hat worth two and a half taels. The broken things I took off were replaced with two packs of matches. Holding two packs of matches, he walked along the main road toward Xizhimen. He hadn't gone far before he felt weak and exhausted. But he gritted his teeth. He couldn't ride in a carriage, nor from any perspective: a country bumpkin could really call a road for ten or eight li, especially since he was a rickshaw puller. Putting that aside, with his own strength and strength, being caught by such a small illness is a joke; Unless he falls and can't get up again, he'll roll all over the ground and get into the city—never yield! If he couldn't get into the city today, he thought, Xiangzi would be finished. He only trusts his own body, no matter what illness he has!

He swayed slowly and let go. Not far from Haidian, a golden star appeared before his eyes. Leaning on a willow tree, he steadied himself for a long time, the world spinning and he was restless for a while, but he still refused to sit down. The rotation of heaven and earth gradually calmed down. His heart seemed to fall from afar back into his own chest. Wiping the sweat from his forehead, he took another step forward. He had already shaved his head and put on new clothes and shoes, thinking this was enough to be fair to him; So, the leg must fulfill its duty—let's go! In anger, he walked to the gate. Seeing the chaotic movement of the people, hearing the complex and piercing sounds, smelling the stench of dryness, stepping onto the fine, filthy gray earth, Xiangzi wanted to climb down and kiss that gray, stinky land, the lovely land, the land where foreign money grew! No parents or siblings, no family relatives; his only friend was this ancient city. This city gave him everything; even starving here, it was more endearing than the countryside. There was something to see, some to hear, everywhere was light and color, everywhere was sound; As long as he worked hard, there would still be countless money and endless good things to eat and wear. Here, begging for food can get meat soup and cured water; in the countryside, there is only sticky noodles. As soon as he reached the west side of Gaoliang Bridge, he sat on the riverbank and shed a few tears!

The sun leans westward, the old willows by the river sway, their tips tinged with golden light. There wasn't much water in the river, but it was full of green algae, like a greasy, long green strip—narrow, deep green, and giving off a faint fishy dampness. The wheat on the north bank had sprouted its sprouts, short and withered, with a layer of gray soil covering the leaves. The green leaves of the lotus ponds in Henan float weakly on the water's surface, with tiny water bubbles occasionally appearing on the left and right sides of the leaves. On the bridge to the east, people and cars come and go, especially hurried in the setting sun, as if sensing a sense of unease as dusk approaches. All of these were very interesting and lovable in Xiangzi's eyes and ears. Only such a small river can truly be called a river; Only trees like this, wheat, lotus leaves, and bridges can be considered trees, wheat, lotus leaves, and bridges. Because they all belong to Beiping.

Sitting there, he wasn't busy anymore. Everything before him was familiar and lovable; even if he died sitting there, he seemed very willing to do so. After resting for half a day, he went to Qiaotou and ate a bowl of old tofu: vinegar, soy sauce, Sichuan pepper oil, minced chives. When the hot snow-white tofu was blanched, it released a fragrant aroma so good it made Xiangzi hold his breath; Holding the bowl, looking at the deep green chives, his hands trembled uncontrollably. One bite, and the tofu carves a path through your body; He added two small spoonfuls of chili oil himself. After finishing the bowl, his sweat was soaked through his waistband. With his eyes half-closed, he handed over the bowl: "One more bowl!" ”

Standing up, he felt like a person again. The sun was still at its lowest point in the west, and the river was slightly reddened by the evening glow. He was so excited he wanted to shout. He touched the smooth scar on his face, touched the money in his pocket, glanced at the sunlight on the corner tower, and forced himself to forget about his illness, forgot everything. As if he had some wish, he decided to head into the city.

The city gate was packed with all kinds of cars and people. No one dared to leave quickly; everyone wanted to get over quickly. The sounds of whips, shouts, curses, horns, bells, and laughter were all buzzed continuously by the gate—like a loudspeaker—as if everyone was making some noise, buzzing loudly. Xiangzi's big feet stepped east and west, his hands flicking left and right, like a slender fish leaping with the waves, squeezing into the city. At a glance, he saw the new street entrance. The road was so wide and straight, his eyes shining, as bright as the reflection on the rooftop on the east side. He nodded.

His shop was still at the Renhe Garage on Xi'anmen Street, so naturally he wanted to go there. Because he had no family, he always lived in a car factory, although he didn't always drive cars from the factory. The boss of Renhe, Liu Siye, is nearly seventy years old; When people age, their hearts are not honest. In his youth, he worked as a warehouse guard, set up casinos, traded people, and even lent the King of Hell's accounts. All the qualifications and skills required for these professions—strength, mental path, methods, social skills, nicknames, and so on—Liu Siye had them all. During the Qing era, he fought in groups, abducted respectable women, and knelt over iron chains. Kneeling on the iron chain, Liu Si didn't frown or say a word of mercy. The lawsuit taught him to tough it out—this is called 'Zihao'. After being released, he happened to enter the Republic of China era. The power of the patrol police grew stronger. Liu Siye realized that the heroes on the ground had become a thing of the past; even if Huang Tianba were to be reborn, he would have little chance. He ran a foreign car factory. Coming from a rustic background, he knew how to deal with the poor—when to tighten the line and when to relax. He had a genius at mobilizing people. The coachmen didn't dare to play tricks with him. He glared and laughed heartily, able to make people dazed, as if one foot was in heaven and the other in hell, so they had no choice but to let him manipulate them. Up to now, he owns more than sixty cars, and even the most damaged ones are seventy to eighty percent new. He doesn't keep any old cars. As for the car rental, his was higher than others', but on the third festival, he had two more days left than others. People and factories have places to stay; even the bachelors pulling his rickshaw can live for free—but you have to pay the car share. If you can't pay the bill and end up with him and you're tired of it, he will lock up your bedding and throw people out the door like a broken water bottle. If anyone had an urgent matter or illness, just let them know. He was unwavering and eager to help through fire and water—this was called a 'Zihao'.

Liu Siye had the appearance of a tiger. Almost seventy, his back isn't bent, and he can walk ten or twenty li when he picks up his leg. It has two large round eyes, a big nose, a square mouth, and a pair of big tiger teeth, opening its mouth like a tiger. He was almost as tall as Xiangzi, had a shiny shave, and didn't keep a beard. He considered himself a tiger, but unfortunately had no sons, only a tiger girl of thirty-seven or thirty-eight—anyone who knew Liu Siye must know tiger girl. She was also sturdy and sturdy, which scared men. She was skilled at helping her father, but no one dared to marry her. She was just like a man in everything, even scolding someone with a man's straightforwardness, sometimes even with more variety. Liu Siye attacked outside, Hu Niu worked inside; father and daughter managed both the people and the carriage factory like iron tubes. Renhe Factory became the authority in the foreign car world, and the Liu family's methods were often spoken of by rickshaw drivers and car owners, like scholars citing classics.

Before buying his own car, Xiangzi had pulled cars from Renhe Factory. His savings were entrusted to Liu Siye to keep safe. After gathering enough money, he came over and bought the new car.

"Master Liu, look at my car!" Xiangzi took the new car to Renhe Factory. The old man glanced at the car and nodded, "Not leaving!" ”

"I still have to stay here. I'll have to take the monthly fee before heading to the residence!" Xiangzi said proudly.

"Alright!" Liu Siye nodded again.

So, Xiangzi found Bao Yue and went to Juzhu Mountain; After losing his job, he went to pull the scattered seats and ended up staying at Renhe Factory.

Not pulling Liu Siye's cart, but being able to stay at Renhe Factory was rare among other coachmen. Therefore, some even speculated that Xiangzi must have been related to Old Liu; Some even say Old Liu probably took a liking to Xiangzi and wanted to find a "petty man" to marry Huniu as a husband. Although this speculation carried a bit of jealousy, what if it really turned out to be the case? Once Liu Siye died, the people and the factory would definitely belong to Xiangzi. This taught them to only dare to make wild guesses, and not to say anything unheard in front of Xiangzi. Actually, Old Liu's favor toward Xiangzi had another story. Xiangzi is such a person: he can maintain old habits even in a new environment. If he had joined the army, he would never have put on that tiger skin and immediately pretended to be foolish to bully others. At the car shop, he was never idle; as soon as he sweated, he would find something to do. He wiped the car, pumped the air, dried the rain cloth, and applied oil...... He didn't need anyone to order him; he was willing to do it himself, working happily, as if it were a wonderful form of entertainment. In the factory, there were about twenty coachmen living under General Manager Chang; After collecting the car, everyone either sat chatting idly or fell asleep; Xiangzi, only Xiangzi's hands are never idle. At first, everyone thought he was trying to curry favor with Liu Siye, trying to curry favor with others; After a few days, they realized he had no intention of trying to please anyone. He was so sincere and natural that there was nothing more to say. Old Liu never once praised him, nor did he spare him a single extra glance; The old man had his own plans. He knew Xiangzi was a skilled man; even if he didn't pull his rickshaw, he would still want Xiangzi to be in the factory. With Xiangzi here, not to mention the other courtyards and entrances, they are always kept spotless. Huniu preferred this big and silly guy; whatever she said, Xiangzi always listened attentively and never argued with her; Other coachmen, having suffered so much, always spoke with a swagger; She wasn't afraid of them at all, but she didn't want to pay much attention to them; Her words, so, were left for Xiangzi to hear. When Xiangzi went to recruit Bao Yue, the Liu father and daughter felt as if they had lost a friend. When he returned, even the old man cursing seemed more straightforward and charitable.

Xiangzi took two packs of matches and entered the Renhe factory. It wasn't dark yet, and the Liu family father and daughter were having dinner. Seeing him come in, Huniu put down her chopsticks: "Xiangzi!" Did you let the wolves carry you away, or did you go to Africa to mine gold? "Hmph!" Xiangzi didn't say anything.

Liu Siye's big round eyes swept around Xiangzi, but he said nothing. Xiangzi, wearing a new straw hat, sat across from them.

"If you haven't eaten yet, have a share!" Huniu seemed to be hosting a good friend.

Xiangzi didn't move, suddenly feeling an indescribable warmth in his heart. He always treats people and factories as his home: 'Pulling a bag month, the owner changes frequently.' Pulling apart seats, changing seats from time to time; Only here did he always stay here, and people would always gossip with him. Now, having just escaped with his life, he returned to an acquaintance and even let him eat. He almost suspected they were trying to deceive him, but he was also on the verge of tears.

"Just ate two bowls of old tofu!" He showed a bit of courtesy.

"Where did you go?" Liu Siye's big round eyes were still fixed on Xiangzi. "Where's the car?" "Car?" Xiangzi spat on the spit.

"Come over here and have a bowl of rice first!" Poison won't kill you! What does two bowls of old tofu have to do with you?! "Huniu pulled him over, just like an old sister-in-law doting on her uncle." Xiangzi didn't go to bring the bowl, but took out the money first: "Fourth Master, hold on to me, thirty yuan." He put some change back into his pocket.

Master Liu Si asked with a raised eyebrow, "Where did this come from?" ”

While eating, Xiangzi recounted the story of being taken away by soldiers.

"Hmph, you silly boy!" After listening, Liu Siye shook his head. "Bring them into the city and sell them for soup pots, which are worth over ten yuan per head; If you had all the camel wool in winter, three horses would sell for sixty yuan! ”

Xiangzi had long regretted it, and hearing this made him even sadder. But then I thought, selling three live animals to the soup pot to get stabbed is a bit unethical; He and Camel both escaped, so they deserve to live. He said nothing, his heart calming down.

Miss Hu removed the weapon, and Liu Siye looked up as if something had occurred to him. Suddenly, he smiled, showing two tiger teeth that grew stronger with age: "Fool, you said the illness is in Haidian?" Why not just go all the way back from Huangcun Avenue? ”

"I came back by the Western Hills, but I'm afraid people will catch up on the main road. What if the villagers get their way back and still treat me like a deserter?"

Master Liu Si smiled, his eyes darting inwardly. He was afraid Xiangzi's words might be a bit crazy. What if those thirty yuan were stolen? He couldn't keep the stolen goods for others. When he was young, he had committed all sorts of illegal acts; Now, he considered himself turning over a new leaf, unable to be careless, and knowing exactly what to be careful with. Xiangzi's account was just a bit of a gap, but Xiangzi didn't bother to explain it at all, and the old man felt relieved.

"What should we do?" The old man pointed at the money and said.

"I'll listen to you!"

"Buy another car?" The old man showed his canine teeth again, as if saying, "You bought the car yourself, and now you're living in my place for free?!" ”

"Not enough! If you're going to buy, you have to buy a new one! Xiangzi didn't look at Liu Siye's teeth, only focused on his own heart.

"Lend it to you?" One percent of the profit, others borrow two and a half to twenty! ”

Xiangzi shook his head.

"Printing with the cart shop is worse than giving me a cent of interest!"

"I don't print money either," Xiangzi said absentmindedly, "I'll save slowly, count enough, and buy goods on the spot!" ”

The old man looked at Xiangzi as if he were staring at some strange character—so annoying, he couldn't get angry. After a while, he picked up the money: "Thirty?" Don't try to dodge the question! ”

"That's right!" Xiangzi stood up: "Go to sleep." Here's a pack of foreign fire for you, sir! He placed a pack of matches on the table and paused for a moment: "No need to tell others about the camel!" ”

5

Old Liu indeed didn't promote Xiangzi, but the story of the camel quickly spread from Haidian into the city. In the past, although no one could find fault with Xiangzi, with his stubbornness, they somewhat thought he was awkward and unsociable. Since the story of "Camel Xiangzi" spread, although Xiangzi still kept his head down and was not very harmonious, people began to see him differently. Some say he found a gold watch, others say he got three hundred silver dollars for free. Only those who know the most clearly nod and say he brought back thirty camels from Xishan! Although the accounts differ, the conclusion is the same—Xiangzi has struck it rich! For those who make illicit money, no matter how "unlucky the buddy" is, everyone is expected to respect them as usual. Working hard to make money is so hard, yet everyone hopes to make some ill-gotten money. Since bad fortune is so rare in a thousand years, some lucky moments must be extraordinary, blessed with great fortune and life. Thus, Xiangzi's silence and isolation suddenly shifted to the role of a noble who spoke too late. He should be like this, and they should be rushing to win him over. "Enough, Xiangzi! Tell me, tell me how you got rich. Xiangzi heard these words every day. He remained silent. It wasn't until he was pushed to the limit and his scar was starting to turn red, that he said, "Fa Cai, damn, where did my car go?" ”

Yes, it's true. Where did his car go? Everyone began to ponder. But worrying about others is always better than loving them, so everyone forgot about Xiangzi's carriage and instead thought about his good luck. After some time, seeing Xiangzi still pulling rickshaws, not changing his profession or buying houses or land, people became somewhat distant toward him. When mentioning Camel Xiangzi, they no longer asked why he insisted on calling him "Camel," as if he should have been called that in the first place.

Xiangzi himself did not casually forget about it. He wished he could buy a new car right away, and the more anxious he was, the more he thought about the old one. He worked hard all day long, but as he worked, he remembered that incident. Just thinking about it made him feel stifled, and he couldn't help but think: so what if you're strong? The world isn't fair just because you're strong. Why should you just snatch his car for nothing? Even if you get another one right away, who knows if you won't encounter such a thing again? He felt that the past was like a nightmare, making him almost afraid to hope for the future. Sometimes, when he saw others drinking, smoking, or running to the dirt kiln, he almost felt a bit envious. If being strong is useless, why not enjoy the present? They are right. Even if he didn't go to the clay kiln first, he should have a couple of cups of wine and enjoy himself freely. Cigarettes and alcohol now seemed to have a special allure for him; he felt these two things cost little but were definitely enough to comfort him; This allowed him to keep moving forward while forgetting past pain.

Yet, he still didn't dare touch them. He had to have one more car left as much as possible; otherwise, he couldn't buy his own car early. Even if he bought it today and lost it tomorrow, he still had to buy it. This is his aspiration, his hope, even his religion. Without pulling his own car, it felt like a wasted effort. He never expected to become an official, get rich, or buy property. His ability was only to pull a cart; his most reliable hope was to buy one; If you don't buy a car and get in it, you can't be fair to yourself. He thinks about this all day, calculating his money; If he ever forgot about it, he would forget himself and think of himself as nothing more than a runaway beast, with no sign of change or humanity. No matter how good the car was, as long as it was rented, he could never pull it up, as if carrying a stone on his back, which felt unnatural. Even with rented cars, he never slacks off, always keeping them spotless and never recklessly bumping into them; But this is just a bit of caution, not a kind of happiness. Yes, packing up your own car is like counting your money—that's true happiness. He still had to avoid smoking and drinking, and even the best tea leaves were inconvenient to drink. In teahouses, a dignified coachman like him, after running a full run, would drink ten zi per bag of tea and two packets of white sugar, to replenish energy and clear internal heat. When sweat dripped down his "ears and lips" while running, his chest felt a bit stingy, and he really wanted to do the same; This is definitely not a habit or a show, but a genuine need to have these two bowls of tea to calm it down. But when he thought of it, he still drank that packet of broken powder. Sometimes he really wanted to scold himself, asking why he was tormenting himself like this; But if a coachman wants to have two coins left per month, how can he not do this? He steeled himself. Buy the car first, buy the car first! Having a car is enough to cover everything!

He was stubborn about spending money, and never relaxed when it came to making money. Without a monthly contract, he would drive all day, leaving early and coming back late. He insisted on hauling a certain amount of money without taking the car, regardless of time or legs; Sometimes he would force himself to pull for a whole day and night. In the past, he refused to take from others' trades, especially for the old, weak, and disabled soldiers; With his body and his carriage, he competed with them for a seat—how could they possibly have a share? Now, he doesn't care much about that; he only cares about money. Every extra one is worth it, regardless of the hardships or sweetness of buying or selling, no matter who he competes with for business; He only cares about making trades, ignoring everything else, like a starving beast. He pulled it up and ran, feeling a bit more at ease, thinking that only by being old and unstable could he have hope of buying a car. Over time, Camel Xiangzi's reputation was far less than when he was alone. Many times, he grabbed a deal and ran off, followed by a chorus of curses behind him. He didn't respond, running off with his head down, thinking to himself, "If I weren't buying a car, I wouldn't be so shameless!" "He seemed to be asking for forgiveness with these words, but he refused to say it so directly." At the entrance of a train or in a teahouse, he would see everyone glaring at him; He wanted to explain things to everyone, but seeing how cold they were, and since he usually didn't drink, gamble, play chess, or chat with them, his words were kept to himself, with no way to say them out. Embarrassment gradually turns into shame and anger, and his anger flares up; They glared at him, and he glared back at them. Thinking back to how everyone respected him when he escaped from the mountain, and now being looked down on like this, he felt even sadder. Holding a pot of tea alone, if he happened to be in a teahouse, or counting the coins he had just earned, or if at the carriage entrance, he would use all his strength to suppress his anger. He didn't want to fight, though he wasn't afraid of fighting. Everyone wasn't afraid of fighting, but fighting Xiangzi was something to consider. None of them were his match, and fighting one would be a shame. Barely holding back, he couldn't think of any other way. He could only endure for a while, thinking it would be easier once he bought the car. With his own car, he doesn't have to worry about renting a car every day, so he can be confident and no longer offend people by stealing business. With this in mind, he glanced at everyone as if to say: Let's wait and see!

Personally, he shouldn't have worked so hard. After escaping back to the city, he didn't wait for his illness to recover and quickly pull the cart up. Although he refused to back down, he often felt tired. Exhausted, he didn't dare rest. He always thought that sweating a few more times would ease his soreness and laziness. When it came to food, he didn't dare pout, but he also didn't dare eat too much of the good stuff. He could tell he had lost a lot of weight, but his build was still as tall and strong, and his muscles and bones were still strong. He felt relieved. He always thought that being taller meant he would suffer more, but he never realized that being bigger means more hardship, and that he should need more nourishment. Miss Hu had already warned him several times: "If you do this, coughing up blood is your own business!" ”

He knew it was a good thing, but because things were not going well and his health was poor, he was a bit overheated. She looked a bit sharp: "If you don't rush like this, how long will you be able to buy a car?" ”

If someone else had such sharp eyes, Huniu would at least curse for half a day; She was truly extremely polite and caring toward Xiangzi. She just pouted: "Even when buying a car, you have to park slowly. Do you think it's made of iron?" You should rest well for three days! "Seeing that Xiangzi wouldn't listen, he said, "Alright, you have your old idea. If you die, don't blame me!" ”

Liu Siye also looked down on Xiangzi: Xiangzi's relentless efforts, leaving early and returning late, were certainly not good for his carriage. Although renting a car all day has no time limit—you can pick up or leave whenever you want. If everyone were as stubborn as Xiangzi, a car would break down at least half a year earlier. No matter how sturdy something is, it can't withstand a pit nailed in! Besides, Xiangzi was so focused on running for the dead that he didn't have time to help clean the car or something, which was another loss. The old man felt a bit displeased. He didn't say much; pulling the whole day without time limits was just the usual rule; Helping tidy up the car is a form of friendship, not an obligation; Given his character name, he couldn't afford to make a pointless reaction to Xiangzi. He could only show a hint of dissatisfaction at the corner of his eye and kept his mouth tightly shut. Sometimes he wanted to kick Xiangzi out; Look at his daughter—he doesn't dare do that. He had no intention of treating Xiangzi as a backup son-in-law, but since his daughter liked this simple-minded boy, he didn't want to meddle. He had only this one girl, and seeing that there was no hope of marriage, he couldn't just drive her away from his friend. Honestly, Huniu is so useful, he really doesn't want her to get married; He felt that this selfishness was a bit strange and unfair to her, so he was somewhat afraid of her. The old man was fearless all his life, but in old age, he became afraid of his daughter. Feeling a bit embarrassed, he came up with a reason: as long as he feared people, it proved he wasn't completely lawless. With this fact, perhaps he wouldn't have suffered retribution at the brink of death. Alright, he admitted he should be afraid of his daughter and refused to drive Xiangzi away. This certainly doesn't mean he can let his daughter act as he pleases and end up marrying Xiangzi. No. He could tell his daughter might have the same intentions, but Xiangzi didn't dare to curry favor.

So, he just needs to be careful—there's no need to upset his daughter first. Xiangzi didn't notice the old man's expression; he couldn't pay attention to these idle dishes. If he truly wanted to leave Renhe Factory, it was definitely not out of idleness, but hoping to bring in Bao Yue. He was starting to hate pulling people apart. First, because he was looked down upon because he fought over the business; second, because his daily income was unpredictable—more today, less tomorrow—he couldn't set a fixed amount to save enough money to buy a car. He wanted to have a clear plan in his heart, even if there was little left, as long as he could have a certain number left each month, he would feel hopeful and reassured. He is someone willing to take one carrot and one pit.

He pulled Bao Yue up. Hmph, just as frustrating as pulling out a loose seat! This time it was at the Yang residence. Mr. Yang is from Shanghai, Mrs. Yang is from Tianjin, and Mrs. Yang is from Suzhou. One gentleman, two wives, speaking with a different accent, had countless children. On the first day at work, Xiangzi nearly fainted. Early in the morning, the eldest madam took a car to the market to buy groceries. When they returned, they would send the young masters and ladies off to school—some in middle school, some in elementary school, some in kindergarten; Different schools, different ages, different looks, but all equally annoying, especially sitting in the car, the most obedient ones have more tricks than the monkeys. After sending all the children away, Mr. Yang went to the yamen. After being sent to the yamen, hurry back and take Second Madam to the Dong'an market or visit relatives and friends. Come back and pick up students to take home for lunch. After eating, I saw him off. After dropping off the students, Xiangzi thought it was time to eat, but the matriarch, speaking in a Tianjin accent, told him to fetch water. The sweet water of the Yang residence was delivered, while the bitter laundry was left to the coachman to carry. This job was outside the required conditions, so Xiangzi, eager to handle the situation, didn't dare argue and silently filled the jar. He put down the bucket and was about to get his bowl when the second madam told him to go buy some things. The first madam and the second madam have always been at odds, but in household affairs, they share the same political views: one is not allowing servants to rest for a while, and the other is not to watch servants eat. Xiangzi didn't know this, so he just assumed it was just that the house was so busy the previous day, so he didn't say anything and instead spent his own money on a few flatbreads. He loved money above all else, but to keep things going, he had to be ruthless.

After buying something, the first madam asked him to clean the yard. The husband, wife, and second wife of the Yang residence all dressed up beautifully when going out, but the whole house and courtyard looked like a huge garbage heap. Xiangzi looked at the courtyard and felt nauseous, so he focused only on cleaning, forgetting that the coachman didn't handle chores. The courtyard was tidy, and the second madam asked him to sweep the house as well. Xiangzi didn't refuse, but what surprised him was that, given the two ladies' dignity and beauty, how could the house be so dirty they couldn't even stand their feet! After tidying up the house neatly, the second madam handed over a one-year-old little mud ghost to him. He was at a loss. He was good at hard work, but he had never held a child before. He held the young master with both hands, not using too much force, afraid of slipping down; if he tried harder, afraid of hurting his bones, he broke out in a sweat. He wanted to hand this treasure over to Aunt Zhang—a big-footed old woman from Jiangbei. When he found her, she immediately scolded him for a good beating. The Yang residence always changed people every three to five days. The master and the wives always thought servants were just household servants, insisting on taking the poor man's life and not enough to deserve the meager wages. Only this Zhang Ma had been with them for five or six years, and the only reason was that whenever she dared to curse, whether husband or wife, if she angered her, she would get a beating. With Mr. Yang's fierce sea-style cursing, Mrs. Yang's Tianjin mouth grand, and Second Madam's fluent Suzhou tune, they have always been invincible; When they encountered Zhang's fierce nature, they began to feel a sense of reciprocity, like heroes meeting heroes, so they appreciated her and recruited her as a personal guard.

Xiangzi was born in the northern countryside and avoided cursing casually on the street. But he didn't dare hit Zhang Ma, because a true man doesn't fight women, nor does he want to fight back. He just shot her a glance. Aunt Zhang fell silent, as if she sensed some danger. At that moment, the first wife called Xiangzi to pick up the student. He quickly returned the clay doll to Second Madam. The second madam thought he was deliberately looking down on her and blurted out a curse about him. The first wife originally didn't want Xiangzi to hold the child for the second wife. Hearing the second wife scold him, she also scolded him with a smooth, slick voice, and it was him as well; Xiangzi became the rattan badge to be scolded. He hurriedly grabbed his bike and left, seemingly forgetting even his anger, because he had never seen anything like this before. Suddenly seeing it on his head made him feel a bit dizzy.

Groups of children were brought back one after another. The courtyard was livelier than the market, with three women cursing, a group of children crying, as chaotic as the Dashilan during a performance, and inexplicably chaotic. Fortunately, he still had to pick up Mr. Yang, so he hurriedly ran out again. The shouting and shouting on the street seemed somewhat more bearable than the chaos inside the house.

He wandered around until midnight before Xiangzi finally found a moment to sigh. He not only felt tired but also had a buzzing in his mind; The young and old of the Yang family were indeed asleep, but in his ears it seemed like the scolding of the masters and ladies was still echoing, like three different phonographs spinning in his mind, making him restless. He couldn't think about anything else—he just wanted to sleep. As soon as he entered his little room, a chill ran through his heart, but he was no longer sleepy. A gatehouse with two doors and a wooden board separating them. Zhang Ma stayed on one side, and he lived on the other. There was no light in the house; on the wall facing the street, there was a small window about two feet wide, right under a streetlamp, giving the room a bit of light. The house was damp and foul-smelling, the soil on the floor was about a copper coin thick, and a mat was placed against the wall, nothing else. He touched the bed frame, knowing that if he put his head down, he'd have to put his feet against the wall, and if he kept his feet flat, he'd have to sit up halfway. He wouldn't sleep like a gold ingot. After thinking for a long time, he pulled the plank diagonally so that both ends faced rhino horns, allowing him to lay his head flat and let his legs hang down a bit, just to make do for the night.

I moved the bedding in through the doorway, roughly made it, and lay down. His legs hung in the air, unused to it, he couldn't sleep. I forced myself to close my eyes and comforted myself: Go to sleep, I still have to get up early tomorrow! I've endured all kinds of suffering, so why can't I just tolerate this! Don't be fooled by poor food and drink; the work is exhausting. Maybe you often play cards, host guests, or have dinners[1]

 。 What are we here for, Xiangzi? Isn't it all for money? As long as you put in more money, you have to endure anything! Thinking this way, he felt much better. He sniffed the room, and it was no longer as foul as before, slowly drifting into his dreams. Groggy and dazed, I thought there were bedbugs, but I didn't bother to pick them up.

Two days later, Xiangzi's heart had grown cold. But on the fourth day, a female guest arrived, and Zhang Ma was busy setting up the card table. It was as if a spring breeze suddenly came over a small lake frozen in his heart. When the ladies started playing cards, they handed all the children over to the servants; Since Aunt Zhang had to serve the tobacco, tea, and handkerchiefs, the group of little monkeys naturally fell under Xiangzi's command. He disliked these monkeys, but sneaked a glance inside. The matriarch was managing the head's money, looking very serious. He thought to himself: Don't be fooled by this lady's skill—maybe she's not muddle-headed, and knows to take advantage of this to get the servants extra thirty or fifty cents. He was especially patient with the monkeys. For the sake of the leader's money, he had to treat these monkey cubs like young masters and ladies.

After the game ended, the lady asked him to escort the guests home. The two female passengers were eager to leave at the same time, so they had to hire another car. Xiangzi called for a car, and the matriarch lifted her robe and dragged her whole body, searching for money, preparing to pay the passenger's fare; The guest politely offered a few words, and the madam seemed desperately shouting, "What's wrong with you, old lady!" It's my turn, don't you have any fare for the ride yet! Old girl! Get on the seat! Only then did she manage to get a dime.

Xiangzi saw it clearly, and when he handed over the one dime, Madam's hand trembled slightly.

After seeing off the guests, Xiangzi helped Aunt Zhang tidy up the card table and other things. Xiangzi glanced at his wife. Madam asked Madam Zhang to get some hot water. When Madam Zhang left the room, she took out a dime: "Take it, don't sweep me tightly with your eyes!" ”

Xiangzi's face suddenly turned purple. He straightened his waist as if his head was about to press against the beam, grabbed the old bill, and threw it at his wife's chubby face: "Give me four days' wages!" "What's wrong with you?" After saying this, the lady glanced at Xiangzi again, fell silent, and gave him four days' wages. As soon as he pulled his bedding out the street gate, he heard the courtyard start cursing.

[1]

 A banquet is a banquet.

6

On an early autumn night, with a gentle breeze stirring among the starlight and leaf shadows, Xiangzi looked up at the distant Milky Way and sighed. On such a cool day, his chest felt so broad, yet he felt as if the air wasn't enough, and he felt stifled inside. He wanted to sit down and cry his heart out. With his physique, his endurance, and his stubbornness, he would make people look like pigs and dogs, unable to sustain a single matter. He not only resented the Yang family but also vaguely sensed a sense of hopelessness, fearing he would never improve again in his lifetime. Pulling the bedding roll, he walked slower and slower, as if he was no longer the kind of Xiangzi who could run ten or eight miles just by lifting his legs.

When he reached the main street, there were few pedestrians, but the streetlights were bright, making him feel even more empty and uncertain about where to go. Where to go? Naturally, it was Renhe Factory. He felt a bit sad inside. Those who do business or work hard are not afraid of lacking business, but of having a caretaker [1]

 But if it doesn't go through a business, it's like a customer entering a restaurant or barbershop, glancing at it and then walking out. Xiangzi knew that quitting work was common, so if he didn't stay here, there would be a place to stay. But he humbly kept things going, willing to swallow his pride just to buy the car, and in the end, it only took three and a half days. He was just like those old slicks used to frequenting the mansion, and he felt heartbroken. He almost felt too ashamed to enter Renhe Factory again, and joked to everyone: "Look, Camel Xiangzi is also bragging in just three and a half days[2]"

 Ah, hmph! ”

If you don't go to Renhe Factory, where else can you go? To avoid further dwelling on this matter, he kept heading toward Xi'anmen Street. The front of Renhe Factory had three shops, one of which served as a cabinet room, allowing only coachmen to come in to pay accounts or negotiate matters. They were not allowed to break through the hall casually, since the east and west rooms were the bedrooms of the Liu family father and daughter. Next to the west room was a carriage door, two large green-lacquered doors, above which was a thick iron strip bent and a bright, uncovered electric lamp hung from it. Beneath the lamp hung four gold-plated iron characters—"People and Carriage Factory." Coachmen used this gate to leave and collect carts and to come and go at any time. The deep green on the door, paired with the gold lettering on it, was illuminated by that bright white electric light. Everyone coming in and out was a beautiful carriage, black and yellow, gleaming with oil, paired with snow-white cushions. Even the coachmen felt a bit proud, as if they considered themselves the nobility among the drivers. Entering through the main gate and turning past the west wing in front, you reach a large square courtyard with an old locust tree in the center. The east and west rooms are all open-faced, serving as storage for cars; The south room and the small rooms in the courtyard behind it were all dormitories for the coachmen.

It was past eleven when Xiangzi saw the bright yet strangely lonely lamp of Renhe Factory. There was no lighting in the cabinet room or the east room, but the west room was still on. He knew Miss Hu was still awake. He wanted to sneak in quietly, so Miss Hu wouldn't see him; Precisely because she usually respected him highly, she didn't want her to be the first to see his failure.

He had just pulled the car under her window when Huniu came out of the car:

"Oh, Xiangzi?" "How—" She was about to ask further, but seeing Xiangzi looking dejected, she pulled the bedding rolls in the car and swallowed her words.

Whatever he feared, Xiangzi's shame and frustration merged into a mass, and he immediately stood firm, standing there in a daze. Unable to speak, he stared blankly at Miss Hu. She was acting strangely today too—whether it was from the electric light or powder, her face was much fairer than usual; Her face turned a bit paler, hiding much of her fierceness. There was indeed a bit of rouge on her lips, and even Huniu exuded a hint of charm. Seeing this, Xiangzi found it very strange and became even more flustered. He had never treated her as a woman before, and seeing those red lips suddenly made him feel a bit embarrassed. She wore a light green silk jacket on top, paired with a pair of blue crepe pants with fat legs. Under the electric light, the green jacket shimmered with a soft, slightly mournful thread. Because it was short, a bit of white waistband was visible, making the green look even more plain and plain. Her thick black pants beneath fluttered slightly in the breeze, like some kind of eerie aura, trying to escape the bright light and merge with the night. Xiangzi didn't dare look any longer, lowering his head in confusion, still holding onto a small glowing green coat in his heart. With the Liu family's wealth, she could have worn silk every day, but after constantly dealing with the coachmen, she always wore plain clothes. Even if there were some patterns, they didn't stand out on the fabric. Xiangzi seemed to see something very novel—familiar yet novel, which made his heart a bit unsettled.

Originally troubled, he encountered this novel living creature under the intense light, and he was at a loss. Since he refused to move, he actually hoped Miss Hu would hurry inside or order him to do something. He simply couldn't bear such torment—a torment that felt utterly uncomfortable and unbearable.

"Hey!" She stepped forward and said in a low voice, "Don't just stand there!" Go, put the car down, come back quickly, I have something to tell you. Seen inside the house. ”

Used to helping her with tasks, he had no choice but to comply. But today she was different from usual, and he really wanted to think it over. He stood there lost in thought, feeling stiff and uneasy. He had no idea what to do and pulled the car inside. Looking at the south room, there was no light, probably everyone was asleep; Or even confiscated cars. After putting the car away, he turned back to her door. Suddenly, his heart started racing.

"Come in, I have something to tell you!" She poked her head out, half smiling and half annoyed. He slowly walked in.

On the table were several white pears that were still not very ripe, their skins still green. A wine pot, three white porcelain wine cups. A large plate of the top was set with half a braised chicken and some smoked liver and sauce tripe.

"Look," Miss Hu pointed him to a chair. After he sat down, she said, "Look, I'm getting a reward today, you should have some too!" As she spoke, she poured him a glass of wine. The spiciness of baigan wine, combined with the flavor of smoked and braised meat, is distinctly rich and heavy. "Drink up, eat this chicken." I've already eaten it, no need to let it go! I just cast a divination with dominoes, so I'm sure you'll know if you're coming back. Will it work?" ”

"I don't drink!" Xiangzi stared at the wine cup, lost in thought.

"If you don't want to drink, then get out." Why are you saying you don't appreciate it when you're well-intentioned? You silly camel! If you can't kill me, I'll kill you! Even I can drink four liang. If you don't believe me, just take a look! She picked up the wine cup, drank more than half, closed her eyes, and let out a huff. Holding up the cup, "You drink!" How about I grab your ear and make you crap! ”

Xiangzi was full of resentment with nowhere to vent; faced with such teasing, he really wanted to glare at her. But he knew that Miss Hu had always treated him well, and she was so straightforward with everyone. He shouldn't have offended her. Since he refused to offend her, he thought it over and decided to vent his grievances to Shuang and her. He had never been very talkative, but today he felt as if a thousand words were weighing on his chest, and he couldn't help but speak up. Thinking this way, he felt that Miss Hu wasn't teasing him, but was honestly caring for him. He took the wine cup and drank it all. A wave of spiciness slowly, precisely, and forcefully descended; he stretched his neck, straightened his chest, and let out two not-so-convenient burps.

Huniu laughed. He had finally managed to move the wine down, and upon hearing the laughter, quickly glanced toward the east room.

"No one," she stopped laughing, though her face still wore a smile. "The old man went to celebrate Aunt's birthday, so he'll be delayed for two or three days. My aunt lived in Nanyuan. As he spoke, he filled the cup to the brim again.

Hearing this, his mind turned, and he felt something was off. At the same time, he was reluctant to go out; Her face was so close to him, her clothes so clean and smooth, her lips so red—all of them gave him a new thrill. She was still as old and ugly as ever, but now she had gained some vitality than usual, as if she had suddenly become a different person—still herself, but with something else added. He didn't dare to think about this new thing in detail, nor did he dare accept it casually, but he couldn't bear to refuse. His face flushed. As if to bolster his courage, he took another sip of wine. Just now, he wanted to vent his grievances to her, but now he had forgotten. Blushing, he couldn't help but glance at her a few more times. The more he looked, the more chaotic his heart became. She increasingly showed the little things he didn't understand, and a fiery energy began to pass through her, gradually turning her into something abstract. He warned himself to be careful; But he still had to be bold. He drank three cups in a row and forgot what it means to be cautious. Groggily watching her, he somehow felt very satisfied and bold; The very brave must immediately grasp a new experience and joy. Usually, he was a bit afraid of her; Now, she had nothing to fear at all. He himself had become more dignified and strong, as if he could treat her like a cat and hold her in his hands. The lights inside the house were turned off. The sky was very dark. From time to time, one or two stars pierce the Milky Way, or streak into the darkness, with red or white light tails, light or firm, falling straight or sweeping horizontally, sometimes flickering and trembling, giving the sky some light and heat turbulence and the darkness flickering and bursting. Sometimes one or two stars, sometimes several, all fall at once, causing the silent autumn sky to tremble slightly and causing the myriad stars to become confused for a moment. Sometimes a single giant star pierces the edge of the sky, with a long light tail, radiating starflowers; Red, gradually yellowing; In the final advance, it suddenly exuberantly illuminated the sky with a white streak, as if piercing through countless layers of darkness, letting in and lingering some milky light. As the peripheral vision faded, the darkness seemed to shake a few times, then closed again. The quiet, lazy stars returned to their places, smiling in the autumn breeze. On the ground, some autumn fireflies searching for a couple fluttered, playing star-like games.

The next day, Xiangzi got up very early, pulled the cart, and went out. His head and throat both ached a little, but since it was his first time drinking, he hadn't paid much attention. Sitting at the entrance of a small alley, the morning breeze blowing through his head, he knew this headache would soon pass. But he had other thoughts weighing on his mind that made him feel stifled and unable to excuse himself for the moment. What happened last night left him confused, ashamed, sad, and felt a bit dangerous.

He didn't understand what was going on with Miss Hu. She was no longer a virgin; Xiangzi only found out at what o'clock. He had always respected her and had never heard of her acting out of line; Although she was very casual and straightforward with everyone, no one spoke about her behind her back; Even if some coachmen spoke ill of her, it was because they said she was powerful, nothing more. So, why was there that scene last night?

This was obviously confused, and Xiangzi also doubted what happened last night. She knew he wasn't at the car factory, so how could she be wholeheartedly waiting for him? If any one is fine...... Xiangzi lowered his head. He came from the countryside. Although he never thought about marriage, he was calculating: if he had his own carriage, life was more comfortable, and he was willing to marry, he would surely marry a young, strong, hardworking, and capable woman in the countryside. For young men his age, even if someone was in charge, who wouldn't sneak off to the "White House"[3]

 ？ Xiangzi never compromised. First, he considered himself a strong-willed person and couldn't spend money on women; Second, he saw with his own eyes those fools who spent money on unlucky money—some only eighteen or nineteen years old—standing in the bathroom with their heads against the wall and unable to pee. Finally, he had to behave properly to live up to his future wife, because if he was to marry, he would have to marry a girl who was completely clean, so he had to be like that himself. But now, now...... Thinking of Huniu, if you were a friend, she really is quite good; As a woman, she's ugly, old, powerful, and shameless! Even those soldiers who had stolen his car and nearly killed him were not as hateful and repulsive as she did! She had destroyed the coolness he brought from the countryside, and now he had become a woman thief!

Besides, what if this got out loud and Liu Si found out? Did Liu Si realize his daughter was a loser? If he didn't know, wouldn't Xiangzi be the scapegoat alone? If they had known long ago and refused to control their daughter, then what exactly were they father and daughter? He got involved with such a person—what exactly was he himself? Even if both father and daughter were willing, he couldn't keep her; Whether Old Liu had sixty cars, six hundred, or six thousand! He had to leave Renhe Factory immediately and cut ties with them completely. Xiangzi has his skills—he can buy a car and marry a wife with his own skills—that's true honor! Thinking of this, he looked up and felt he was a true man—nothing to fear, nothing to worry about. As long as he worked hard, he would surely succeed.

He gave up his seat twice, but couldn't pull it up. That bit of awkwardness suddenly returned. He didn't want to think further, but his heart was tightly blocked. This seems completely different from other matters; even if there is a solution, it is not easy to forget it easily. Not only did something seem to cling to her body, but it also felt like a black spot had appeared in her heart, impossible to wash away. No matter how much resentment or hatred she had, she seemed to keep clutching his heart. The less she wanted to think about it, the more suddenly she jumped out of his heart. A naked version of herself gave him all the ugliness and beauty in an instant, like buying a pile of junk—among the scraps of copper and metal, there were a few shining little items that made it hard to refuse. He had never been this close to anyone before. Although it happened suddenly and was a deception, such a relationship couldn't be easily forgotten. He just wanted to set it aside, and it would naturally coil in his heart, as if it had taken root. This was not only an experience for him, but also an indescribable disturbance that left him at a loss for what to do. He was powerless against her, with himself, for the present and the future, like a tiny bug caught in a spider's web—it was too late to struggle.

Half-asleep, he pulled a few business deals. Even while running, he never forgot this in his heart. It wasn't a clear recall with a clear beginning or end, but a constant thought of something, a certain feeling, or some emotion—all vague yet intimate. He really wanted to drink alone, to drink until he lost his mind. Maybe he could feel better, so he couldn't endure this torment anymore! But he didn't dare drink it. He couldn't afford to ruin himself over this. He remembered buying a car again. But he couldn't concentrate on it; something was always blocking his thoughts; Before he could even imagine the car, this little thing had already slipped out, occupying his heart like a black cloud blocking the sun, interrupting the light. By evening, planning to pack up the car, he felt even more upset. He had to go back to the car factory, but he was really afraid of going back. What if you run into her? What then? He drove his empty car around the street, and after two or three visits, he was close to the depot, then turned back and walked elsewhere, just like a child who skipped school for the first time, afraid to enter his home. Strangely, the more he tried to avoid her, the more he wanted to run into her. The darker it gets, the stronger the thought comes to mind. A kind of boldness and confusion, knowing it's inappropriate but eager to try, gripped his heart tightly. When he was little, poking a wasp nest with a pole was just like this—scared, but his heart beat with the urge to try, as if some evil force was urging him. In his vague state, he felt a force even stronger than himself, trying to crush him into a ball and throw him into a raging fire; He couldn't stop his own advance.

He circled back to Xi'an Gate, this time not wanting to hesitate any longer and was going straight to the court to find her. She was no longer anyone; she was just a woman. His whole body was heating up. Just as he reached the front of the storefront, a man in his forties walked in under the light. He seemed to recognize the man's face and attitude, but didn't dare greet him. Almost instinctively, he said, "A car?" The man was taken aback: "Xiangzi?" "Yes," Xiangzi smiled. Mr. Cao? ”

Mr. Cao smiled and nodded. "Xiangzi, if you're not inside the mansion, come to me, right? The person I use now is too lazy; he never cares about cleaning the car, even though he runs surprisingly fast [4]

 . Are you coming? ”

"How could I not come, sir!" Xiangzi seemed to forget how to smile, constantly wiping his face with a small towel. "Sir, when am I starting work?"

"Well," Mr. Cao thought for a moment, "the day after tomorrow." ”

"That's right, sir!" Xiangzi also thought for a moment: "Sir, shall I take you home?" "No need. I went to Shanghai for a while, but after I came back, I didn't stay in the usual place anymore. He currently lives on North Chang Street; I'm going out for a walk tonight. See you the day after tomorrow. Mr. Cao told Xiangzi the house number, then added, "I'll still use my own car." ”

Xiangzi was so delighted he wanted to fly; all the troubles of the past few days suddenly vanished, like a white stone road washed by heavy rain. Mr. Cao was his former master. Although they hadn't been together for long, their relationship was very close; Mr. Cao was a very amiable person, and his family was small—just one wife and a young boy. He pulled the cart all the way toward Renhe Factory. The lamp in Miss Hu's room was still on. As soon as the light came on, Xiangzi was instantly stunned.

After standing for a long time, he decided to go in and see her, telling her he had found Bao Yue again; Hand over the train for the next two days; He had to give up his savings; From then on, they cut ties completely—naturally, it wasn't appropriate to say it outright; she would understand eventually.

He went in and parked the car first, then boldly called out to Miss Liu. "Come in!"

He pushed open the door. She was lying on the bed, wearing her usual clothes and barefoot. Still leaning sideways, she said, "How is it?" So what if you get the sweetness? ”

Xiangzi's face was as red as eggs given away when giving birth. After a long pause, he slowly said, "I've already found something to do, and I'll start work the day after tomorrow." He has his own car......

She picked up the conversation: "You kid, you don't know what's good for you!" She sat up, half smiling and half annoyed, pointing at him: "Here's your food, your clothes; Why is it that you have to go and sweat a lot, but that's not enough? The old man can't control me, I can't stay a widow for my whole life! Even if the old man really gets into trouble, I have two of my own hands. We can get two or three cars, making just 80 yuan a day. Isn't that better than running around the streets all day stubborn? What's wrong with me? Except for me being a bit older than you, not much bigger! I can protect you and cherish you! ”

"I'm willing to pull a rickshaw!" Xiangzi couldn't find any other defense.

"Authentic cornbread head!" Sit down first, I can't bite you! After she finished speaking, she smiled, showing a pair of tiger teeth.

Xiangzi sat down, his veins bulging. "Where's my money?"

"The old man is holding it; If you can't lose it, don't be afraid; Don't ask him for anything. Do you know his temper? If you ask for enough to buy a car, not a single kid can shorten your value; If you want it now, he'll curse you out of his wits! He's good to you! If you can't lose it, I'll compensate you both for one shorter! You country bumpkin! Don't let me tease you! ”

Xiangzi had nothing more to say. He lowered his head and rummaged for a long time, then took out two days' worth of car rental and placed it on the table: "Two days' worth of tax." "I suddenly remembered: "Let's take delivery today, and tomorrow I'll take a day off." He had no desire to rest even for a single day; However, this is remarkably straightforward; After handing over the car, I will never live in Renhe Factory again.

Miss Hu came over, grabbed the money, and stuffed it into his pocket: "These past couple of days, even the car and people have been given away for free!" You kid, you're a bit lucky! Just don't be ungrateful! After saying that, she turned around and locked the door behind her.
