Xiangzi entered the Cao residence.

He felt a bit ashamed of Miss Hu. But since it was all about her temptation, and he didn't want to covet her money, he thought that from now on, cutting ties with her would mean he wouldn't feel guilty about anyone. What worried him was that Fourth Master Liu was holding his little money. If I go to ask for it right away, I'm afraid the old man is overthinking. From then on, she never saw the father and daughter again. Perhaps in a fit of anger, Miss Hu would say a few bad things to the old man and "blow up the money." She kept asking the old man to save money for her, but as soon as she arrived at Renhe Factory, she had to run into her, which felt a bit embarrassing. He couldn't think of a proper solution, and the less he could, the more uneasy he became.

He wanted to ask Mr. Cao for an idea, but what should he say? The part about Miss Hu is something no one can talk about. Thinking of this, he truly regretted it. This matter was that he began to realize that he couldn't just cut ties. Such things can never be erased, like a black scar on the flesh. Losing the car for no reason, and now being tangled up again, he felt his whole life was probably over. No matter how hard he tried, it was all for nothing. After much thought, he realized this: in the end, he would probably have to swallow his pride and accept Miss Tiger; If not for her, then why not to want those few cars? "A turtle eating two stir-fried pork feasts!" He couldn't bear it, but when the time came, he had to do it! So we just keep moving forward—doing the good and waiting for the bad; He no longer dared to be as confident as before. His stature, strength, and mind were all insignificant; Life is your own, but you teach others to manage it; Teach some top-tier bastards to keep an eye on them.

Logically, he should have been quite happy, because the Cao residence was, among the houses he had lived in, the most lovable. The wages at the Cao residence were no higher than elsewhere. Apart from the three festival bonuses, there wasn't much change, but Mr. Cao and Mrs. Cao were very amicable, treating everyone like a personal person. Xiangzi was willing to earn more, working hard to earn money, but he also wanted a place to live like a house and enough food to eat. The Cao residence was clean everywhere, even the lower rooms were the same; The food at the Cao residence was not bitter, and they never gave the servants anything foul. He had a spacious room and could quietly eat three meals. Plus, the host was very polite, and even Xiangzi himself refused to focus solely on money. Besides, the food and shelter are both suitable, and the work isn't exhausting. Taking good care of your health isn't a loss. If he paid for his own meal, he would never eat so well. Now he already had ready-made dishes and meals that wouldn't go down his spine. Why not eat until full? The meal was also bought with money, and he calculated this account very clearly. Moreover, although the Cao family didn't play cards or often entertain guests, and they didn't have much change, they could still earn a few cents from any temporary job. For example, if the wife asked him to buy pills for the child, she would give him an extra dime and ask him to take the carriage, even though he knew he was faster than anyone. This amount of money was nothing, but it made him feel a kind of human kindness and understanding, which made him feel satisfied. Xiangzi had met quite a few masters; nine out of ten could delay paying a day's wages, so they were paid one day late, showing that the best were useless, and the servants were basically cats or dogs, or even worse. The Cao family was an exception, so he liked to be here. He went to tidy the courtyard and water the plants without waiting for their instructions, and whenever they saw him doing these things, they would say nice things. Taking advantage of this season, they would find some old things and teach him to trade them for foreign firewood. Although those things were still usable, he kept them himself. Here, he sensed a hint of humanity.

In Xiangzi's eyes, Liu Siye could be considered Huang Tianba. Although he was impressive, he cared about face and brand, never being shameless. In his heart, besides Huang Tianba, the only respectable person in his heart was Confucius. He was puzzled about what kind of person Confucius was, but it was said that Confucius could read many characters and was quite reasonable. In the residences he frequented, there were both scholarly and martial men; Among martial arts, there wasn't even one who could match Liu Siye; Among the scholars, although there were teachers teaching at the university hall and those holding good positions in the yamen, they certainly recognized quite a few characters, but never met a single reasonable. Even if the husband is reasonable, the ladies and ladies are hard to please. Only Mr. Cao was literate, reasonable, and well-behaved, winning people's hearts. Therefore, Mr. Cao must be Confucius. If Xiangzi cannot recall what Confucius looked like, he must be like Mr. Cao, regardless of whether Confucius likes it or not.

Actually, Mr. Cao wasn't very skilled. He was just a mediocre person who sometimes taught a little, and sometimes did other things. He considered himself a "socialist" and was also an aestheticist, greatly influenced by Vilian Morris. In politics and art, he had no profound insights; But he has one advantage: even the little bit of faith he believes in can be put into practice in small events in daily life. He seemed to realize that he didn't have extraordinary talent or the ability to accomplish earth-shaking achievements, so he arranged his work and family according to his ideals; Although it may not benefit society, at least I hope my words and actions are consistent, and I won't be a hypocrite or act well. Therefore, he pays close attention to small matters, as if saying that as long as the small family is well organized, society can be as full as it is. Sometimes this made him feel ashamed, and sometimes he felt pleased, as if he could see clearly that his family was a small oasis in the desert, only able to provide some fresh water and food for those who came here, with no greater significance.

Xiangzi happened to arrive at this small oasis; After walking in the desert for so many days, he thought it was a miracle. He had never met anyone like Mr. Cao, so he regarded him as a sage. Perhaps this was due to his lack of experience, or perhaps even such people are rare in the world. He pulled Mr. Cao out. His clothes were so simple, his personality lively and generous, and he himself was so clean, neat, and burly. He was especially happy to run away, as if only he was worthy to hold Mr. Cao's hand. At home, everything is so clean and quiet, making him feel comfortable and at ease. When he was in the countryside, he often saw elderly people sitting silently in winter or under the autumn moon, holding bamboo pipes in their mouths. Although he was still young and couldn't imitate these elders, he loved watching them sit quietly like this, and surely—he pondered—something that felt like it. Now, although he was in the city, the tranquility of the Cao residence was enough to remind him of the countryside. He truly wished he could smoke a pipe and get a taste of it.

Unfortunately, that woman and that little money made him uneasy; His heart was like a green leaf, wrapped in silk by a worm, ready to spin a cocoon. He couldn't let go of such a small matter; He was often stunned by others, even Mr. Cao, and answered questions that were not the same as the questions asked. This made him very sad. The Cao residence went to bed very early, and by a little after nine in the evening, he would be fine. He sat alone in the room or courtyard, tossing and turning, thinking about these two things. He even thought about going to marry her soon, thinking that would surely put an end to Huniu's hopes. But how could he support his family by pulling rickshaws? He knew the hardworking men in the crowded courtyard: men pulled carts, women sewed poor jobs, children collected coal cores, in summer they picked watermelon rinds from the dirt piles, and in winter, they all went to the porridge factory. Xiangzi could not accept this. Besides, if he marries, Old Liu will definitely never get back the money he has; How could Huniu let him off so easily! He couldn't give up that little money; it was bought with his life!

His own car was bought in early autumn last year. After more than a year, he now had nothing, only the remaining thirty-something yuan he couldn't get and some entanglements! The more he thought about it, the more unhappy he became.

It's been more than ten days since the Mid-Autumn Festival, and the weather is gradually getting cooler. He was calculating that he had to add two more pieces to wear. Money again! If you buy clothes, you can't keep the money at the same time. The hope of buying a car is simply beyond reach! Even if you're an old man with a monthly job, what does this whole life really mean?

One evening, Mr. Cao returned from Dongcheng a bit later. Because of this, Xiangzi was cautious and walked all the way from in front of Tiananmen. The road was open and empty, with few people. A gentle cool breeze and quiet lights made him feel energized to run. For many days, the frustration in his heart was temporarily forgotten. Listening to his footsteps and the gentle sound of the carriage bow, he forgot everything. He unbuttoned his button, and the cool breeze whistled against his chest. He felt exhilarated, as if he could just keep running like that, running until he didn't know where he was—even if he ran to death, it would be a bit straightforward. He ran faster and faster. There was one car ahead, and he "drove" another. Soon after, he passed Tiananmen. His feet seemed to have two springs, bouncing up almost as soon as he touched the ground; The wheels behind were spinning so badly they could barely make sense, and the leather wheels seemed to have left the ground, and both the rider and the vehicle seemed to be blown up by a gust of wind. Mr. Cao was swept by the cool breeze, probably half-asleep; otherwise, he would have stopped Xiangzi from running like this. Xiangzi ran off, his mind vaguely thinking that after sweating all over, he could sleep soundly tonight without worrying about anything.

Not far from Beichang Street, the northern half of the road was darkly shaded by the locust trees outside the red wall. Just as Xiangzi was about to step back, his foot touched something that had risen up. When the feet arrive, the wheels arrive too. Xiangzi fell out. Crack, the handlebar broke. "What's wrong?" Mr. Cao fell out of his mouth following his words. Xiangzi didn't say a word and got up on the spot. Mr. Cao also sat up cheerfully. "What's wrong?"

A pile of newly unloaded stones for the repair road, but there was no red light.

"Did you fall?" Xiangzi asked.

"No. I'll walk back, you pull the cart. Mr. Cao remained calm, feeling on the stone to see if anything had fallen.

Xiangzi touched the broken section of the handlebar: "Not much broken, sir, still sitting on it, can pull!" As he spoke, he yanked the car out of the rock. Sit down, sir! ”

Mr. Cao didn't want to sit any longer, but hearing Xiangzi's words tinged with tears, he had no choice but to go up.

Under the electric light at the entrance of Beichang Street, Mr. Cao saw a patch of skin wiped off his right hand. "Xiangzi, stop right there!"

Xiangzi turned around, his face covered in blood.

Mr. Cao was scared and couldn't remember what to say. "Hurry, hurry—"

Xiangzi was baffled, thinking he was being taught to run quickly. He grabbed his waist and ran home in one go.

After getting off the car, he saw blood on Mr. Cao's hand and hurried into the courtyard, hoping to ask his wife for medicine.

"Don't worry about me, look at yourself first!" Mr. Cao ran inside. Xiangzi looked at himself and began to feel pain—both knees and right elbows were all broken; On his face, he thought it was sweat, but it turned out to be blood. Ignoring what he was doing or thinking, he sat on the stone steps of the doorway, staring blankly at the broken carriage. The brand-new black cart had its head bent off, leaving two bare pieces of white wood chips exposed, looking very discordant and unsightly, like a beautiful paper figure stuck in a pasted paper figure whose feet hadn't yet been put on, barely poking two stalks of millet straw. Xiangzi stared blankly at the two pieces of white wood scraps. "Xiangzi!" The Cao family's maid, Aunt Gao, shouted loudly, "Xiangzi!" Where are you? ”

He sat still, his eyes fixed on the broken handlebar, and those two pieces of white wood seemed to pierce his heart.

"What the hell are you! Not a single word is spoken, hiding here; Look, you scared me! Sir, you should be called by now! "Gao Ma's words always blend events and emotions, making them both complex and moving." She was a widow of thirty-two or thirty-three—clean, straightforward, quick-witted, and meticulous. Elsewhere, some people complained that she was too scheming, often acting a bit cunning and scheming. The Cao family preferred clean and bright people, but paid little attention to minor disputes[1]

 So she had been with them for two or three years, and even when the whole Cao family went elsewhere, they always brought her along. "Sir, what do you call me?" She said again, emphasizing the words. When Xiangzi finally stood up, she saw the blood on his face: "You scared me to death, my mother!" What's going on? Why don't you change yet? If you get tetanus, that's a big deal! Hurry up! Sir, you have medicine over there! ”

Xiangzi walked ahead, while Aunt Gao chatted behind them, and together they entered the study. Mrs. Cao was also there, wrapping the teacher's hands and applying medicine. When she saw Xiangzi enter, she also exclaimed, "Yo."

"Madam, he's really fallen hard this time." Afraid Madam wouldn't notice, Aunt Gao hurried to pour cold water into the basin and spoke even more hurriedly: "I knew it all along. As soon as he started running, he didn't care about his life. Sooner or later, something would go wrong." Just as expected! Hurry up and wash up where you are! After washing, apply some medicine, sigh! ”

Xiangzi supported his right elbow, motionless. The study was so clean and elegant. Standing there with such a blood-soaked face was quite unnatural. Everyone seemed to sense something was off, and even Aunt Gao was speechless.

"Sir!" Xiangzi lowered his head, his voice low but strong: "Sir, please find someone else!" Save this month's wages for packing up the car: the handlebar is broken, the left light is broken with a piece of glass; Everything else is fine. ”

"Wash up first, apply some medicine, then talk about anything else." Mr. Cao looked at his own hand and said, as his wife was slowly wrapping the gauze upward.

"Let's wash up first!" Aunt Gao also remembered the conversation. "Sir didn't say anything, so don't turn the tables first!"

Xiangzi still didn't move. "No need to wash, it'll be fine soon!" A monthly luggage puller, someone fell and bumped into a car, and he has no face to ...... again." His words didn't fully convey his meaning, but his emotions had already been fully vented, and he was just short of crying out loud. Quitting and asking for wages, in Xiangzi's eyes, was almost tantamount to suicide. But responsibility and pride seemed more important than life at this moment, because the one who fell wasn't someone else, but Mr. Cao. If he had thrown Mrs. Yang, if he had fallen, he had fallen—he deserved it! With Mrs. Yang, he can show his street toughness, because if she doesn't treat him well, he won't be polite; Money is everything; if you don't care about face, how can you call it rules? Mr. Cao was not that kind of person at all; he had to sacrifice money to save face. He couldn't care who he hated, only his own life. He almost thought: once he left the Cao family, he would never pull a rickshaw again; Even if your life isn't worth much, you can risk it; Where is his life? What if a whole family really dies from the fall? He hadn't thought of this before, but since this time he had injured Mr. Cao in the fall, he had come to understand the truth. Alright, he could skip the wages, change careers, and no longer do this risky business. Pulling a rickshaw was his ideal career; giving up on this meant giving up hope. He felt that his whole life would just be spent in shame, without even thinking about becoming a good rickshaw puller, just empty and growing so big! When outside, he would "copy" without hesitation[2]

 Buying and selling, everyone mocked him, but this shamelessness was precisely because he was strong-willed; wanting to buy a car, he could forgive himself. What could he say if he caused trouble by taking over the moon? If people found out, Xiangzi would fall and damage his car; Which company is chartering the car? What the heck! Xiangzi had no way out! He couldn't wait for Mr. Cao to resign, so he had to leave himself!

"Xiangzi," Mr. Cao's hand was already wrapped, "You should wash up!" Not to mention quitting work for now. It's not your fault; if you put a stone, you should put a red light. Forget it, just wash up and apply some medicine. ”

"Yes, sir," Aunt Gao recalled again, "Xiangzi just can't break through; Originally, I ended up falling like this! But since the gentleman said it wasn't your fault, you don't have to be awkward anymore! Look at him like this—he's strong and strong, and he's just like a child. He's really anxious! Madam said, "Let him rest assured!" "Gao Ma's words are very much like a phonograph—she spun in circles, saying everyone in the middle without any trace of beginning, development, transition, or conclusion.

"Hurry up and wash up, I'm scared!" Mrs. Cao only said this sentence.

Xiangzi's heart was in turmoil, and when he heard his wife say she was afraid of blood, it seemed he had found something to comfort her. He brought out the washbasin and washed it a few times at the study door. Aunt Gao waited for him inside the door with a medicine bottle.

"What about the arms and legs?" Aunt Gao wiped her face with a breath. Xiangzi shook his head, "It's okay!" ”

The Cao couple went to rest. Aunt Gao held the medicine bottle and followed Xiangzi. When she arrived at his room, she put down the medicine bottle and stood at the doorway: "You can wipe it yourself later." I said, there's no need to be so obsessed over such a small matter. Back then, while my old man was alive, I often quit my job. First, I was exhausted outside, and he didn't want to force me, making me angry. Second, youthful and rough—if you don't get along with a single word, you'll break up! Working hard to earn money is not being a servant; You have your dirty money, and even my clay figurines have a rustic nature; The old lady has something she can't serve! Now I'm much better. Now that the old man died, I have nothing left to worry about, and my temper has improved a bit. Here—I've been here for almost three years now; It's not like the work on September 9th—the change is too little, but they treat people quite well. We sell strength, for money; He just said he didn't take the promise seriously. But to put it another way, taking a long-term view also has its benefits: working odd jobs every few days, even if you take six months off a year, doesn't count; If you meet someone amiable, after working for a while, your pocket money may be a bit small, but relying on Chang'er to get by can still save you a couple of coins. Since the gentleman didn't say anything about today's matter, forget it, so be it—why bother? It's not that I'm trying to get married, and you're still a little brother—it's easy to get caught in trouble. Not at all, a strong temper won't make a living. Someone as honest as you and just hanging around here for a while is better than flying around with oil. I'm not speaking for them at all; I'm doing it for you. It's really nice to be together! She took a breath: "Alright, see you tomorrow." Don't be so stubborn—I'm straightforward and speak my mind! ”

Xiangzi's right elbow hurt badly, and he couldn't sleep in the middle of the night. After calculating everything, he felt that Aunt Gao's words made sense. Everything is fake; only money is real. Save money to buy a car; Hanging a fire can't make a living! Thinking of this, a bit of peaceful sleepiness crept in.

Learning Resource Sharing 8

Mr. Cao packed up the car and didn't dock Xiangzi's wages. Mrs. Cao gave him two pills of 'Three Yellow Precious Wax,' but he didn't take them either. He didn't mention quitting again. Although she felt a bit embarrassed for several days, Gao Ma's words won in the end. After some time, life went its way again. He gradually forgot about it, and all hope rekindled. When sitting alone in his room, his eyes shine as he plans how to save money and buy a car; He kept muttering to himself, as if he had some hidden trouble. His calculations were not very sophisticated, but he often recited "six six thirty-six" in his heart and aloud; This had nothing to do with how much money he had; he just kept talking about it, and it felt like he felt a bit fuller, as if he had a ledger.

He admired Aunt Gao quite a bit, believing that this woman had more mental and capable inner thoughts than most men, and that her words were based on the roots. He didn't dare catch her to chat idly, but if he ran into her in the courtyard or at the door, if she had time to say a few words, he was happy to listen. Every time she said something, he would think for a long time, so whenever he ran into her, he would give her a silly smile, making her realize he admired her. She would feel a bit proud, and even if she didn't have time, she would at least bring up a few words.

However, when it came to handling the money, he didn't dare to blindly follow her lead. He thought her idea wasn't so bad; But it was somewhat risky. He was very willing to listen to her, learned many tricks, and felt noticeably generous; In practice, he stuck to his old idea — not to hand over money lightly.

Indeed, Aunt Gao did have a plan: ever since she became a widow, she put out the little money she could have left each month—one yuan was one yuan, two yuan was a sum—to servants, second- and third-class patrol officers, and small traders, with a profit of at least three fen. These people often panicked anxiously over one yuan, even if someone lent them one yuan and counted it as two, they would still reach out to take it. Except for this, money won't teach them to see; The money they saw was poisoned, and if they took it, it would drain their blood, but they had to keep going. Whatever could help them catch their breath, they had the courage to pick up; Life is meant to take a breather and talk about it tomorrow, tomorrow. Aunt Gao had suffered from this poison while her husband was alive. Her husband came to see her drunk, and couldn't be sent off without a single yuan; No, he was drunk and causing trouble outside the house; She had no choice; no matter how much interest she had, she had to borrow this yuan immediately. From this experience, she learned this method not out of revenge, but as a reasonable and almost emergency act of charity. Some urgently needed money, some were willing to lend money. Zhou Yu hit Huang Gai, willing to take it!

In terms of purpose, she believed there was nothing impossible to go down, but in terms of methods, she had to be more clever—she couldn't just pay to get away with water; Say whatever you want. This requires vision, skill, caution, and boldness—even if you release the eagle [1]

 。 She puts in more effort than bank managers, because she needs more caution. Capital has its size, but ideology is the same, because this is a capitalist society, like a tiny and huge sieve, sifting money bit by bit from the top—the further down, the less money you get; At the same time, it also sifts the Doctrine downward, but there are more on both sides, because Ideology is not like money, which is not afraid of small holes; it is intangible and can slip through any tiny hole. Everyone says Aunt Gao is amazing, and she admits it herself; Her strength was forged through hardship and torment. Whenever she thought of her past hardships, even her own husband was so heartless and unreasonable, she gritted her teeth. She can be gentle or ruthless; she knows she cannot live in this world without that.

She also advised Xiangzi to lend the money, purely out of goodwill. If he was willing, she could help him: "Let me tell you, Xiangzi, keep it in your pocket, and every penny will always be a penny!" Once you release it, money will be spent! That's right, what are our eyes for? Only release money after you've spotted it; don't let the bald-tailed eagle go. If the patrol officer doesn't pay the benefit or doesn't pay the principal, go find his patrol officer! In short, his job must be put on hold, dare not! Find out exactly when they pay their salaries, and clean up the nests; If you don't pay back, Xin Xin! If it's 1 to 10, who should we give it to? We all have to know our own background; Alright, let's release it, and we can take the blame from the sea. Is that okay? Listen to me, I'm definitely sure! ”

Xiangzi didn't need to say anything; his pride was enough to show he greatly admired Aunt Gao's words. When he thought it over alone, he felt that money was more secure than anything else in his hands. That's right, this is dead; money can't be spent; But it's true that you can't lose it. He gently took out the few yuan left over from the past two or three months—all fresh cash—and fiddled with them piece by piece, afraid of making a sound; The modern silver was so bright, thick, and eye-catching that he felt he absolutely couldn't let go, unless it was meant to buy a car. Everyone has their own way; he couldn't just follow Aunt Gao.

Originally, in a family with the surname Fang, the master and his entire family, including the servants, all had a deposit slip at the post office. Madam Fang also advised Xiangzi: "You can write a memorial for just one yuan, why don't you write one?" As the saying goes, always think of days that are present, and hope for a time when there is no time; never wait for a time when there is no time; Young and young, if you don't take advantage of youth and strength, only a few remain; you can't have sunny days and blazing sun every day of the year. It's not troublesome, reliable, and profitable. When will you be holding on, you can carry some money? How else would it be convenient? Go, go get a form. You can't write it, I'll fill it out for you, I'm just kind! ”

Xiangzi knew she meant well, and that both the cook Wang Liu and the wet nurse Qin Ma had memorials. He really wanted to give it a try. But one day, Miss Fang asked him to put in ten yuan. He looked closely at the small slip, which had words and a small red stamp; Tong Gong, hmph, is only as heavy as a small piece of toilet paper. After handing over the money, the other person drew a few words on the memorial and stamped a small seal. He felt this wasn't a scam, but it had to be; Putting in the white, fresh silver coins, with just a few strokes and that's it—Xiangzi wouldn't fall for that. He suspected the Fang family was involved in the post office—he always thought the post office was a business with branches everywhere, probably old in number, at least similar to Ruifu Xiang or Hongji—so he was so enthusiastic about pulling in business. Even if that's not the case, having cash in hand is still much better than writing a small memorial! The money on the memorial was just a few words!

As for the bank bank, he only knew it was a place to "get a seat." If the patrol officers didn't stop him from stopping the car there, he could definitely pull up a "deal." As for what was happening inside, he couldn't figure it out. Indeed, there must be a lot of money here; But why come here to tease me?

 He doesn't understand money; He himself wasn't easily involved with them, so he didn't have to worry about it. There were many things in the city he didn't understand, and listening to friends gossiping in teahouses made him even more confused, because everyone had their own explanations, and none of them were quite accurate. He didn't want to listen or think about it further. He knew that if he went to rob, it would be a bank robbery; Since he didn't want to become a bandit, he might as well keep his own money and not worry about anything else. He thought this was the most experienced method.

Aunt Gao knew he was blushing and wanted to buy a car, so she gave him another idea: "Xiangzi, I know you won't pay the bill. It's a good idea to buy your own car early!" If I were a man and also pulled a rickshaw, I would have to pull my own rickshaw; Singing and playing by yourself, never asking others for anything! If you can do this, I wouldn't trade it for a county magistrate! Pulling a rickshaw is tough, but if I were a man and had some strength, I wouldn't have been a patrol officer even if I pulled a cart; Evergreen through winter and summer, always standing on the street, earning only a couple of yuan a month, no extra money, no freedom; Growing a beard and still bragging, showing no improvement at all. I mean, by the way, if you want to buy a car quickly, here's a good idea: start a while, about ten people, at most twenty, two yuan per person per month, and you can use it for the first time; Isn't it about forty yuan right away? At least you have some savings, so just scrape together and get a car—just make it big! Once the car is in hand, you can get a black lottery session—no profit, it's a respectable matter, and it must match your heart! If you really want to invite me, I'll give you one—no slumping or scrutiny! How about it? ”

This really made Xiangzi's heart race faster! If you really scrape together thirty or forty yuan, plus the thirty-something yuan Liu Siye has and the few yuan he has now, wouldn't that make it eighty or so? Although it's not enough to buy a car that's 100% new, at least 80% new can be achieved! Besides, this way, he could go get the money back from Master Liu, so he wouldn't have to keep it sitting like this, which wouldn't seem right. Eighty percent new is one thing, at least keep it and replace it when there's some leftover.

But where could they find such twenty people? Even if he could get close, it was a matter of face. If he waited for the money, he would invite him in. Would they invite him over tomorrow? In these difficult years, there are often times when things get chaotic! A true man does not rely on others; Simply because you have the life to buy a car yourself, buy it; No need to rely on others!

Seeing Xiangzi still unmoved, Aunt Gao wanted to tease him, but thinking about his sincerity, she felt a bit embarrassed: "You're really something!" 'Driving pigs in a small alley—straight and straightforward'; Sounds good! ”

Xiangzi didn't say anything. After Aunt Gao left, he nodded to himself, as if admitting that his own death was admirable, and he felt quite pleased.

It was already early winter. At night, in the alleys, vendors sold sugar-roasted chestnuts, and besides peanuts, there was also the mournful "night pot vomiting." The chamber pot had a clay gourd jar on its handle. Xiangzi bought a large one. The first seller couldn't find money. Xiangzi thought to himself that the tiny little green chamber pot was very interesting—so green, pouting, he said, "No need to change money, I'll take one for you!" Putting down the bottle of silence, he brought the little green night pot inside: "Young master, where are you sleeping?" Here's a good toy for you! ”

Everyone was watching Xiao Wen—the little boy from the Cao family—taking a shower. Seeing this thing, they couldn't help but laugh. The Cao couple said nothing, probably thinking that although this thing was a bit silly, Xiangzi's kindness was something to be appreciated, so they both smiled and thanked him. Gao Ma wasn't idle about it: "Look, seriously, Xiangzi!" At such a big age, how could he come up with such a clever idea? How unpleasant it is! ”

Xiaowen really liked this thing and immediately scooped water from the bathtub into the small kettle: "This little teapot has a big mouth!" ”

Everyone laughed even harder. Xiangzi straightened up—because he was so proud he didn't know what to do—and walked out. He was very happy; this was something he had never experienced before. Everyone's smiles were directed at him, as if he were someone very important. Smiling, he carried out those fresh foreign goods and gently placed them into the stuffed gourd jar one by one, thinking to himself: This is more reliable than anything else! If you count enough, just bump into the wall; Snap at it, there are more silverware than tiles!

He decided not to ask anyone for help anymore. Even though Liu Siye is so reliable, sometimes it's noticeably awkward. The money can't be lost, and in Liu Siye's hands, he still feels a bit uneasy. Money is like a ring—it's always best in your own hand. This decision made him feel satisfied, as if his belt had tightened again, making his chest straighter and firmer.

The weather was getting colder and colder, but Xiangzi seemed unaware. Once you have a certain idea in your heart, your vision will be filled with light; You won't feel cold in the light. Ice ice was first visible on the ground, and even the soil on the pathway had solidified, showing dryness and firmness everywhere. The black soil had already turned slightly yellow, as if the moisture had dissipated. Especially early in the morning, the ridges of earth rolled up by the carts are edged with several frosty edges, and a gentle breeze swiftly scatters the morning glow, revealing a sky of extremely high blue, refreshing clarity; Xiangzi was willing to pull the cart early and make a trip; the cool breeze brushed into his sleeves, making his whole body shiver as if taking a cold shower, feeling a sudden relief. Sometimes when a fierce wind blows, he is battered until he can't breathe, but he keeps his head down, grits his teeth, and dodes forward like a big fish floating against the current; The stronger the wind, the greater his resistance, as if he was fighting to the death with the wild wind. A sudden gust of wind struck him, making it hard for him to breathe. He shut his mouth for a long time, then burped, as if he had been stabbed into water. With this burp, he kept running forward, charging forward, and nothing could stop the giant; Every muscle in his body didn't relax in a single place, like a green worm besieged by ants, swaying all over to resist. Sweat all over! "Pulling up a deal, they warmed up, sweat soaking through their thin, tattered clothes. As soon as they stopped, their sweat froze on their backs. The streets are gradually starting to look a bit youthful. Xiangzi remained silent throughout, but he paid close attention to what they said. Although everyone's words differ in tone, tone, and facts, they are all curses and injustices. These words, when touched by his own grievances, were like raindrops falling on dry soil, swallowing them all. He can't, and won't, speak his words with a clear beginning and end to everyone; He could only absorb some of the bitterness of life from others' words; everyone else was troubled, and he was no exception; Having come to know myself, I also want to sympathize with everyone. When people mention the sorrowful parts, he frowns; When it came to something ridiculous, he would curl his lips. In this way, he felt he was close to them, that they were all old friends, and although he didn't say a word, it didn't really matter. In the past, he thought everyone was just talkative and sharp-tongued, and that with their endless poverty, they couldn't get rich. Today, it seemed like for the first time, they didn't speak poorly, but rather spoke for him, sharing the hardships he faced with all the coachmen.

Just as everyone was chatting into the middle of the excitement, the door suddenly opened and a wave of cold air came in. Almost everyone glared angrily outside, wondering who was so unpopular as to push the door open. The more anxious everyone was, the slower the person outside became, as if deliberately prolonging the crowd[1]

 。 The teahouse attendant shouted with a half-anxious smile, "Hurry up, I'm the only uncle!" Don't let even a little heat out! ”

Before he could finish speaking, someone outside came in—he was also a rickshaw puller. He looked to be over fifty, wearing a cotton jacket that was neither too short nor long enough, like a lotus pod basket, with cotton already showing on his collar and elbows. His face seemed to have been unwashed for many days, with no visible flesh color, only his two ears were bright red from the cold, red like fruit about to fall. His pale hair was messy and beard under a tattered little hat; On his eyebrows and short whiskers, there were some ice beads hanging from them. As soon as he entered, he grabbed a stool and sat down, struggling to say, "Brew a pot." ”

This teahouse has always been a gathering place for monthly coachmen; an old coachman like this would never come in on a weekday.

Everyone looked at him, as if they felt something deeper than what had just been said, and no one wanted to speak again. On ordinary days, there would always be one or two immature young men who would pick a few playful remarks to mock such tea guests, but today not a single one spoke up.

Before the tea was brewed, the old coachman slowly lowered his head, and as he kept lowering it, his whole body slipped down.

Everyone immediately stood up: "What's wrong?" What's wrong? As he spoke, he all wanted to run forward.

"Don't move!" The experienced teahouse manager stopped everyone. He went alone, untied the old coachman's collar, helped him up on the spot, slammed a chair behind his back, and squeezed his shoulders with his hands: "Sugar water, hurry!" After saying that, he slid his tongue against the old coachman's neck and muttered to himself, "It's not phlegm!" ”

No one moved, but no one sat down again. All blinked in the room filled with smoke, glancing toward the door. It seems everyone unanimously thinks to themselves: "This is our role model!" When hair turns deathly white, everyone has a market where they fall to their death! ”

As soon as the sweet water was brought to the old coachman's mouth, he snorted twice. With his eyes still closed, he raised his right hand—shining black as if painted—and wiped his mouth with the back of his hand.

"Have some water!" The shopkeeper whispered into his ear.

"Huh?" The old coachman opened his eyes. Seeing himself sitting on the ground, his legs curled up, trying to stand up.

"Have some water first, no need to rush." The shopkeeper said, letting go. Almost everyone ran over.

"Hey! Sigh! The old coachman glanced around, cupped his teacup firmly with both hands, and sipped the sugar water one sip after another.

After slowly finishing the sweet water, he glanced at everyone again: "Hey, thank you all for your help!" He spoke very gently and warmly, not at all coming from that bearded man's mouth. After saying that, he tried to stand up again, but three or four people hurried over to help him up. A faint smile appeared on his face, and he said gently, "Alright, alright, it's fine!" I was cold and hungry, and suddenly dizzy! It's okay! Although his face was covered in thick mud, that little smile made everyone feel as if he were seeing a gentle, fair face.

It seemed everyone was completely moved. The middle-aged man holding the bowl of wine had already finished his drink, his eyes red and tears streaming down his face: "Come on, two taels!" When the wine arrived, the old coachman was already seated on a chair against the wall. He was a bit tipsy, but he properly placed the wine in front of the old coachman: "Mine on me, please drink!" I'm already over forty, and to be honest, a monthly job is just a makeshift job—a year is a year of progress, and my legs know! In another two or three years, I'll be just like you! Ni Heng is [2]

 Almost sixty, right? ”

"Still young, fifty-five!" The old coachman took a sip of wine. "It's cold, I can't get a seat. Me, ah, my stomach is empty; There were a few of them I drank, so it was warm! Walking here, I really can't hold on any longer and want to come in for some warmth. It was too hot inside, and I hadn't eaten, so I ended up fainting. It's okay, it's okay! Thanks for the good ride, guys! ”

At this moment, the old man's hair was as gray as dry grass, the mud on his face, his hands like charcoal strips, and his tattered hat and cotton jacket—all seemed to emit a pure light, like a statue in a broken temple—though broken, it still carried dignity. Everyone looked at him, as if afraid he would leave. Xiangzi remained silent, standing there in a daze. Hearing the old coachman say his stomach was empty, he dashed out, but Fei Ye seemed to come back, holding ten lamb-filled buns with a cabbage leaf in his hands. The old man seemed both amused and crying, nodding to everyone. "Are you really a buddy?" "Xiao Ma is only twelve or thirteen years old, his face quite thin, but he's dressed very roundly, his nose is red from the cold, with two white strands of snot hanging from it, and he wears a pair of worn-out ear hats." A severely damaged cart, the paint on the panels already cracked, the handlebars already worn to reveal wood grain, a cracking broken lamp, and the shaft's poles tied with hemp rope. Xiangzi stood outside the door in a daze, staring at the old man, the young man, and the broken car. The old man walked and spoke as he spoke, his voice rising and falling; The streetlights and shadows flickered on and off. Xiangzi listened and watched, feeling a pain he had never experienced before. In Xiao Ma'er, he seemed to see his own past; In the old man, it seemed he saw his own future! He had never easily given up a single coin, but now he felt satisfied and bought ten buns for the old and the young. Only when they were out of sight did he go back inside. Everyone started laughing and chatting again. He felt a bit confused, paid for the tea, and came out again, pulling the car outside the cinema to wait for Mr. Cao.

The sky was freezing. Some gray sand floated in the air, the wind seemed to rush over it, making it hard to see the stars—only the big ones trembled slightly in the air. There was no wind on the ground, but cold air was blowing from all directions. Several long, cracked cracks were already in the ruts, the earth grayish-white, as cold and hard as ice. Xiangzi stood outside the cinema for a while, already feeling cold, but unwilling to return to the teahouse. He wanted to quietly think alone. The old man and the young man seemed to shatter his greatest hope—the old man's car was his! Ever since he started pulling a rickshaw the first day, he decided to buy his own rickshaw and still works hard all day for this volunteer job; With his own car, he thought he had everything. Hmph, look at that old man!

He refused to take Huniu because he wanted to buy a car. Buy a car, save money, and then get married with nothing at all; Hmph, look at Xiao Ma! Having a son might just be like that.

Thinking about it this way, it seems there's no need to resist Huniu's threats; Anyway, if he couldn't break out of his circle, what kind of woman wouldn't be acceptable? Besides, she had brought a few cars before—why not enjoy a few days of ready-made treats? Once you see through yourself, there's no need to underestimate others. A tiger girl is just a tiger girl—there's nothing more to say!

After the movie ended, he hurriedly put the small water bottle back up and lit the lamp. Even his little cotton-padded jacket was off, leaving only a small jacket. He wanted to run away in one breath, to forget everything and die from the fall!

Eleven

Whenever he thought of that old man and little horse, Xiangzi would let go of all hope and just enjoy each day. Why keep gritting his teeth and making things difficult for him?! He seems to understand the fate of the poor: jujube pits with pointed ends at both ends: fortunate not to starve to death as a child; It's hard not to starve to death when you're old. Only the middle section is young and vigorous, unafraid of hunger or toil, and can still act like a human. In this section, when it's time to be happy, you don't dare to act—a true fool; Once you pass this village, there will be no more shops! Thinking this way, he didn't even want to worry about the matter with Huniu.

But when he saw that quiet gourd jar, his thoughts shifted again. No, it can't be done casually; Just a few dozen yuan short of getting in the car is enough—don't waste all your efforts; At the very least, you can't just throw away the little savings in the jar—it's not easy to save! We still have to go the right way, absolutely! But what about Huniu? Still, there was no other way; he still had to worry about that hateful twenty-seven.

Worried and helpless, he hugged the clay pot and muttered to himself: 'Do whatever you want, this money is mine anyway!' No one can snatch it! With this little money, Xiangzi feared nothing! When I get anxious, I can stomp my feet and run. With money, my legs will move!

The streets are getting livelier and livelier, with sugar melons for the Kitchen God worship filling the streets. Wherever you go, you can hear the sound of "Peeling candy, peeling sugar." Xiangzi had been looking forward to the New Year, but now he was completely unmotivated. The messier the streets became, the tighter his heart became—the terrifying twenty-seventh was just around the corner! His eyes sank, and even the scar on his face darkened. Pulling the cart, the streets were so chaotic, the ground so slippery, he was extra cautious. With both worries and alertness taking on each other, he felt his energy was lacking. Thinking about this and forgetting about that, he would often be startled, his body itching like a child with prickly heat in summer.

On the afternoon of the Kitchen God worship ceremony, a steady east wind brought a day of dark clouds. The weather suddenly warmed up a bit. As the lanterns were about to come out, the wind grew even softer, and sparse snowflakes fell from the sky. The sugar melon sellers were all anxious. The weather was warm, and with snowflakes falling, everyone kept sprinkling white soil on the candy, afraid it would stick together. Not many snowflakes fell, turning into tiny snowflakes with a soft rustling sound, turning the ground white. After seven o'clock, shopkeepers and families began the Kitchen God ritual, with dense snowflakes mixed with the fragrant light and firecrackers, bringing a somewhat eerie atmosphere to the lively atmosphere. People on the street all looked a bit anxious—whether walking or taking a car, everyone was eager to return home to worship the gods, but the ground was slippery and they didn't dare to walk freely. The sugar vendor was eager to sell the seasonal goods, gasping for breath and shouting, which was quite heart-wrenching.

It was about nine o'clock when Xiangzi pulled Mr. Cao home from the west city. After passing the bustling street at Xidan Archway, heading east into Chang'an Avenue, the crowds gradually thinned out. The flat asphalt road was covered with a thin layer of snow, dazzling by the streetlights. Occasionally, a car would pass by, its lights shining in the distance, and the snowflakes would glow with a hint of yellow in the light, as if scattered with thousands of golden grains. Approaching the area near Xinhua Gate, the road was already very wide, and with the light snow, it made it even more refreshing and refreshing, and everything seemed even more serious. "Chang'an Archway," the Xinhua Gate gate, and the red walls of Nanhai are all adorned with plain crowns, paired with vermilion pillars and red walls, quietly displaying the dignity of the ancient capital under the lights. At this moment and place, it feels as if Beiping has no inhabitants, but rather a jade-like palace and crystal-clear sky, with only some old pines silently catching the snowflakes. Xiangzi had no time to admire these beautiful sights. When he saw the "Jade Road" before him, he just wanted to run home in a single step; That straight, white, and calm road seemed to keep his mind fixed on the door. But he couldn't run fast. Although the snow on the ground wasn't thick, when he took his feet, a thick layer would stick to the soles of his shoes; He stomped down, and after a while, it stuck again. The grapetons were very small, but heavy and heavy—they could hold his feet and blind his eyes, so he couldn't run fast. The snow didn't melt easily on his body; a thin layer had already formed on his shirt shoulder. Although it wasn't much, the wetness made him feel uncomfortable. There were hardly any shops in this area, but the distant cannon fire continued, constantly firing double shots or five ghosts causing chaos in the dark sky. Sparks flew, and the air grew even darker, almost terrifyingly black. He listened to the sound of cannons, saw the sparks and darkness in the air, and wanted to get home immediately. But he didn't dare let go of his legs—it was awkward!

What annoyed him even more was that starting from Xicheng, he felt a bicycle following behind him. When they reached West Chang'an Avenue, the street became quieter, and I could feel the following—cars rolling through thin snow. Though the sound wasn't loud, I could tell they were coming out. Like other rickshaw drivers, Xiangzi hated bicycles the most. Cars are annoying, but their noise is loud, so you can avoid them from far away. Bicycles always squeeze through every gap, swaying here and there, making you dizzy just by looking at them. If you bring it along, it's better not to make mistakes. If you make a mistake, it's always the rickshaw driver's fault. The patrol officers' mindset is that no matter what, the rickshaw driver is easier to deal with than the rider, so the one who sent the rickshaw driver first is at fault. Several times, Xiangzi wanted to hit the brakes to stop the car, but the kid behind him fell and fell. But he didn't dare, and the rickshaw driver endured it everywhere. Whenever he was about to tap his shoe sole, he had to shout, "Stop!" "When we arrived at the front gate of Nanhai, the street was so wide, and that bicycle was still closely following behind. Xiangzi grew even more agitated. He deliberately stopped the car and brushed the snow off his clothes. He stopped, and the bicycle brushed past the car. The person in the car even glanced back. Xiangzi deliberately tried to annoy him, only grabbing the handlebars after the bike had gone far and cursing, "Annoying!" "Mr. Cao's "humanitarianism" made him unwilling to install the wind-ridden cotton carriage shed; even the canvas carriage shed was not allowed to be set up during heavy rain, just to teach the coachman a bit more effort. With this little snow, he thought there was no need to set up a carport, especially since he was still eager to see the snowy scenery at night. He also noticed the bicycle. After Xiangzi finished scolding, he whispered, "If he keeps following me, don't stop at my doorstep—go to Mr. Zuo of Huanghuamen; Don't panic! ”

Xiangzi was a bit flustered. At the entrance of Nanchang Street, Xiangzi took advantage of the turn to glance back—the man was still following. He almost forgot about the snow on the ground and pressed harder on his feet. "What did I do to offend whom?!" Xiangzi, with a sobbing voice, said this and then sat back down on the edge of the bed. "You have no tricks to anyone; It just hit the right spot! People are born rich; we all have the foundation. Nothing more to say! Detective Sun shook his head, as if filled with endless emotion. Enough, I'm the one who wronged you, so don't keep pestering me! ”

Xiangzi thought for a while longer, but there was no other way. His hands trembled as he pulled the silent gourd jar out from under the covers.

"Let me see!" Detective Sun laughed, grabbed the clay pot, and slammed it against the wall.

Xiangzi looked at the money scattered on the ground, his heart about to split.

"Is that it?"

Xiangzi didn't speak, only trembling left.

"Forget it! I don't wipe them out; friends are friends. You should know that this money is buying a life—it's a cheap thing! ”

Before Xiangzi could speak, he trembled as he tried to wrap himself in bedding.

"But don't move!"

"It's so cold......" Xiangzi's eyes widened with rage.

"I told you not to move, so don't move! Get lost! ”

Xiangzi took a deep breath, bit his lip, and pushed the door open as he walked out.

The snow had fallen more than an inch thick, and Xiangzi walked with his head down. Everything was pure white, with only a few large black footprints left behind him.

Twelve

Xiangzi wanted to find a place to sit down and carefully reflect through everything. Even if he could only cry after thinking it over, he would know exactly what he was crying about; Things changed so fast that his mind couldn't keep up. There was nowhere for him to sit; snow was everywhere. The small teahouses had already arrived, already past ten o'clock; Even if it was open, he refused to go in. He preferred to find a quiet place, knowing that tears welling up in his eyes could fall at any moment. With nowhere to sit, I had no choice but to walk slowly; But where did he go? In this silvery world, there is no place for him to sit, nor for him to go; In the vast whiteness, only hungry little birds and those at their wit's end know what lament means.

Where to go? That became a problem, so there was no need to think about anything else for now! Xiaxiaodian? No! With just this set of clothes, he could have lost something in the middle of the night, not to mention how terrifying the lice in the shop were. A bigger store? He couldn't afford it; he only had five yuan, and it was his entire property. Going to the bathhouse? Arrive at midnight, can't stay overnight. Nowhere to go.

Because he had nowhere to go, he felt even more embarrassed. After years in the city, all I got was a set of clothes and five yuan; Even the bedding was gone! Because of this, he thought about tomorrow. What should he do tomorrow? Pulling a rickshaw, and even trying to pull one—hmph, the result of pulling a rickshaw is that you can't find a place to stay, and the remaining money is snatched away! Running a small business only requires five yuan in capital, and even carrying poles and shoulders must be bought on the spot. Besides, which trade is sure to make a profit? Pulling a cart could be made for just 0.3 or 40 yuan on flat ground, but running a small business required capital and had no guarantee of earning enough for three meals. If you spend all your capital and then pull a rickshaw, you'll just lose five yuan for nothing. You can't easily give up a single cent of these five yuan—this is your last hope! When a servant leaves, it is not in the path: when serving others, one does not know how to do so; Washing clothes, cooking, and cooking—no way! He can't do anything, can't do anything, he's just a foolish and rough loser!

Unconsciously, he arrived at Zhonghai. On the bridge, the space on both sides is empty, and as far as the eye can see, it's all snowflakes. Only then did he realize the snow hadn't stopped. He touched his head, and the woolen hat was already very wet. There was no one on the bridge, and even the police hiding had disappeared. A few lights were being struck by the snowflakes, making them seem to blink nonstop. Xiangzi looked at the snow beyond, feeling bewildered.

He stood on the bridge for a long time. The world seemed dead, silent, not a sound. The gray-white snowflakes seemed to have found an opportunity—hurried, light, and falling relentlessly, trying to bury the world without anyone noticing. In this silence, Xiangzi heard the whisper of his conscience. Better not worry about myself for now; I still need to go back and check on the Cao family first. Only Mrs. Cao and Aunt Gao remained—not a single man! Wasn't that last five yuan given by Mr. Cao? Not daring to think further, he took off his leg and walked back, very quickly.

There were some footprints outside the door, and two newly marked car lanes on the road. Could it be that Mrs. Cao has already left? Why didn't that Sun take them?

He didn't dare go over and push open the door, fearing he'd be caught again. Looking left and right, no one was there. His heart raced, so he thought, 'Let's give it a try. After all, I'm homeless, so if someone caught, so be it.' She gently pushed the door, and it was open. He walked a couple of steps along the wall and saw the light in his own room—his own room! He was about to cry. Bending over, I walked over and listened outside the window. Inside the room, I coughed—it was Aunt Gao's voice! He opened the door. "Who?" Oh, you! It scared me to death! "Aunt Gao clutched her chest, steadied herself, and sat down on the bed." Xiangzi, what's going on? ”

Xiangzi couldn't answer, feeling as if it had been many years since he last saw her, a warm flush in his heart.

"What's going on?" Aunt Gao also looked like she was about to cry and asked, "You haven't come back yet. The gentleman called and asked us to go to the Zuo residence, saying you'll be there soon." You're here—wasn't it me who opened the door for you? I saw you were still with a stranger, so I didn't say a word and hurried inside to help Madam pack. You never went in. In the darkness, my wife and I were taught to scratch blindly. The young master was already sound asleep, and Sheng was carrying him out of the warm quilt. After wrapping up, you went to the study to pick up paintings, but you never met each other. What's wrong with you? Let me ask you! Roughly tidied up, I'll come out to see you. Fine, you're nowhere to be seen! Madam was so angry—half anxious—that she was trembling. I had no choice but to call for a ride. But we can't just go with the 'Empty City Strategy' and leave it all. Alright, my wife and I slapped the bridge of our noses [1].

 I said, 'Madam, let's go, I'll watch.' When Xiangzi returned, I immediately rushed to the Zuo residence; If you don't come back, I'll accept my fate! How could you say that! What's wrong with you? Tell me! ”

Xiangzi had nothing to say.

"Speak!" Is it really okay to just stand there and settle things? What exactly happened? ”

"You should go!" Xiangzi finally found a sentence: "Let's go!" "Are you watching the house?" Gao Ma calmed down a bit.

"When you see the teacher, just say the detective caught me, but, but, again, didn't catch me!"

"What kind of talk is that?" Gao Ma was so angry she almost laughed.

"Listen up!" Xiangzi hung upside down: "Tell the gentleman to run. The detective said they'll definitely catch him." The Zuo residence is not a safe place either. Run! If you leave, I'll jump to the Wang family and sleep for the night. I locked the main gate here. Tomorrow, I'll go find my business. Sorry, Mr. Cao! ”

"The more you talk, the more confused I become!" Gao Ma sighed. "Alright, I'm leaving. The young master might still be freezing, hurry and go check on him!" When I saw the teacher, I said, 'Xiangzi told me to run quickly.' Tonight, Xiangzi locked the front door and jumped to the Wang family to sleep; Tomorrow he'll go look for trouble. Is that how it is? ”

Xiangzi nodded in deep shame.

After Gao Ma left, Xiangzi locked the door and returned inside. The muffled gourd jar was still lying on the ground. He picked up a tile to look at it and tossed it back on the ground as usual. The bedding on the bed was untouched. Strange, what on earth is going on? Could it be that Detective Sun is not a real detective? No! If Mr. Cao hadn't noticed any danger, why would he have abandoned home and run away? I don't understand! I don't understand! Unconsciously, he sat down on the edge of the bed. As soon as he sat down, he seemed startled and stood up again. We can't stay here for long! What if that Sun guy comes back?! His mind quickly circled: Sorry, Mr. Cao, but Gao Ma brought back a letter to teach him how to run fast, so at least he got by. In terms of conscience, Xiangzi did not intend to deceive anyone, and he himself was being wronged. If you lose your own money first, you can't deal with Mr. Cao anymore. Muttering to himself, he muttered as he got up and started packing up.

He picked up his bedding, turned off the lights, and headed to the backyard. He put down the bedding, gripped the wall with his hand, and called out softly, "Old Cheng!" Old Cheng! "Old Cheng is the coachman of the Wang family. No one agreed, so Xiangzi made up his mind to skip over first. He threw the bedding over, which landed on the snow without making a sound. His heart skipped a beat. He immediately climbed onto the wall and jumped over. Picking up bedding in the snow, she quietly went to find Old Cheng. He knew where Old Cheng was. It seemed everyone was asleep; there was not a single sound in the entire courtyard. Suddenly, Xiangzi felt that being a thief wasn't that difficult. He mustered his courage and walked steadily. The snow was thick and made a faint sound. He found Old Cheng's room and cleared his throat. Old Cheng seemed to have just lain down: "Who?" ”

"I, Xiangzi! Open the door! Xiangzi spoke very naturally and gently, as if he heard Old Cheng's voice, as if comforting a loved one. Old Cheng turned on the light, wearing a tattered fur coat, and opened the door: "What's wrong?" Xiangzi! It's the middle of the night! ”

Xiangzi went in, put the bedding on the floor, sat down on it, and fell silent again.

Old Cheng was in his thirties, with lumps of flesh on his face and body, hard enough to stand out. Usually, Xiangzi had no real friendship with him; they only nodded and spoke whenever they met. Sometimes, Mrs. Wang and Mrs. Cao would go out together, and the two of them would have the chance to drink tea and rest together. Xiangzi greatly admired Old Cheng. Old Cheng ran fast but was flustered and seemed to struggle to hold the handlebars steadily. In terms of character, Old Cheng was quite good, but with this flaw, Xiangzi could not fully admire him.

Today, Xiangzi found Old Cheng completely adorable. Sitting there, I couldn't say anything, but my heart was filled with gratitude and affection. Just now, standing on the bridge at Zhonghai; Now, sitting in the room with an acquaintance; The sudden changes left him feeling empty; At the same time, there was some heat rising from it.

Old Cheng crawled under the covers again, pointed at the tattered leather jacket and said, "Xiangzi, have a smoke, you have some in your pocket, don't go wild." "Since the villa brand cigarettes were introduced, the coachmen had changed them to 'bieye' (separate fields).

Xiangzi usually doesn't smoke, but this time he couldn't refuse, so he held a cigarette to his lips and smacked it.

"What's wrong?" Old Cheng asked, "Quit your job?" ”

"No," Xiangzi still sat on the bedding, "Something happened!" Mr. Cao's whole family has run away, and I don't dare watch the house alone! ”

"What kind of mess is this?" Old Cheng sat up again.

"It's hard to say. Anyway, the chaos is huge—even Aunt Gao left!"

"Four gates wide open, no one is taking notice?"

"I've locked the main gate!"

"Hmph!" Old Cheng thought for a long time, "Can I tell Mr. Wang to go?" "As he spoke, he was about to put on his clothes.

"Let's talk about it tomorrow. It's just impossible to explain!" Xiangzi was afraid Mr. Wang would interrogate him.

The things Xiangzi couldn't explain were like this: Mr. Cao taught at a university at what time it was. There was a student at school named Ruan Ming, who had always gotten along well with Mr. Cao and often came to talk to him. Mr. Cao was a socialist, while Ruan Ming's ideas were more intense, so the two got along well. However, age and status caused some minor conflicts: from a teacher's perspective, Mr. Cao believed he should teach diligently, while students should properly assign their homework and not be careless in grades due to personal feelings. In Ruan Ming's view, in such a chaotic world, an ambitious young man should do some revolutionary work, and his academic performance could be set aside for now. He and Mr. Cao interacted partly to get along well and partly to earn a promotion score through their relationship, no matter how bad his exam results were. Patriots in troubled times often have some unruly behavior; history has many such forgivable examples.

When it came time for the exam, Mr. Cao did not give Ruan Ming a passing grade. Even though Mr. Cao passed Ruan Ming's grades, he was still more than enough to skip school. But he hated Mr. Cao deeply. He thought Mr. Cao didn't care about saving face; Face in China holds the same value as revolution. Because he was eager to do something, Ruan Mingqing read the scholarly path. Because they disregard scholarship, they gradually become lazy, hoping to gain everyone's admiration and care without any effort; No matter what, my thoughts are moving forward! Mr. Cao didn't give him a passing grade, clearly because he didn't understand an ambitious young man; So, don't try to get close to each other in daily life! Since they were usually on good terms and awkwardly made the exam difficult, he thought Mr. Cao was a treacherous person. His grades were beyond repair, and suspension was impossible to resist. He wanted to vent his anger on Mr. Cao. Since he lost his education, he would bring in a tutor to help him tie him up. This way, it can be a bit pretentious while also showing off its strength. Ruan Ming is not someone to mess with! Besides, if he could use this as a way to break into a new group, it would be better than having nothing to do.

He edited the political and social issues Mr. Cao said in lectures and the casual conversations he had with him about political and social issues, and reported them to the Party headquarters—Mr. Cao was promoting radical ideas among the youth.

Mr. Cao had heard of this too, but he found it quite amusing. He knew how incomplete his socialist spirit was, and also how his traditional interest in art hindered intense action. Ridiculous, he actually got the title of revolutionary mentor! Ridiculous, so he didn't pay much attention, even though students and colleagues all told him to be careful. Calmness cannot—in troubled times—guarantee safety. Winter break is a great opportunity to purge the school, and detectives begin to busy with investigations and arrests. Mr. Cao had felt several times that someone was following him. The figure behind him shifted from laughter to seriousness. He must think about it: to build a reputation, this is a good opportunity; Spending a few days in prison is easier, safer, and more valuable than setting a bomb. Imprisonment is a qualification for important people. But he refused. He refused to turn the tables and create a false reputation for himself. With a clear conscience, he hated himself for not being able to become a warrior; With a clear conscience, he refused to be a fake warrior. He went to see Mr. Zuo. Mr. Zuo had an idea: "When necessary, move to me. They won't go so far as to search for me!" "Mr. Zuo knows people; People are more powerful than the law. "Come stay here for a few days and hide. At last, we were afraid of them. Then going to clear it might cost two more money. With enough face and money, you can go home and be fine. ”

Detective Sun knew Mr. Cao often visited the Zuo residence, and he also knew that when the pursuit got closer, he would definitely go there. They didn't dare offend Mr. Zuo, so they were just a bit intimidated by Mr. Cao. If we send him to the Zuo residence, they'll have hope of getting the money, and it'll be a big deal. Knocking on Xiangzi wasn't part of the detectives' plan, but since they saw Xiangzi, why not pick up a piece of work to help him with first?

By the way, Xiangzi happened to be at the "right spot"—serves him right. Everyone has their way; everywhere there are gaps, but Xiangzi couldn't escape, because he was a cart puller. A cart puller swallowed coarse grain, and blood spilled out; He wants to sell his greatest strength for the lowest pay; To stand at the lowest point in the world, waiting to be struck by all people, all dharmas, and all suffering.

After finishing a cigarette, Xiangzi still couldn't figure it out. He was like a chicken in the hands of a cook, just taking a breath and having no other ideas. He was very willing to talk with Old Cheng, but had nothing to say. His words didn't fully express his feelings. He had experienced all the hardships and couldn't open his mouth, like a mute. Bought a car, but lost it; Save money, lose money; All my efforts were only for others to bully me! No one dared to provoke him, not even a stray dog to hide. In the end, he was bullied so badly he couldn't breathe his breath! Don't dwell on the past for now—what about tomorrow? The Cao residence couldn't be returned; where could they go? "I'll sleep here for one night, okay?" He asked, like a stray dog finding a sheltered corner, letting it endure for a while; But even with these small matters, you have to understand and see if it will interfere with others.

"You stay here, where are you going in this icy and snowy environment? Is it okay on the ground? Come up and squeeze in! ”

Xiangzi refused to climb up and squeeze in, so the ground was fine.

Old Cheng fell asleep, but Xiangzi tossed and turned, unable to fall asleep. The chill on the floor quickly turned the mattress cold like iron. He curled up his legs, his calves seeming to cramp. The cool breeze coming through the door crack stabbed like a bunch of tiny needles to my head. He squeezed his eyes shut, covered his head, and couldn't sleep. Hearing Old Cheng's shout, he grew anxious, wishing he could stand up and beat Old Cheng up to satisfy him. It got colder and colder, making his throat itch, and he was afraid Old Cheng would cough and wake him up.

Unable to sleep, he really wanted to sneak up and visit the Cao residence again. Anyway, the matter was blown up, and there was no one in the courtyard, so why not go get a few things? The little money he had painstakingly saved was stolen by someone, and he was taken away for the sake of the Cao residence. Why couldn't he steal something? Losing money for the Cao residence and then having the Cao family compensate for it is just right, isn't it? Thinking this way, his eyes lit up, and he instantly forgot about the cold; Let's go! Such hard-won money, lost, then so easily come back, let's go!

He had already sat up, then hurriedly lay down, as if Old Cheng was watching him! His heart skipped a beat. No, I can't stand against the bandits, I can't! I just stripped myself clean and didn't do what Mr. Cao instructed, which is already a letdown; How could I steal him again? You can't go! Poor to death, no stealing!

How do you know others won't steal? Who knows if that Sun guy takes some things? He sat up again. In the distance, a dog barked a few times. He lay down again. Still can't go; others will steal it. Steal it, but have a clear conscience. He was so poor that he couldn't let another dark mark on his heart!

Besides, Aunt Gao knows he's coming to the Wang family. If something goes missing at night, it has to be him, and whether it's not, it has to be him! Not only did he refuse to steal, he was also afraid someone else would break in. If he really lost something that night, he wouldn't be able to clear his name even if he jumped into the Yellow River! He wasn't cold anymore, but a bit of sweat appeared on his palms. What should be done? Jump back into the house to watch? I dare not. His life was bought with money; he couldn't walk into a trap again. If you don't go, what if you lose something?

I couldn't figure it out. He sat up again, hunched over, his head almost knee-deep. My head felt heavy, and I wanted to close my eyes, but I didn't dare to sleep. The night was so long, Xiangzi barely had time to close his eyes.

He sat for an unknown length of time, and his minds changed countless times. Suddenly, his heart lit up, and he reached out to push Old Cheng: "Old Cheng!" Old Cheng! Wake up! ”

"What are you doing?" Old Cheng was very reluctant to open his eyes: "Urine, there's a chamber pot under the bed." ”

"Wake up! Turn on the lights! ”

"What's the problem with the thief?" Old Cheng groggily sat up.

"You've woken up and understood?"

"Mm!"

"Old Cheng, look! This is my bedding, this is my clothes, and this is the five yuan Mr. Cao gave me; Nothing else? ”

"That's it, what are you doing?" Old Cheng yawned.

"You've woken up and understood?" That's all I have. I didn't take a single blade of grass or tree from the Cao family? ”

"No! We guys have been eating at the mansion for so long—can our hands be sticky and superficial? If you can do it, do it; If you can't do it, don't do it; You can't take their things! Is this the case? ”

"You understood it now?"

Old Cheng laughed, "That's right! I said, aren't you cold? ”

"Alright!"
