Learning Resource Sharing 17

Xiangzi slowly learned about the people and the factory: Liu Siye sold part of the car and gave the rest to a well-known car owner in Xicheng. Xiangzi could guess that the old man was getting old, and without his daughter's help, he couldn't manage the business, so he simply closed it and took the money to enjoy life himself. Where did he go? Xiangzi hadn't found out.

He couldn't say whether he liked or disliked this news. From his own ambitions and boldness, Liu Siye was determined to abandon his daughter, so Huniu's plan was completely ruined; He could honestly pull a rickshaw to earn a living, without relying on anyone. Considering Liu Siye's modest wealth, it was truly a bit of a pity; Who knew how Old Liu managed to get the money out? He and Huniu didn't even get a single coin of copper in it.

But at this point, he didn't really think about it, nor did he feel tempted. He thought, since his strength was his own and he was willing to work hard to earn money, making a living wouldn't be a problem. Without any emotion, he simply told Huniu. She was quite tempted. Hearing this, she immediately saw her future clearly—it's over! What do you mean, it's all over! She had no choice but to be the coachman's wife for life! She would never escape this crowded courtyard! She had imagined her father would marry another wife, but never expected to leave so easily. If the old man really marries a concubine, Huniu will fight over the inheritance, and maybe even manage to contact his stepmother, and he will gain some benefits...... He had plenty of ideas, as long as the old man kept running the car factory. She never expected the old man to be so resolute and ruthless, turning all his assets into cash and secretly hiding them! When she first fell out with him, she thought it was just a tactic and that they would soon reconcile. She knew that she couldn't be without her; Who would have thought the old man would let go of the car factory?!

Spring has arrived, and the scales on the branches are already prominently red and plump. But in this large courtyard, spring does not arrive first on the branches; there is not a single flowering tree here. Here, the spring breeze first blows the ice in the courtyard into small pockmarks, blowing out a fishy scent from the filthy soil, and blowing trivial petty scraps and scraps of paper into the corners of the walls, creating a small whirlwind. People in the courtyard are troubled all year round. Only now do the elderly dare to come out and bask in the warmth; Only now have the young girls removed a bit of the soot stain from their noses, revealing a bit of reddish-yellow skin; Only then did those women dare to send the children out to the courtyard to play with shame; Only then did those children dare to use a piece of torn paper as a kite and run around the courtyard without letting their little black hands crack open from the cold. However, porridge factories stopped cooking, relief workers stopped giving rice, and charity workers stopped putting in money; It seems as if the suffering people are all entrusted to the spring breeze and spring light! It was just as the spring wheat was fresh and green like grass, and the old grain was scarce, so as usual, the price of grain and rice had increased. The sky grew longer, and even the elderly couldn't lie down early to deceive their hungry hearts with dreams. Spring has come to the world, but in this crowded courtyard, difficulties only increase. Elderly lice—especially fierce—sometimes crawl outside the cotton lumps of the elderly or children, to enjoy a glimpse of spring!

Huniu looked at the melting ice in the courtyard, along with the tattered clothes, smelled the complex, slightly warm scent, and listened to the elders' laments and the cries of children, feeling a chill in her heart. In winter, people hide indoors, and all the filth freezes on the ice; Now, people have come out, and the things have been revealed. Even the broken brick walls are falling to earth, as if they are about to collapse in the rain. The courtyard was filled with colorful flowers, blooming with miserable flowers, several times uglier than in winter. Hmph, just at this moment, she felt she would live here forever; Her little money would eventually run out, while Xiangzi was just a rickshaw puller!

She told Xiangzi to watch the house, then went to Nanyuan to find her aunt and inquire about the old man. Aunt said Fourth Master did visit her home once, probably on the twelfth day of the first lunar month. First, to thank her, and second, to tell her he planned to go to Tianjin or Shanghai for some fun. He said: Having spent a lifetime without ever leaving the capital, you can't really be called a hero. Take advantage of your pride and go everywhere to see for yourself. Besides, he himself couldn't face the city anymore, because his daughter had embarrassed him. Her aunt's report only covers this point, so her judgment is even simpler: maybe the old man really is out, maybe he's just talking about it, hiding somewhere secluded; Who knows!

Back home, she buried herself on the kang bed, crying quietly for a long time, without any pretense or cunning, until her eyes were swollen.

After crying, she wiped her tears and said to Xiangzi, "Fine, you're so bold!" It all depends on you! I bet on the wrong place this time. If you marry a chicken, follow the chicken—there's nothing more to say. I'll give you 100 yuan, and you can buy a car and drive it! ”

Here, she was cautious: she originally wanted to buy two cars—one for Xiangzi to pull himself, and one to rent. Now she had changed her mind and only bought one, asking Xiangzi to pull it along; The rest of the money was still kept in his own hands. The money is in her own hands, but the power is also with herself; she refuses to hand it all over; What if Xiangzi—after spending all his money on a car—changes his heart? This can't be left unguarded! Besides, with Old Liu gone like this, she felt unreliable about everything. No one could know what tomorrow would bring. At best, she could be happy and have some money in hand, able to eat whatever she liked. She had always been used to snacks. Taking what Xiangzi earned — he was a first-class rickshaw puller — to get by, and with his own money to cover his pocket money, he decided to focus on his immediate pleasures first. There is a day when money is spent, but people don't live forever! Marrying a rickshaw puller—though out of necessity—was already a grievance to herself. She couldn't keep asking him for money every day, with no copper coins in her pocket. This decision made her a little happier. Although she knew the future would be impressive, she wouldn't immediately turn her head down; It was as if by sunset, the distance had already dimmed, but there was still some light ahead. He decided to take a few extra steps while the light was bright.

Xiangzi didn't argue with her; buying one was enough. As long as it was his own car, he could at least make six or seventy cents a day, enough to eat grain. Not only did he not argue, he actually felt somewhat pleased. All the hardships I endured in the past were just about buying a car. If you can buy it now, what is there to say? Naturally, if one cart supplies food for two people, there will be no money left; This car sometimes gets old, but you don't prepare to buy a new one—dangerous! But since buying a car is so difficult, being able to buy it now should be enough to satisfy you. Why think so far ahead?

Erqiangzi in the courtyard was about to sell the car. Last summer, Erqiangzi sold his daughter Xiaofuzi—nineteen years old—to a soldier. It sold for 200 yuan. After Xiao Fuzi left, Erqiangzi was quite wealthy for a while, ransomed the pawn, and even made a few new clothes. The whole family was dressed neatly. Erqiang's wife was the shortest and ugliest woman in the entire courtyard, with a prominent forehead, large cheeks, little hair, old teeth sticking out, and her face covered in freckles, making her look disgusting. She also had red eyelids, crying for her daughter while putting on a new blue coat. Er Qiangzi has always had a bad temper; after selling his daughter, he often drank several cups of wine; Tears in the eyes after drinking are especially easy to spot. Although Erqiang's wife put on her new big shirt and ate her fill, her joy was overwhelming, and the number of times she got beaten had nearly doubled compared to before. Er Qiangzi is over forty and plans not to pull a rickshaw anymore. So I bought a basket and a grocery cart—melons, pears, peaches, peanuts, cigarettes—a complete stock. After two months of trading, a rough stack of accounts not only lost money, but also lost a lot. Used to pulling carts, he won't handle business dealings; Pulling a cart is a matter of charging and crashing; if you succeed, you succeed; if not, forget it. Doing small business is tough, but he doesn't know how. The rickshaw puller knows how to buy things on credit, so he can't let acquaintances owe debts; If you owe it, it's hard to get it back. That way, if you take care of the good owner and don't give them back, and those who trade with him are greedy and refuse to pay, he can't avoid losing money. If he loses money, he feels sad; When you're sad, drink more. When drunk, he often argued with patrol officers outside, and at home he acted with his wife and children. Offending patrol officers, beating wives—all because of alcohol. When he sobered up, he was filled with regret and pain. Thinking further, he realized that this money was bought with his daughter, and that was paid for nothing, and he even drank and hit people. He felt he was not human. At times like this, he could sleep in for a day and hand over his worries to dreams.

He decided to give up the business and go pull a rickshaw, not wanting to waste all that money. He bought a car. When he was drunk, he was completely unreasonable. When he is sober, he loves to be respectable. Because he loved appearances, he often put on a poor front, always following a plan. He bought a new rickshaw and was dressed neatly. He felt he was a high-class coachman who needed to drink good tea and pull a decent seat. He could stand at the carriage door, showing off his own car and wearing white pants and jackets, chatting with everyone, always disdainful of arranging business. One moment he would slap the car with a new blue cloth duster, sometimes he would stomp his new white double-faced shoes, sometimes he would look at the tip of his nose and stand by the car smiling, waiting for others to praise his car, then spark conversation endlessly. He could spend a day or two like this. When he pulled onto a good seat, his legs couldn't keep up with his bike or clothes, and he couldn't run! This made him very sad again. Whenever she felt sad, she thought of her daughter and had no choice but to go drink. In this way, all his money was spent on the car, leaving only that car.

Around the beginning of winter, he got drunk again. As soon as they entered, their two sons—one thirteen and one eleven—tried to hide. This move knocked him down, giving each of them a kick. Erqiang's wife said something, and he rushed to her, kicking her in the stomach. She lay on the ground for a long time without making a sound. The two kids panicked—one grabbed a coal shovel, the other grabbed a rolling pin, and fought desperately against their father. The three of them fought together, all hands and feet stomping on Erqiang's wife a few more times. The neighbors came over and finally managed to pin Erqiangzi down on the kang. The two children hugged their mother and started crying. Erqiang's wife woke up, but she still couldn't get out of the fields. By the third day of the twelfth lunar month, her breathing stopped, and she wore the blue coat she had made when she sold her daughter. Erqiang's wife's family refused and insisted on a lawsuit. After persistent persuasion from friends, her family finally gave in. Er Qiangzi agreed to send her off properly and gave her fifteen yuan. He escorted the car out and pawned sixty yuan. The following year, he wanted to sell that car, but he had no hope of redeeming it himself. When drunk, he wanted to sell his son, but no one wanted him. He had also sought out Xiao Fuzi's husband, but he never acknowledged him as a father-in-law, so there was no need to say anything else.

Xiangzi knew the history of this car and didn't like it. There were plenty of cars, so why buy just this one? This unlucky car, the one he traded for his daughter, and only sold it after beating his wife to death! Huniu didn't see it that way; she wanted to buy it for just over eighty—cheap! The truck had only been running for half a year, and even the color of the belt hadn't changed much. Plus, it was made by Decheng, a famous factory in Xicheng. Buying a 70% new car—wouldn't that cost 50 or 60 yuan? She couldn't bear to part with this bargain. She also knew that not long after the New Year, money was tight everywhere, and Erqiangzi wouldn't sell for a high price, but he was eagerly waiting for cash. She personally went to check out the car, negotiated the price with Er Qiangzi, and after the payment; Xiangzi could only wait to pull the cart, saying nothing and not saying much—since the money wasn't his own. After buying the car, he looked at it carefully—it really was tough. But he still felt a bit awkward. What annoyed him most was the black paint of the car, paired with the white brass craftsmanship. When Er Qiangzi drove the car, the black and white contrast beautifully; Xiangzi always felt this was a bit discouraged, like mourning clothes. He really wanted to change the condom, one in earthy yellow or moon white, which would be enough to reduce the simplicity and purity. But he didn't discuss it with Huniu to avoid getting her gossip again.

When this car was pulled out, everyone paid special attention—someone even called it the 'Little Widow.' Xiangzi felt uneasy. He tried every possible way not to think about it, but the car followed him all day long. He was fussy and fussy, as if something might go wrong at any moment. Sometimes he suddenly thought of Er Qiangzi and his experiences; it was as if he wasn't pulling a cart, but rather a coffin. On this car, he often saw ghostly shadows, as if they were.

However, ever since he got into the car, nothing had gone wrong, although he was still feeling uneasy inside. The weather is getting warmer, and with the cotton off, you hardly need to wear a jacket—just a single pants and a single jacket; There isn't much spring in Beiping. The sky is so long it almost makes people impatient, and everyone feels drowsy. Xiangzi went out early in the morning, wandering around until four or five o'clock, feeling he had enough energy. The sun is still very high. He didn't want to run anymore, but also refused to take the car, letting out a long, lazy yawn hesitantly.

The days are so long; if Xiangzi feels tired or bored, Huniu feels even lonelier at home. In winter, she could warm herself by the stove, listen to the wind outside, and although it was frustrating, she still had a bit of "it's better not to go out" as a kind of self-pleasure. Now, with the stove moved under the eaves, there was practically nothing to do inside the house. The courtyard was so dirty and foul, not even a single blade of grass remained. When he went out on the street, he worried about the neighbors, so even if he went shopping, he had to go straight and not dare wander around for a while. She was like a bee trapped inside the house, staring blankly at the sunlight outside and unable to fly out. She couldn't talk with the women in the courtyard. What they talk about is family trivial matters, but she is used to being wild and silent, not liking to talk or listen to such things. Their grievances stem from the pain of life; every small thing can bring tears; Her grievances were dissatisfaction with life; she had no tears to shed but wanted to scold someone to vent her frustration. She and the others couldn't really understand each other, so they each did their own thing and didn't need to talk too much[1]

 。

It wasn't until mid-April that she finally had a companion. Er Qiangzi's daughter, Xiaofuzi, has returned. Xiao Fuzi's "People"[2]

 He was an officer. He built a simple home everywhere, spent a hundred or two hundred to get a young girl, bought a large mat and two chairs, and could live happily for a while. When the army was redeployed elsewhere, he let go and left, leaving both the man and the bedding back where they were. Spending a hundred or two hundred yuan like this lasts a year or so without losing out. Just for small tasks like sewing, laundry, cooking, and other small tasks, if you hire a servant, wouldn't the monthly expenses for both food and earnings be about ten or eight yuan? Marrying a girl like this means being both a servant and sleeping with her, and ensuring she is clean and healthy. Happy, I cut her a floral cloth coat—just a matter of a little money. If you're not happy, teaching her to just stay at home with her eyes is something she can't do. When he was away on a business trip, he didn't mind the bedding and one or two chairs, because she had to find a way to pay the two months' rent. If she sold the bedding and other things, it wouldn't be enough to pay off the debt.

Xiao Fuzi had sold the bedding and paid the rent, coming home wearing only a floral cloth coat and a pair of silver earrings.

After selling the car, Erqiangzi had about twenty yuan left aside from repaying the mortgage and interest. Sometimes he feels it's just losing his wife in middle age, feeling very pitiful; Since others didn't pity him, he drank a cup of wine himself, drank good food, and pity himself and soothe himself. At times like this, it was as if he had a grudge against money, spending it recklessly. Sometimes he thought he should work harder to pull the rickshaw, raise the two boys well, and have some hope for the future. Thinking of his son, he hurriedly bought a huge pile of food for the two of them to eat. Watching them wolf down those things, tears welled up in his eyes as he muttered to himself, "A child without a mother!" A child with a hard life! Dad goes to work hard, but he's after his child! I refuse to give in. Whether I eat or not doesn't matter; I have to make sure the child eats enough first! Eat up! When you grow up, don't forget me! "At times like this, he spends quite a bit of money. Gradually, about twenty yuan was all put forward.

Without money, and when he got drunk and lost his temper, he didn't care what the children ate for a day or two. The children couldn't do it and had to come up with ideas to get a few copper coins and buy something to eat. They would beat the stewards for weddings and funerals, pick up scraps of copper and paper along with the cart, sometimes buy a few baked buns, sometimes only a jin of stubble and sweet potatoes, swallowing the skin and whiskers. Sometimes, only one big copper coin for two people could buy peanuts or iron broad beans. Although they couldn't stave off hunger, they could chew a bit longer.

Xiao Fuzi came back, and when they saw their loved ones, each hugging one of her legs, they had nothing to say, only tears streaming down their faces as they smiled at her. Mom is gone, but my sister is Mom!

Er Qiangzi showed no reaction to his daughter's return. When she came back, she added another person to eat. But seeing his two sons so happy, he couldn't deny that there should be a daughter at home to cook and do laundry for everyone. He didn't want to say much; wherever he went, he decided to go as he pleased.

Xiao Fuzi isn't unattractive. Although he used to be very thin and small, ever since he started following that officer, he'd gained a lot of weight and grown taller. She has a round face, well-proportioned eyebrows and eyes, nothing particularly remarkable, but she's solid and not unattractive. The upper lip is very short; whether angry or smiling, it opens its lips first, revealing some white, neat teeth. That officer was especially fond of her teeth. Showing these teeth, she looked a bit silly and clueless, and at the same time, seemed a bit playful. This bit of pride made her—like any poor but not beautiful girl—like flowers and plants, picking up even a hint of fragrance or color to be sold in the market.

Huniu usually ignored the people in the courtyard, but she regarded Xiaofuzi as a friend. First, Xiao Fuzi looked somewhat good; second, he wore a floral robe of foreign fabric; third, Huniu thought that since she had married an officer, she had seen the world, so she agreed to associate with her. Women find it hard to make friends, but when they do, they do get along quickly; Within days, the two of them had become close friends. Huniu loved snacks. Whenever she got something like sunflower seeds, she would always call Xiaofuzi over, chatting and laughing as they ate. Amid their laughter, Xiao Fuzi foolishly showed his white teeth and told many things that Huniu had never heard before. She didn't enjoy the good fortune with the officer, but when the officer was in high spirits, he took her back to the restaurant to watch the opera, so she had many things to say and tell Huniu to make Huniu envious. She still has many things she cannot say: for her, it is ravaging; At Huniu, this is a pleasure. Hu Niuyang told her, saying she was too embarrassed to say it, but also too embarrassed to refuse. She had seen the erotic palace, but Huniu had never seen it. Things like this, Huniu heard it once, and now she loves to hear it a second time. She saw Xiaofuzi as the cutest, most enviable, and also someone worthy of jealousy. After hearing all that, and looking at her own appearance, age, and husband, she felt her whole life had been so wronged. She never had youth, and there was no hope for the future. Now, Xiangzi was once again like a dead brick! The more dissatisfied she was with Xiangzi, the more she loved Xiaofuzi. Although Xiaofuzi was so poor and pitiful, in her eyes he was someone who had enjoyed life and witnessed formations. Even if he died soon, it wouldn't be unfair. In her view, Xiaofuzi was the perfect example of the enjoyment a woman should have.

Huniu seemed not to see Xiaofuzi's hardship. Xiao Fuzi didn't bring anything back, but she had to—no matter how much her father insisted—take care of her two brothers. Where was she supposed to get money to prepare meals for them?

Erqiangzi, drunk, came up with an idea: "If you truly care about your brothers, you'll have a way to earn money to support them!" They're all counting on me. I spend all day being livestock for others, so I have to eat my fill first; Can I run empty-stomached? Teach me to fall to my death, what do you think of Cola? You're just idle, you have ready-made ones—if you don't sell, what are you waiting for? ”

Looking at his father like a drunken cat, at himself, at his two younger brothers starving like mice, Xiao Fuzi was left crying. Tears can't move her father, nor can they feed her brother. She has to show something more genuine. To feed her brothers, she had to sell her own meat. Holding her little brother, her tears fell onto his hair as he said, "Sister, I'm hungry!" "Sister! The older sister is like meat, it has to be eaten by the younger brother!

Not only did Huniu not comfort Xiaofuzi, she was actually willing to help her: she was willing to contribute some capital to teach her how to dress up and repay her with the money she earned. Huniu was willing to lend her a place because her own room was too dirty, and Huniu's house had at least a Xiangzi. Besides, there were two rooms, so everyone had a place to turn around. Since Xiangzi wouldn't come back during the day, Huniu was happy to help her friend, and she could see more and understand more about what she lacked, things she couldn't do even if she wanted to. Every time Xiaofuzi used a room, Huniu made a condition: she had to give her twenty cents. Friends are friends, and matters are matters. For Xiao Fuzi's sake, she had to tidy up the house well. She had to work and spend more money. Wouldn't buying a broom and dustpan cost money? Two dimes was definitely not much; because they were friends, they could only meet face to face.

Xiao Fuzi bared some teeth, tears falling into his stomach.

Xiangzi knew nothing, but he couldn't sleep well either. Huniu "granted" Xiaofuzi, and also sought to find lost youth in Xiangzi.

[1]

 'Passing the talk' means having a conversation.

[2]

 Here, 'person' refers to a man. This term is reserved for informal relationships between men and women.

Eighteen

By June, the courtyard was almost deserted during the day. Children pick up the early morning and pick up whatever they can find in broken baskets; By nine o'clock, the scorching sun was about to crack their thin backs, so they had to take back what they had found and eat whatever adults could give them. Then, if the bigger ones can find the smallest capital in the world, they buy and pick up some ice cores to sell. If they couldn't find that capital, they would leave the city together to bathe in the moat, steal a few pieces of coal at the station, or catch some dragonflies and cicadas to sell to the children of wealthy families. The smaller ones didn't dare run far; they all went to the trees outside the door, picked locust worms, and dug up 'Vajra' (Diamond Worms)[1]

 Going out to have fun or something. Children all went out, men went out, women all went out naked inside, and no one wanted to come out; It wasn't that he was afraid it would look bad, but because the ground in the courtyard was already scorching to the feet.

Only when the sun was about to set did the men and children gradually return. At that moment, the courtyard had the shadow of the wall and some cool breeze, and the room was enclosed by the day's warmth, like a fire cage; Everyone sat in the courtyard, waiting for the women to cook. At this moment, the courtyard was bustling, as if it were a market without goods. Everyone had been in the heat all day, their eyes red and their tempers bad; Hungry again, everyone was anxious and pale. One wrong sentence can lead some to hit their children, others to hit their wives; Even if the fight didn't break out, they would curse to their heart's content. The commotion continued until everyone had finished eating. Some children would lie in the courtyard and fall asleep, while others would go out to the street to have fun.

 。 After the adults ate their fill, their tempers became much calmer, and the talkative ones gathered in groups of three or five, sharing the day's hardships. Those who have no food are left to pawn, and there is nowhere to sell them—even if there is something to pawn or sell—for night is already falling. No matter how hot the room was, the man would bury himself on the kang, silent, perhaps shouting loudly at the curse. The woman, with tears in her eyes, pleaded with everyone, hitting countless obstacles before finally borrowing a twenty-bill bill. Clutching this precious bill, she went out to get some mixed noodles and bought a pot of porridge for everyone to eat.

Huniu and Xiaofuzi are not part of this life order. Huniu is pregnant, and this time it's real. Xiangzi left early in the morning, but she always didn't get up until eight or nine o'clock; The traditional mistaken belief that pregnancy shouldn't exercise much, Hu Niu believes this and uses it to express some identity: everyone has to get up early and get up early, and only she can lie down as long as she wants. At night, she would take a small stool to a windy spot outside the street gate and sit down, only coming in after most people in the courtyard had fallen asleep. She disdained chatting idly with everyone.

Xiao Fuzi also got up late, but she had her own reason. She was afraid the men in the courtyard would glance at her sideways, so she waited until they had all left before daring to step outside. During the day, she either invited Huniu to come or went out for walks, because her advertisement was herself. At night, to avoid the attention of people in the courtyard, she went out to stroll the streets, making sure everyone had lyn down before sneaking in quietly.

Among men, Xiangzi and Erqiangzi were exceptions. Xiangzi was afraid to enter this courtyard, and even more afraid to walk inside. The poor talk from the people in the courtyard made him anxious, and he preferred to find a quiet place to sit alone. Inside, he increasingly felt that Huniu was like a tigress. The cabin was so hot and suffocating, and with that tiger on top of that, he felt like he couldn't breathe as soon as he entered. A few days ago, he couldn't stay away from coming back early, so as not to make a fuss with Huniu with him. Recently, with Xiao Fuzi by her side, she hasn't managed him much anymore, so he comes back a bit later.

As for Erqiangzi, he hardly comes home lately. He knew about his daughter's business and was too embarrassed to enter that street. But he couldn't stop her; he knew he didn't have the strength to support his children. He had no choice but to come back, out of sight, out of mind. Sometimes he hates his daughter. If Xiao Fuzi were a boy, Guan Bao wouldn't have embarrassed himself like this; If she's a girl, why cast her here! Sometimes he pities his daughter, who sells herself to support her two younger brothers! Whether he hated or hurt, he had no choice. When he got drunk and had no money left, he no longer hated or pitied her, and came back to ask her for money. At times like this, he saw his daughter as someone who could earn money, and as a father, asking her for money was only right. At this point, he also remembered his dignity: didn't everyone look down on Xiao Fuzi? Her father didn't let her off either. He forced her to take the money and cursed him as if he was cursing everyone—Er Qiangzi was not wrong, but Xiao Fuzi was born shameless.

He was noisy, and little Fuzi didn't even breathe. But Huniu was half scolding and half persuading. If she got rid of him, of course he had to have some money on hand. This money only allowed him to drink again, because if he saw them clearly, he would jump into the river or hang himself.

On June 15th, the heat was so intense it went crazy. As soon as the sun came out, the ground seemed to be on fire. Some gray vapor, neither clouds nor mist, floated low in the air, making one feel suffocated. Not a trace of wind. Xiangzi looked at the gray-red sky in the courtyard, planning to go take Wan'er—to go out after four in the afternoon; If he couldn't make money, he could keep dragging it out until dawn: nighttime was always better than daytime.

Huniu urged him to leave, worried he might get in the way at home, worried that Xiao Fuzi might bring in a guest. "Do you think being at home is comfortable? By noon, even the walls inside the house were hot! ”

He didn't say a word, drank a ladle of cold water, and walked out.

The willows on the street look sick, their leaves covered in dust and curling on the branches; The branches are too lazy to move, drooping listlessly. There wasn't a single drop of water on the road, just a dry white glow. Dust rose high on the road, merging with the ashes in the sky, forming a vicious gray sand formation that scorched the faces of passersby. Everywhere is dry, everywhere is hot to the touch, everywhere is stifling—the entire old town feels like a burned brick kiln, making it hard to breathe. Dogs lie on the ground, sticking out their red tongues, mules and horses have their nostrils wide open, vendors dare not shout, and the asphalt melts; Even the copper plaque in front of the shop seemed to be melted in the sun. The street was unusually quiet, except for the monotonous clanging of the copper and iron shops that made people restless. The people pulling the carts knew that without moving there would be no food, they were too lazy to arrange trades: some left the carts in shady places, set up sheds, and dozed off on the carts; Some sneak into small teahouses to drink tea; Some didn't even get a car out at all, but went out to the street to see if there was a chance to get a car. Those who were doing business, even the most handsome young men, were content to lose face, not daring to run away, just walking slowly with their heads down. Every well platform became their savior; no matter how many steps they had taken, they would run to the well as soon as they saw it; Unable to catch up with the freshly drawn water, he and the donkeys and horses would sip in the trough. Others, after heatstroke or sha rash, end up collapsed on the ground and never get up.

Even Xiangzi was starting to feel a bit timid! After walking a few steps with the empty car, he felt heat swirling from his face to his feet, even sweating on the backs of his hands. But when he saw the seat, he still wanted to pull it, thinking maybe running would give him some wind. He pulled up a deal, pulled the cart up, and only then realized the weather had become so severe that no one was allowed to work. Once running, he would be out of breath, and his lips would be parched. Even though he knew he wasn't thirsty, he would still want to drink water at the sight of it. If you don't run, the scorching sun will crack your hands and back. At least he managed to get to the spot, and his pants and jacket were completely wrapped around him. Picking up the banana fan, the wind is hot. He had already drunk several puffs of cold water, but then ran off to the teahouse. After drinking two pots of hot tea, he felt a bit more at ease. The tea enters the mouth, and sweat immediately flows out from the body, as if the body is hollow, no longer storing any moisture. He didn't dare move anymore.

After sitting for a long time, he grew tired of it. He didn't dare go out, but with nothing to do, he felt as if the weather was deliberately making things difficult for him. No, he couldn't back down. He had been pulling a rickshaw for more than a day, and this wasn't his first time in summer. He couldn't just "soak" for nothing. He wanted to go out, but his legs were too lazy to move. His body was very weak, as if he hadn't showered thoroughly. Although he sweated a lot, his heart still felt uncomfortable. After sitting for a while longer, he couldn't sit still any longer. After all, sitting still made him sweat, so he decided to go out and give it a try.

Only after coming out did I realize my mistake. The gray mist in the sky had dissipated, and it was no longer stifling, but the sunlight was much stronger: no one dared to look up at where the sun was; all was dazzling everywhere—in the sky, on rooftops, on walls, on the ground, everything was bright white, with a hint of red in the white; From top to bottom, the entire beam looked like a huge fire mirror, with each beam of light acting as a focal point of the fire mirror, making anything so intense it would catch fire. In this white light, every color was dazzling, every sound unpleasant, and every scent mixed with the fishy stench evaporating from the ground. The street seemed deserted, and the road suddenly widened considerably, empty and without any coolness, pale and frightening. Xiangzi didn't know how he had recovered. He kept his head down, pulled the cart, and walked forward very slowly, without ideas or purpose, dazed, covered in sticky sweat and reeking of stench. After walking for a while, the soles of my feet and my shoes and socks stuck together, as if stepping on a patch of wet mud, which made me feel very sad. He originally didn't want to drink any more water, but upon seeing the well, he couldn't help but take another breath. Not to quench his thirst, but to enjoy the cool air from the well water, he suddenly felt a chill, his pores contracting sharply, shivering in comfort. After drinking, he burped repeatedly, the water about to rise!

He walked for a while, sat for a while, and was always too lazy to arrange any business. Even by noon, he still didn't feel hungry. Wanting to eat something as usual, just seeing food makes you feel sick. His stomach was almost filled with all kinds of water, sometimes making a soft sound, like a mule or horse drinking it with a glistening sound.

Comparing winter to summer, Xiangzi always thought winter was more frightening. He never expected Xia Tian to feel so uncomfortable. He had spent more than a summer in the city, and he couldn't remember it ever being this hot. Is the weather hotter than usual, or is it that your body is weak? Thinking this way, he suddenly felt less drowsy, and his heart seemed to sink cold. His own body—yes, his body couldn't hold on anymore! He was scared, but there was nothing he could do. He couldn't drive Hu Niu away; he was about to become Erqiangzi, the tall guy he met that time, and Little Ma'er's grandfather. Xiangzi is finished!

Around 1 p.m., he started another deal. It was the hottest time of the day, and it happened to be the hottest day of the summer, but he decided to go for a run. He does not care how hot it is under the sun: if he does nothing after a trip, it proves his body is not bad; If they can't pull off this deal, then what's there to say? It's better to fall and die on the angry ground!

After just a few steps, he felt a bit of cool air, like a bit of chill coming through the crack in a scorching hot room. He couldn't believe himself; Looking at the willow branches by the roadside, they did indeed move slightly. Suddenly, more people joined the street. The shopkeepers rushed outside, all clutching palm fans to cover their heads, searching everywhere: "There's a cool breeze!" With the cool breeze! The cool breeze is blowing! "Everyone was almost jumping up and shouting. The willow trees by the roadside suddenly turned into angels, conveying messages from above: "The willow branches have moved!" Heavens, please bless us with a bit more cool breeze! ”

Still hot, but my heart was much calmer. The cool breeze, even just a little, gives people much hope. After a few cool breezes, the sunlight was less strong—sometimes bright, sometimes dim, as if a patch of flying sand was floating on it. The wind suddenly picked up, and the willow branch, which had been motionless for a long time, seemed to have received something delightful. It swayed gently, and the branches seemed to have grown a section. A gust of wind passed, darkness fell, and dust flew into the air. Some dust settled, and dark clouds like ink appeared on the northern horizon. Xiangzi was no longer sweating. He glanced north, stopped the car, and covered it with a rain cloth. He knew that summer rain could come at any moment, leaving no room for delay.

Just as the rain cloth was put on, another gust of wind blew and the rolling black clouds darkened half the sky. The heat from the ground mixed with the cool breeze, mixed with the fishy, dry earth, feeling both cool and hot; The southern half of the sky was clear and bright, while the northern half was dark as ink, as if a great disaster was approaching, and everything was in panic. The coachmen hurried to lay down the rain cloth, shopkeepers busied themselves with their covers, and street vendors hurriedly packed up their stalls, while those on the road hurried forward. Another gust of wind. When the wind passed, the street signs, stalls, and pedestrians seemed to be swept away, vanishing except for willow branches dancing wildly in the wind.

Before the clouds covered the sky, the ground was already very dark. The bright and hot Qingnoon suddenly turned into night. The wind carried the rain stars, as if searching for something on the ground, fluttering chaotically from one end to the other. In the distance to the north, a flash of red appeared, as if a cloud had been lifted open, revealing a large patch of blood. The wind was calm, but the wind was sharp and powerful, making people tremble. After such a gust of wind passed, everything seemed to change somehow; even the willow tree was anxious, waiting for something. Another flash was overhead, and bright white raindrops fell right after, kicking up a lot of dust with a faint rain in the soil. Heavy raindrops hit Xiangzi's back several times, making him shiver twice. The rain stopped, and dark clouds spread across the sky. Another gust of wind, stronger than before, willow branches fly across, dust spreads in all directions, rain falls in the path; Wind, earth, and rain all blend together, forming a vast patch of gray and cold cold both horizontally and vertically. Everything is wrapped inside, making it impossible to tell where the trees are, the earth, or the clouds. All directions are chaotic, full of sounds, all confused. The wind passed, leaving only a straight rain channel, dragging down the sky and earth, making it impossible to see the strips, just one patch. Suddenly, countless arrows shot up from the ground, and thousands of waterfalls fell from the houses. Within minutes, heaven and earth became inseparable. The river in the sky poured down, while the river on the ground flowed across, forming a gray, dim, sometimes bright water.

Xiangzi's clothes were already soaked through, with not a single dry or loose spot on his body; Through the straw hat, his hair was completely soaked. Water on the ground has passed above your feet, making it hard to take a step; The rain above hit his head and back, sweeping across his face and wrapping around his crotch. He couldn't lift his head, open his eyes, breathe, or take a step. He seemed to stand in the water, unsure where the path was, or what was in front or behind, left or right, only feeling bone-chilling water pouring over him everywhere. He knew nothing, only a haze of warmth in his heart, and the sound of rain beside his ears. He wanted to put the car down, but didn't know where to put it. He wanted to run, but water wrapped around his legs. He was half-dead and half-alive, head down, dragging forward step by step. The passengers seemed to die on board, silently letting the driver struggle for his life in the water.

The rain eased a bit. Xiangzi straightened his back slightly and exhaled: "Sir, let's avoid the distance before we leave!" ”

"Run! What are you doing by leaving me here? The passenger stomped his foot and shouted.

Xiangzi really wanted to forcibly put the car down and find a place to hide. But looking at his body, he saw water already flowing down. He knew that if he stopped, he'd shiver all over. He gritted his teeth and waded through the water, running away regardless of the height or depth. He had just run a short distance when it got dark for a while, then brightened again, and the rain blinded his eyes again.

When they arrived, the passengers didn't even give a single extra coin. Xiangzi said nothing; he was already too worried about his own life.

The rain stopped for a while, then stopped again, much lighter than before. Xiangzi rushed home in one go. Holding onto the fire, after roasting for a while, he shivered like leaves in the wind and rain. Huniu made him a bowl of ginger syrup, and he foolishly hugged the bowl and drank it all in one go. After drinking, he crawled under the covers, knowing nothing, half-asleep, with the sound of rain rustling in his ears.

By a little after four o'clock, the black cloud began to show fatigue, flickering weakly and faintly. After a while, the clouds in the west split open, black peaks edged with gold, and some white mist swirled beneath the clouds; Shan Du went south, dragging a few not-so-loud thunderclaps. After a while, sunlight appeared through the clouds in the west, turning the rain-soaked leaves into a golden green. In the eastern sky, a pair of seven-colored rainbows hung, with both ends stuck into the dark clouds, and behind the bridge stood a patch of blue sky. The rainbow soon dissipated, and there was no longer a single cloud in the sky. The washed blue sky and everything washed were like a new, cool, and beautiful world just born from the darkness. Even a few colorful dragonflies appeared in the puddles in the courtyard.

But aside from the children chasing the dragonflies barefoot, the people in the courtyard had no time to enjoy the sunny day after the rain. A section of the back eaves wall of Xiao Fuzi's house had collapsed, and the three sisters hurriedly lifted the kang mat to seal the hole. Several sections of the courtyard wall had collapsed, and no one had time to fix it, only focused on tidying up their own rooms: some steps were too low, and water had already flooded the rooms. Everyone was scrambling to scout water outside with dustpans and broken bowls. Some have toppled gables and tried to fill them in. Some roofs leaked like spray bottles, soaking everything and hurrying to carry them out, roasting them by the stove, or placing them on the windowsill to dry. When it was raining, everyone hid in a house that could collapse at any moment and bury them alive, entrusting their lives to heaven; After the rain, they calculated and tidied up those losses; Although after the heavy rain, a jin of grain might cost half a copper coin, their losses could not be compensated by this. They spend money on their houses, but no one ever comes to repair them; Unless it collapses beyond habitation, only one or two brickworkers arrive and loosely pave it with plain mud and broken bricks—preparing for another collapse. If the rent couldn't be paid, the whole family would be kicked out and their belongings confiscated. If the house is broken, it can smash people to death, and no one cares. Their little money could only rent such a place; Damn, danger, everyone deserves it!

The greatest loss was being struck by rainwater. Even children and adults spend all day looking for business on the streets, and summer rains can pour down on them at any moment. They all work hard to earn money, always drenched in sweat, while the northern rains are so sudden and cold, sometimes carrying hailstones the size of walnuts; The cold raindrops hit those open, hair-chilling eyes, at least teaching them to lie on the kang and burn for a day or two. When a child is sick, they have no money to buy medicine; A single rain pushed the old corn and sorghum in the fields upward, but it could also soak many poor children in the city. When adults are ill, it's even more remarkable; After the rain, poets chant about lotus pearls and twin rainbows; In poor families, when the adults fell ill, the whole family went hungry. A single rain brings a few prostitutes or petty thieves, and many end up in prison; When adults are sick, it's better for children to be thieves or prostitutes than to go hungry! Rain falls on the rich and also on the poor; Give to the righteous, and also to the unrighteous. In fact, rain is not fair, because it falls in a world without justice.

Xiangzi fell ill. He was not the only patient in the crowded hospital.

[1]

 Vajra refers to the pupa of the locust worm.

[2]

 'Sahuan' originally referred to animals running wildly, and is also used to describe children's actions.

Nineteen

Xiangzi slept drowsily for two days and nights, and Huniu was startled. At the Niangniang Temple, she prayed for a divine remedy: besides a bit of incense ash, there were two or three herbs. He gave him a drink, and he did open his eyes to look, but after a while, he fell asleep again, mumbling and mumbling something he couldn't even tell. Only then did Huniu remember to go call the doctor. After two needles and taking a dose of medicine, he regained consciousness and immediately asked, "Is it still raining?" ”

The second dose was ready, but he refused to take it. He felt sorry for the money but also hated his own misfortune, being driven sick by a rainstorm and refusing to drink that bowl of bitter juice. To prove he didn't need medicine, he wanted to put on his clothes and get out of the fields right away. But as soon as he sat up, a large stone smeared on his head, his neck went weak, golden sparks appeared before his eyes, and he collapsed again. There was no need to say more. He took the bowl and swallowed the medicine.

He lay down for ten days. The more he lay down, the more anxious he became; sometimes he would crawl onto his pillow, crying silently with tears. He knew he couldn't earn money, so all expenses had to be covered by Huniu; If I pay her all the way forward, I'll rely entirely on his car; With Huniu's love of flowers and food, he couldn't afford to provide for her, especially since she was pregnant! The harder you get up, the more you tend to overthink; the more you think, the more anxious you become, and the harder it is for the illness to recover. Just as he was about to take his life, he asked Huniu, "Where's the car?" ”

"Don't worry, Ding Si rented it out!"

"Ah!" He was worried about his car, afraid that Ding Si or anyone else might damage it. But since he couldn't work in the fields, of course he had to rent it out—how could he just sit idle? He calculated in his mind: if he does it himself, at least carry a bowel every day [1]

 There was always an income of fifty or six cents. Rent for the house, coal, rice, firewood, lamps, oil, tea—not to mention adding clothes, which is just enough for two people, and still needs to be stingy and stingy. She can't be as careless as Huniu. Now, with just over ten yuan a day for car rental, he has to pay forty or fifty yuan, not even counting the medication. What if old age and illness do not heal? Yes, it's not Erqiangzi's fault for drinking, nor for those troubled friends acting recklessly—pulling a rickshaw is a dead end! No matter how hard you work or try to be strong, don't start a family, don't get sick, don't make any mistakes. Hmph! He remembered, his first car, the little money he'd saved—who had he offended this time? Not because of illness, nor for the sake of starting a family, but so heartlessly and unreasonably abandoned! Neither good nor bad is acceptable; on this road, there is only death, and it could arrive at any moment, without any idea at all. Thinking of this, I turned from worry to despair. Whatever, if I couldn't get up, I'd just lie down—that's just how it is! He didn't think about anything else and lay quietly there. Soon he couldn't hold back anymore, wanting to get up immediately, but had to run hard; The road is dead, but the human heart is alive; before entering the coffin, one always keeps hoping. But he couldn't stand up. So bored and begging, he wanted to say a few words to Huniu:

"I said that car was unlucky, really unlucky!"

"Take care of your illness!" Always talking about cars, car enthusiasts! ”

He didn't say anything more. By the way, I'm a car fan! Ever since he started pulling the cart, he believed the car was everything, turns out ...... His illness had just eased a bit, and he went to the ground. Looking in the mirror, he didn't recognize the person in the mirror: his face was scruffy, his temples and cheeks sunken, his eyes were two deep pits, and the scar was covered with many wrinkles! The room was extremely stuffy and stuffy. He didn't dare go into the courtyard—partly because his legs were weak as if he had lost his bones, and partly because he was afraid someone would see him. Not only in this courtyard, but at every train entrance in the east and west cities, everyone knows Xiangzi is on top of his head[2]

 Cool young man. Xiangzi can't be this kind of ill ghost! He refused to leave. Inside the room, she felt stifled again. He wished he could eat something strong at once so he could go out and pull a rickshaw. But illness is destructive; its coming and going are entirely up to itself.

After resting for a month, he didn't care whether he was fully recovered, and just got into the car. He wore his hat very low so people wouldn't recognize him, so he could run slowly. "Xiangzi" and "fast" are inseparable; he can't just swagger and slowly get disrespected.

My body hadn't fully recovered, and I was eager to have a few more lapses to make up for the deficit from my illness. After a few days of diarrhea, the illness returned. This time, dysentery was added. He anxiously slapped his own mouth, but it was useless; his belly seemed to be reaching his waist, and he was still sucking. After finally stopping the dysentery, his legs struggled even to squat down and get up, let alone run for a while.

He rested for another month! He knew that Huniu was probably almost out of money! On August 15th, he decided to set out by carriage. If he got sick again, he swore he would jump into the river!

During his first illness, Xiao Fuzi often came by to check on him. Xiangzi's words never beat Huniu's, and he felt so stifled inside, so sometimes he would say a few words to Xiao Fuzi. This one completely captivated Huniu. When Xiangzi wasn't home, Xiaofuzi was a good friend; Xiangzi was home, and Xiaofuzi was, following Huniu's idea, "Come and hang the stick!" Shameless! She forced Xiao Fuzi to pay back the money she owed, "From now on, you're not allowed to come in again!" ”

Xiaofuzi lost her place to entertain guests, and her own room was so dilapidated—the kang mat blocked the back eave wall. Helpless, she had no choice but to sign up with the "Transfer Company." However, the "Transshipment Company" didn't need goods like her. They introduced "female students" and "well-bred ladies" with high connections and large spending, not an ordinary person like her. She was at a loss. If you want to go to a brothel, since you don't have the capital and can't trade your own business, of course you have to pawn it to the troupe. But doing so would completely lose freedom—who would look after the two brothers? Death is the simplest and easiest thing; living is already in hell. She wasn't afraid of death, but she didn't want to die either, because she wanted to do something braver and greater than dying. She wanted to see her two brothers earn money, so she could rest assured after death. He was doomed to die sooner or later, but he had to die one to save two! After much thought, she had only one path left: sell at a low price. Of course, those willing to enter her little room wouldn't pay a high price. Fine, whoever comes, just give some money. This saves on clothing and cosmetics; Those who come to see her don't dare expect her to dress up with great style; they are just entertaining by the amount of money; She was very young, already a bargain.

Huniu's health was already strained; she was afraid of losing her touch even when shopping outside. Xiangzi had been gone all day, and Xiaofuzi refused to come over. She felt as lonely as a dog tied up in the room. The lonelier she felt, the more hateful she felt. She thought Xiao Fuzi's discounted sale was just to annoy her. She couldn't afford to be so stubborn: sitting in the outer room, opening the door, she waited. When someone walked toward Xiaofuzi's room, she would gossip at the top of her lungs, embarrassing them and making it hard for her to endure. With fewer guests for Xiao Fuzi, she was delighted. Xiao Fuzi knew that if this continued, everyone in the courtyard would gradually respond to Huniu and drive him out. She was just afraid, afraid not to get angry, and at her point, the world knew to put the facts before anger and tears. She brought her little brother over and knelt down to Huniu. She said nothing, but her expression was clear: if kneeling still won't work, she herself isn't afraid of death, and no one else will survive! The greatest sacrifice is endurance; the greatest endurance is preparation for resistance.

Huniu was at a loss. No matter how she thought about it, it felt off, but with such a big belly, she didn't dare to fight. Since Wu couldn't come up with it, she had to give herself a way out: she was just teasing Xiao Fuzi, who wanted to make it real? Xiao Fuzi was too scheming. After explaining it this way, they became friends again, and she continued to support Xiao Fuzi with everything.

Since setting out for the Mid-Autumn Festival, Xiangzi has become more cautious in every way. Two illnesses have taught him that he is not made of iron. He never completely forgot his ambition to earn more money, but repeated setbacks made him realize how weak personal power really is; A true hero will have to grit his teeth when the time comes, but even if he does, he'll cough up blood! Although the dysentery had healed, his stomach still hurt from time to time. Sometimes, when his legs just happened to spread and he wanted to try to speed up his steps, his stomach twisted like a rope, and he slowed down, even suddenly pulling his foot back, lowering his head, shrinking his stomach, and forcing himself to hold back for a while. It was easier to pull the seat alone, but when the cart was pulled, he suddenly stopped, leaving everyone confused and himself very embarrassed. You're only in your twenties and already making such a fool of yourself. When you're in your thirties or forties, what should you do? Thinking about it, he broke out in a cold sweat!

For the sake of his health, he was very willing to go back to charter a car. After all, there is a moment to catch your breath in a job; You have to run fast, but rest is long—it's still easier than pulling apart. He knew very well that Huniu would never let him go. Once you have a family, you lose your freedom, and Huniu is especially formidable. He accepted his bad luck. Over the past six months, from autumn to winter, he had only managed half of it and half struggled, not daring to be careless or lazy, his heart stifling as he ran with his head down. With his head down, he no longer dared to be as carefree as before, no longer caring about anything. As for making money, he still earned a bit more than the average coachman. Unless his stomach was aching, he never let a deal slip away. He had to go when needed, and he never picked up a bad habit. He hadn't learned how to deliberately inflat, reverse midway, or wait for a good seat. This way, he was exhausted, but he still made money every day. He didn't take shortcuts, so there was no danger.

But the money coming in was too little, and it couldn't be left over. Left hand in, right hand out, one clean day a day. He didn't even dare to think about saving money. He knew how to be frugal—Huniu would spend well. Huniu's "postpartum period" was in early February after the New Year. Since the first winter arrived, her arms had already become visible, and she deliberately kept her arms open to show her importance. Looking at her belly, she couldn't even be bothered to get off the kang. He entrusted cooking and cooking to Xiao Fuzi, so naturally, the leftover soup and cured water had to be taught to Xiao Fuzi to share with his brothers. This costs a lot. Besides meals, she also had to eat snacks. The more her stomach was visible, the more she felt she needed to eat good food; You can't boast. Not only did she buy some odds and pieces at any time, but she also instructed Xiangzi to bring her some home every day. Xiangzi earned as much as she spend, and her demands fluctuated with his money. Xiangzi couldn't say anything. When he was sick, he spent her money, so with repayment, of course he had to spend it for her. Xiangzi tightened his grip slightly; she would soon fall ill. "Pregnancy is just a disease that lasts more than nine months. What do you know?" "What she said was also true. By the time the New Year arrived, her ideas grew even more. She couldn't move her own nest, so she sent Xiao Fuzi to go shopping for things several times. She hated that she couldn't get out, but loved herself but refused to go. If she didn't, it would be stifling, so she could only buy more things to look at and feel more comfortable. She kept saying she wasn't buying for herself, but actually felt sorry for Xiangzi: "You've been running hard for a year, and you still haven't eaten a bite yet." Since you fell ill, you haven't been strong enough; If you still don't eat by the end of the year, you'll be starving like a shriveled bug. "Xiangzi neither dares to argue nor will he argue; When she finished cooking, she would eat two or three big bowls at a time. After eating, without exercising, she was so full that she clutched her belly, surely saying she had a fetal awkwardness!

After the New Year, no matter what, she wouldn't allow Xiangzi to go out at night. She didn't know when she would give birth, and she was afraid. Only then did she remember her actual age. Although she was still unwilling to say it, she never told him again, "I'm only a little older than you." She was so noisy that Xiangzi was dazed. The continuation of life is nothing more than having children and raising daughters. Xiangzi couldn't help but feel a bit fond of it. Even though he didn't need a child at all, the simplest and most mysterious character "dad" that would come to him would make even those with a firm heart close their eyes and think about it. No matter how he thought about it, that word always moved his heart. Xiangzi was clumsy, unable to think of any merit or pride in himself; Thinking of this wonderful character, he suddenly felt his own dignity, as if nothing mattered; as long as there was a child, life would never be empty. At the same time, he wanted to provide for and serve Huniu as much as he could; she was no longer "alone"; Even though she disliked it, she deserved 100% of the credit for this matter. However, no matter how much credit she had, her fuss was really unbearable. She would come up with an idea and cause chaos like a ghost, while Xiangzi had to go out to earn money and rest. Even if money could be spent recklessly, he had to settle down and sleep through the night, so he could go back tomorrow. She wouldn't let him go out at night, nor would she let him sleep well. He had no idea at all, feeling dizzy and unsure of what to do. Sometimes joyful, sometimes anxious, sometimes frustrated, sometimes ashamed for joy, sometimes to masturbate in anxiety, sometimes to be delighted. Emotions circled in his heart, making even the simplest person lose his sense of direction. Once, he even pulled the seat over himself, forgetting where the person had been hired!

Around the Lantern Festival, Huniu decided to ask Xiangzi to hire a midwife, but she couldn't take it anymore. The midwife arrived, told her it wasn't time yet, and gave some signs of coming into labor. She endured for two days, then started making a scene again. They invited the midwife again, but it was still not the right time. She cried and cried out that she wanted to die, determined not to endure this torment any longer. Xiangzi had no way out. To show his dedication, he had to comply and temporarily not pull the rickshaw.

The commotion lasted until the end of the month, and even Xiangzi realized that the time had really come—she was no longer human. The midwife came again, giving Xiangzi a hint that she might have a difficult labor. Huniu was older, and this was her first child. She usually lacked exercise, and the fetus was large because she was greedy and greasy during pregnancy; Putting all these factors together, it's impossible to expect smooth production. Moreover, it had never been examined by a doctor, and the fetus had never been corrected; The midwife doesn't have this surgery, but she says: I'm just worried about a sudden birth reversal!

In this courtyard, the birth of a child and the death of a mother have become a habitual topic of conversation. But Huniu is more dangerous than others. Other women stay active until the day of labor, and if they don't eat enough, the fetus won't be very large, so it's easier to have babies. Their danger lies in postpartum disorders, while Huniu is the exact opposite. Her superiority was precisely her source of trouble.

Xiangzi, Xiaofuzi, and the midwife stayed by her side for three days and nights straight. She summoned all the gods and Buddhas, and no matter how many vows she made, it was all in vain. In the end, her voice was hoarse, and she could only softly call out, "Mom!" Oh my! "The midwife had no choice, no one else could do anything. She came up with her own idea: send Xiangzi outside Desheng Gate to invite Second Madam Chen—with a toad immortal on her head. Second Madam Chen wouldn't come unless it cost five yuan, so Huniu took out her last seven or eight yuan: "Good Xiangzi, hurry up and go!" Spending money doesn't matter! When I get better, I'll obediently live with you! Go quickly! ”

Second Madam Chen arrived with her 'child'—a burly man in his forties, with a yellow face—just before the lamp was lit. She was in her fifties, wearing a blue silk jacket, a red pomegranate flower on her head, and a full set of gilded jewelry. He knelt straight before Gao Xiang, not knowing what kind of god he was praying for, but deep down, he wanted to be devout. He lowered his head, resting his hands on the ground, groggy and somewhat sleepy. He hadn't slept properly for two or three days. Suddenly, his neck went weak, startling him. Second Madam Chen and 'Tong'er' had already slipped away quietly.

Foolishness and cruelty are some of the phenomena here; So foolish, so cruel, but for another reason.

Xiangzi only rested for one day before going back to pull the cart as usual. He no longer had the same passion for business as before, nor did he deliberately slack off, just drifting through each day in a simple and untiresome way. After drifting through this for over a month, he felt very calm. His cheeks were a bit fuller, but not as rosy as before; His complexion was yellowish, not showing much strength, nor did he show signs of thinness. Her eyes were clear, but expressionless. She always seemed so energetic, yet also seemed to see nothing. His demeanor was like a tree after a storm, standing quietly in the sunlight, not daring to move at all. He never liked to talk before, and now he was even less willing to speak. The sky is already warm, and the willow branches are covered with tender leaves. But cigarettes were already addictive. As soon as he sat in the car, his large hand reached under the bra pad. Lighting a cigarette, he inhaled and exhaled very slowly, his eyes following the smoke rings upward, staring blankly before nodding, as if he had figured something out.

When pulling the cart, he was even faster than most coachmen, but he stopped running as hard as he could. He was especially careful when turning corners and going up and down hills. Almost excessive caution. Someone wanted to race with him, and no matter how much teasing or provoking him was, he kept his head down without making a sound, continuing to run at a steady pace. He seemed to see through what pulling the cart meant and no longer wanted any glory or praise from it.

At the factory, he made friends; Although they don't talk much, the silent geese also like to fly in flocks. If he doesn't make friends soon, his loneliness will probably be unbearable. As soon as he took out his cigarette case, he would circle around and hand it to everyone. Sometimes, when people see only one stick left in his box and feel too embarrassed to reach out, he simply says, "Buy more!" "When everyone was gambling, he wouldn't hide aside like before. He would come over to take a look, and sometimes place a bet, indifferent to winning or losing, as if just to show everyone he was sociable and to understand that after a few busy days, they should be happy." When they drink, he keeps them company; He didn't drink much, but paid for some food and wine for everyone to enjoy. Things he once looked down on now seemed interesting—since his path was blocked, he couldn't deny that others were right. If a friend had a wedding or funeral, he used to not know how to show favors, but now he would contribute forty copper coins or follow a "public agreement"[2]

 。 Not only did he pay money, but he personally attended memorials or celebrations, because he understood that these things were not just for wasting money, but rather to show some favors that must be fulfilled. Here, people are truly crying or laughing, not making a fuss.

He didn't dare touch those thirty-plus yuan. He made a piece of white cloth and, clumsily, took a large needle to sew money inside, always keeping it close to the flesh. I don't want to spend it, nor do I want to buy a car anymore, just keep it with me as a precaution—who knows what disaster might come in the future! Illness and unexpected disasters can come to you at any time, so you need to be prepared. People aren't made of iron; he understood.

Now he knows how to choose things and only works when he has a suitable monthly contract; Otherwise, pulling the guests away would be fine, unlike before, when they rushed into the house with a fiery heart. Experience teaches you how to be more slick, because you only have one life!

This time, he started working near Yonghe Palace. The host's surname is Xia, over fifty years old, well-educated and well-versed in propriety; He had a wife and twelve children at home. Recently, he married a concubine and didn't dare let his family know, so he deliberately chose a secluded spot to start a new family. In this small family near Yonghe Palace, only Mr. Xia and his newly married concubine remained; In addition, there was a maid and a coachman—that is, Xiangzi. Xiangzi really liked this thing. Unable to go to the teahouse, he parked his car at the "parking lot" west of the city gate, called over a child selling tea carrying a large clay pot and a yellow clay bowl, and drank two bowls of tea that looked like brushed water; It tastes terrible, but he tells himself that from now on, he'll have to drink it all the time and can't keep spending money on good tea and meals. With that decision, I'll eat a bit more—things that aren't good to swallow—as the start of a hardworking new life. He bought ten pan-fried buns, all filled with cabbage stems and a "crust" on the outside[1]

 Also Grudge [2]

 。 No matter how bad it was, he would swallow them. After finishing, he wiped his mouth with the back of his hand. Where to go?

There are only two people he can turn to, someone he can rely on. Planning to work hard and improve himself, he had to find these two—Xiao Fuzi and Mr. Cao. Mr. Cao is a "sage" who will surely forgive him, help him, and offer him good advice. Following Mr. Cao's ideas, and then with Xiao Fuzi's help; He plays outside, she works inside—he will surely succeed, he will surely succeed—there's no doubt about it!

Who knows if Mr. Cao has returned? No worries, tomorrow I'll go to Beichang Street to inquire; If he couldn't find out there, he would go to the Zuo residence to ask. As long as he found Mr. Cao, everything would be easy. Alright, I'll go to relieve myself all night today, and tomorrow I'll go find Mr. Cao; After finding him, she went to see Xiao Fuzi and told her the good news: Xiangzi didn't do well, but he decided to go for the better. Let's work hard together and move forward!

With this plan, his eyes were as sharp as an eagle's, shining as he swept his gaze outward. Whenever he saw a seat, he would run over as if flying, but before he could even settle the price, he took off his big cotton coat. When he started running, his legs were no longer what they had been before, but a wave of heat supported his whole body, and he fought desperately! Xiangzi was, after all, Xiangzi. Even if he ran desperately, no one else would get involved. Whenever he saw one, he would drive another, as if he had gone mad. Sweat poured out with satisfaction. After finishing the run, he felt much lighter, his legs regained that springiness, and he wanted to run again, like a famous horse that hadn't run enough and still kicked its hooves after stopping. He ran until 1 a.m. before collecting the car. Back at the factory, besides the car allowance, he still left over ninety cents.

He slept until dawn; I turned over and opened my eyes again; the sun was already high in the sky. Resting after exhaustion is the sweetest pleasure. I got up and stretched, my joints cracking lightly, my stomach felt completely empty, and I desperately wanted to eat something. After eating a bit, he smiled and told the factory owner, "Take a day off, something to do." "I calculated in my mind: take a day off, get everything done, and start a new life tomorrow." He kept heading toward North Chang Street, hoping to see if Mr. Cao had already returned. As I walked, I prayed in my heart: Mr. Cao, please come back, don't let me come empty-handed! If one thing goes wrong, then nothing goes smoothly! Xiangzi changed—wouldn't heaven be watching over him?

When he arrived outside the Cao residence, his hands trembled as he pressed the bell. Waiting for someone to open the door, his heart was pounding. He didn't care about the familiar door, only hoping that when it opened, he would see a familiar face. He waited, suspecting maybe no one was in the courtyard, otherwise why was it so quiet? It was almost frighteningly quiet. Suddenly, there was a noise inside the door, which startled him instead. The door opened, and the sound was mixed with the most precious, affectionate, and adorable "Yo!" "Aunt Gao!" Xiangzi? That's really rare! Why have you lost weight? "Aunt Gao has gained a bit of weight." Is the gentleman at home? Xiangzi didn't have time to say anything else.

"At home. And you, you only know about the teacher, as if we don't know each other! Not even asking about a good child! If you really succeed, you'll always be a 'timid carpenter—a single saw'! Come in! You're doing pretty well, huh? She asked as she walked inside.

"Hmph! Not good! "Xiangzi smiled.

"Well, sir," Gao Ma called out from outside the study, "Xiangzi is here!" ”

Mr. Cao was inside the house, chasing the sunlight as he moved the narcissus: "Come in!" "Sigh, go in, we'll talk later; I'll go tell my wife; We constantly talk about you! Fools have a foolish connection, but don't even look at it! "Aunt Gao muttered as she walked in.

Xiangzi entered the study: "Sir, I'm here!" "I wanted to ask a question, but didn't say it."

Old Cheng said you were at the factory—yes, Renhe Factory." Gao Ma even went to look for you, but couldn't find you. Sit down! How about you? Is the situation going well? ”

Xiangzi's tears were about to fall. He doesn't talk to others because his words are written in blood, buried deep inside. After calming himself for a long time, he wanted to pour out the simple words that had turned the blood into words. Everything was in memory; once he thought about it, everything came to mind. He had to slowly arrange and organize them. He wanted to tell a living history. Although he didn't know its meaning, the string of grievances was real and clear.

Mr. Cao noticed he was deep in thought, so he sat down quietly, waiting for him to speak.

Xiangzi hung his head in a daze for a long time, then suddenly looked up at Mr. Cao, as if if he couldn't find someone to listen to, so he wouldn't say anything.

"Go ahead!" Mr. Cao nodded.

Xiangzi began to talk about the past, starting with how he traveled from the countryside to the city. He originally didn't want to talk about these useless things, but without them, he felt unsatisfied and the matter was clearly incomplete. His memories were built from sweat and pain; they couldn't be spoken of lightly, nor could he be spoiled when he did. Every drop of sweat, every drop of blood, flows from life, so every event has value worth mentioning.

After coming to the city, he did hard labor, and then switched careers to pull carts. How to save money to buy a car, and how to lose it...... He kept talking about his current situation. Even he found it strange—how could he speak so long and so freely? Things happened one after another, all wanting to leap out of their hearts. It seems you can find the right words for things, one sentence after another, each one real, lovable, and sad. His heart couldn't stop those things from going out, so his words couldn't be stopped. Without a hint of hesitation or confusion, it was as if he wanted to take out his entire heart in one breath. The more he spoke, the more satisfied he became, forgetting himself, because he was already wrapped in those words. Every sentence contained him—the strong-willed, the wronged, the hardworking, the fallen side. After speaking, sweat appeared on his forehead, his heart emptied, feeling comfortable, as empty and comfortable as fainting and breaking out in a cold sweat.

"Now teach me to give you advice?" Mr. Cao asked.

Xiangzi nodded; Having finished speaking, he seemed unwilling to speak again. "Do you still have to pull a rickshaw?"

Xiangzi nodded again. He won't do anything else.

"If that's the case, we still have to pull the cart," Mr. Cao said slowly, "then there are two routes to leave." One is to pool money to buy a car, the other is to rent a car for now, right? If you have no savings, borrowing money to buy a car and earn interest is the same, isn't it? It was better to rent a cart and take it along. Renting a monthly package is better; matters are important, and food and lodging depend on the plate. I think you should come back to me then; I sold my car to Mr. Zuo. If you want to come, you'll have to rent one; Is that okay? ”

"That's great!" Xiangzi stood up. "Sir, do you forget about that?" "What happened?"

"That time, both the gentleman and madam ran off to the Zuo residence!"

"Oh!" Mr. Cao laughed. "Who remembers that! That time, I was a bit too nervous. I stayed in Shanghai with my wife for a few months. Actually, it was perfectly fine. Mr. Zuo had already agreed that that Ruan Ming was now an official and treated me quite well. Well, you probably don't know this; Forget it, I don't remember it at all. And let's talk about ours: what about the little Fuzi you just mentioned? ”

"I have no idea!"

"Let me think about it: if you marry her and rent a room outside, it still doesn't count; Rent, coal lamps, and charcoal are all money—it's not enough. She follows you to work, so how could it be so coincidental? You pull the cart, she's a maid, and it's hard to find! That's not easy to handle! Mr. Cao shook his head. Don't overthink it—is she really reliable? Xiangzi's face flushed, and after choking back tears for a long time, he finally said, "She had no choice but to do that. I dared to put my head down, and she's fine!" She ...... "His heart was in chaos: many different feelings had condensed into a mass, suddenly about to burst apart, and all wanted to run out; He was speechless.
