She eats my food for free, so I won't pay her wages. What do you think? ”

"That's great!" Xiangzi smiled innocently.

"But I can't make all the decisions on this. I need to discuss it with Madam!"

"That's right! If Madam isn't at ease, I'll bring her here and show her what happens! "That's good," Mr. Cao laughed too, not expecting Xiangzi to be so cunning. Here's what we'll do: I'll let my wife know first, and you'll bring her over another day; Madam nodded, and we succeeded! ”

"Well then, sir, shall I go?" Xiangzi was eager to find Xiaofuzi and report this good news that he hadn't even dared to hope for.

Xiangzi left the Cao residence around eleven o'clock, which is the most charming time of the winter day. That day was especially clear and beautiful, with not a single cloud in the blue sky, sunlight streaming down from the dry, cool air, giving a refreshing warmth. The crowing of roosters and barking dogs, along with the shouts of vendors, could be heard far away. Across the street, you could hear some loud, crisp sounds, like cranes crying from the sky. The carriages had their canopies opened, and the copper on them gleamed with a yellow light. Camels walk slowly along the roads, cars and trams speed through the streets, people and horses pass by on the ground, white doves fly in the sky, and the entire old city is filled with both movement and calm—chaotic yet joyful, a chorus of sounds and countless forms of life—all under the clear blue sky, with trees standing quietly everywhere.

Xiangzi's heart wanted to leap out and soar into the sky, circling together with the white doves! Everything was there: matters, wages, little blessings—everything was happily resolved in just a few sentences. No one expected it! Look at this sky, so clear and dry, just like the straightforward and straightforward people of the north. When people encounter good news, even the weather improves; he seems to have never seen such a lovely Dongqing before. To show his happiness more concretely, he bought a frozen persimmon, and with one bite, his mouth was covered in icicles! The chill stabbing at the base of his teeth slowly cooled from his mouth to his chest, making his whole body shiver. After a few bites, his tongue felt numb, but his heart felt comfortable. He strode forward to find Xiao Fuzi. In his heart, he already saw that courtyard, that little hut, and the one he loved; All that was missing was a pair of wings to send him there in one go. As long as he saw her, all his past could be wiped clean, and from then on, a new world could be found. At this moment, his urgency surpassed that of when he went to see Mr. Cao, their relationship was that of friends, master and servant, always exchanging kindness. She is not just a friend; she will entrust her entire life to him, and two people from hell will wipe away their tears and walk hand in hand with smiles. Mr. Cao's words could move him, and Xiaofuzi could move him without speaking. He told Mr. Cao the truth, and he was about to say more caring things to Xiaofuzi, saying things that no one else could say to her. Right now, she was his life; without her, nothing was meaningful. He couldn't just work hard for his own food and drink; he had to rescue her from that little room and then live with him in a clean, warm room, like a pair of little birds—happy, dignified, and affectionate! She could ignore Erqiangzi or her two younger brothers; she had to come help Xiangzi. Er Qiangzi could have earned his own livelihood, and his two younger brothers could have gone to pull a rickshaw or do other things; Xiangzi couldn't do without her. His body, mind, and everything needed her. She needed a man like him exactly as well.

The more I think of him, the more anxious and happy he becomes; There are so many women in the world, but none as good and suitable as Xiao Fuzi! He has married, stolen; Having encountered both the beautiful and the ugly, the old and the young; But none of them could be on his heart; they were just women, not partners. Indeed, she was not the innocent girl he had in his heart, but precisely because of that, she was more pitiful and able to help him more. That foolish country girl may be very innocent, but she would never have the skills or inner path of Xiao Fuzi. And what about himself? There are plenty of dark spots in my heart too! So, he and she were a perfect pair—neither tall nor low, like a pair of jars with cracks that could hold water, placed right in one place.

No matter how you look at it, this is the most fitting thing. After thinking about all this, he began to think of something practical: first, pay Mr. Cao a month's salary, buy her a cotton robe, tidy up her shoes, and then take her to see Mrs. Cao. Wearing a new, plain long cotton robe, her head and feet were spotless. With her looks, age, and presence, she was sure to be impressive and would win Madam Cao's favor. That's right!

When he got there, he was drenched in sweat. Seeing that broken front door feels like seeing my old home I haven't returned to for years: broken door, broken wall, a few dry yellow grasses on the gatehouse—all very charming. He entered the main gate and headed straight for Xiao Fuzi's room. Without knocking or calling out, he yanked it open. As soon as he opened the door, he instinctively stepped back. On the kang sat a middle-aged woman. Since there was no fire in the room, she wrapped herself in a very tattered quilt. Xiangzi stood stunned outside the door, and someone from inside called out, "What's wrong!" Reporting the loss? Why didn't you say a word and just stunned into the house?! Who are you looking for? "Xiangzi didn't want to speak. Sweat suddenly dripped down his body. Holding onto the broken door, he didn't dare to give up all hope: "I'm looking for Xiao Fuzi!" "I don't know!" When you look for someone tomorrow, ask first before you open the door! What little blessings, big blessings! ”

Sitting at the front door, he stood there stunned for a long time, his heart empty, forgetting what he was doing. Gradually, he remembered something—this was only the size of Xiaofuzi. Xiaofuzi kept walking in and over in his mind, like a paper figure on a revolving lantern, always going back and forth, useless. He seemed to have forgotten about their relationship. Gradually, little Fuzi's figure shrank a little, and his heart became more active. Only then did I realize how sad it was.

When it's hard to know the outcome of events, people always look for the better. Xiangzi guessed that perhaps Xiaofuzi had moved and nothing major had changed. If you're feeling bad, why don't you come to see her often? Ashamed makes people move, hoping to make up for their own mistakes. It's best to ask around first. He went into the compound again and found an old neighbor to ask around. No proper news was received. Not daring to be disappointed, he didn't even bother to eat, wanting to find Er Qiangzi; It's fine to find those two younger brothers. These three men are always on the street, so it's not hard to find.

He asked people he saw—at the train entrance, in the teahouse, in the courtyard, he used all his strength to walk all day, asking for answers all day, but there was no news.

That night, he returned to the car factory, exhausted, but he still refused to forget the incident. After a day of disappointment, he dared not hope for anything more. The suffering die easily, and the suffering are easily forgotten when they die. Could it be that Xiao Fuzi is already gone? Taking a step back, even if she hadn't died, Er Qiangzi might have sold her far away, far away; This is worse than death!

Tobacco and alcohol became his friends again. How can you think without smoking? How can one stop pondering without getting drunk?

Twenty-three

Xiangzi wandered the street, fearing for his life, and met Xiao Ma'er's grandfather. The old man no longer pulled the cart; his clothes were thinner and tattered than before. He carried a willow wooden stick, with a large clay pot hanging in front and a tattered ingots basket hanging behind, containing some baked flatbreads, oil ghosts, and a large brick. He also knew Xiangzi.

As they spoke, Xiangzi learned that Xiao Ma'er had been dead for more than half a year. The old man had sold that old cart and now made a pot of tea and some baked cakes to sell at the carriage door every day. The old man was still as kind and adorable as ever, but his waist had bent a lot, his eyes brimming with tears in the wind, and his eyelids were red as if he had just finished crying.

Xiangzi drank a bowl of tea from him and shared a few words of grievances in his heart. "You want to live alone?" The old man judged Xiangzi's words: "Who doesn't think so? But who managed to get by? Back then, I was healthy and cunning, and I've survived until now. I've ended up like this! Good health? Even the unforgettable, you can't escape our trap. Good-minded? What's the use! Good begets good, evil begets evil retribution—there are no such eight things! When I was young, I was truly warm-hearted, treating others' matters as my own. Is it useful? No! I've even saved lives—people who jumped into rivers, hanged themselves. Have I ever gotten what I got? No! Let me tell you, I might freeze to death one day. I understand now—trying hard work to survive alone is harder than climbing to the sky. What can a person possibly do?

 ？ Have you seen grasshoppers? Even a child can jump quite far by themselves, but if a child catches it and ties it with a string, it can't even fly off. They gather into herds, fight in formation, and in no time, they consume an entire acre of crops. No one can control them! Don't you agree? I'm pretty cunning, I can't even protect a little grandson. He's sick, I don't have money to buy him good medicine, and I watch him die in my arms! Forget it, don't say anything else! —Chalai! Who wants a hot bowl? ”

Xiangzi truly understood: Liu Si, Mrs. Yang, Detective Sun—they can't get their retribution just because of their curses; He himself couldn't gain benefits just because he was strong. Relying solely on oneself, just as the elders say, is like a grasshopper tied by a child with a string—so what if it has wings?

He had no intention of going to the Cao residence at all. As soon as he entered the Cao residence, he had to be strong—but what good was being strong? So they just carelessly hang out: if there's no food, just pull the car out; If it's enough for a day, just rest for one day, and we'll say tomorrow about it. This is not just a solution, but the only way. Saving money, buying cars—all prepared for others to snatch them—why bother? Why not enjoy it while you can?

Besides, if he found Xiao Fuzi, he should still try hard—not for himself, but for her. Since she couldn't be found, it was just like this old man whose grandson had died—who was he doing it for? He also told the old man about Xiaofuzi's situation, treating him as a true friend.

"Who wants a hot bowl?" The old man first called out, then thought for Xiangzi: "From what I guess, there are two ways out: either Er Qiangzi is sold to someone as a concubine, or he is locked in a white house." Hmph, most likely they've gone into the White House! How should I put it? Since Xiao Fuzi is, as you just told me, once married, it's hard for anyone to want him again; When buying concubines, they need to buy whole goods. So, there's an eighty percent chance she's bought the White House. I'm almost sixty and have seen plenty of things: if a strong young man pulling a rickshaw doesn't come up to the street corner for a day or two, go find him. If he's not a monthly hauler, he'll probably be crawling in a white house; If the girls and wives of our rickshaw pullers suddenly disappeared, there was a good chance they would have gone there too. We sell sweat, our women sell meat—I understand, I know! Go look for her. If you're not hoping she's really there, just ,—— tea!" Who wants a hot bowl?! Xiangzi rushed to the outside of Xizhimen.

As soon as I left the pass, I immediately felt emptiness; the trees stood thin by the roadside, and not a single bird was on the branches. Gray trees, gray land, gray houses—all stand quietly under the gray-yellow sky; Looking out from this patch of gray, one sees the desolate and cold Western Hills. North of the railway, a forest stretches out, and outside the woods are a few low houses. Xiangzi calculated—this must be the White House. Looking at the woods, there was no movement at all; Looking further north, you can see some watery areas outside the Wansheng Garden, where only a few withered cattails and ruined reeds remain on uneven heights. There was no one outside the small house, no movement. With so quiet near and far, he wondered if this was the famous White House. He boldly walked toward the house, where grass curtains hung on the doors, newly hung and glossy. He had heard that the women here would sit outside bareback, greeting passersby in summer. Those who come to take care of them still have to sing kiln tunes from afar[2]

 , showing that he was not an outsider. Why is it so quiet now? Is this place no longer for business in winter?

While he was wondering this, the straw curtain in the room next to it moved slightly, revealing a woman's head. Xiangzi was startled. That head, upon closer look, looked very much like Huniu's. He thought to himself, "I came looking for Xiao Fuzi. If I find Huniu, that would truly be a ghost!" ”

"Come in, silly girl!" That head spoke, but his voice didn't sound like Huniu's; His voice was hoarse, much like the old man selling wild herbs he often heard on the overpass—hoarse yet noticeably urgent.

There was nothing in the room, only the woman and a small kang bed. There was no mat on the kang, but the fire was burning inside, and the stench was extremely foul. On the kang bed was an old quilt, its edges shiny just like the bricks on the kang. The woman was in her forties, with dishap, and hadn't washed her face yet. She wore paired shorts and a small cotton jacket made of blue cloth, with no buttons fastened. Xiangzi lowered his head before stepping inside, but as soon as he entered, she hugged him. The little cotton jacket wasn't buttoned up, and her chest showed a pair of long, large breasts.

Xiangzi sat on the edge of the kang, unable to straighten his neck because he was standing. He really liked meeting her, and often heard people say that the White House has a "white pocket," and that must be her. The nickname "White Flour Pocket" comes from her two large breasts. Xiangzi got straight to the point, asking if she had seen a little Fuzi, but she didn't know. Xiangzi described Xiao Fuzi's appearance, and she remembered: "Yes, there is such a person!" She's not very old, showing a few white teeth. Yes, we all call her 'Little Tender Meat.' ”

"Which room is she in?" Xiangzi's eyes suddenly widened with murderous intent. "Her?" It's already over! "The white flour pocket" pointed outward, "Hanged in the woods!" ”

"What?"

"Since Little Tender Meat arrived here, she's been very popular. She was a bit overwhelmed; her body was quite frail. One day, when I lit the lamp, I still remembered it really because I was sitting at the door with two or three women. Sigh, it was at such a time that someone came by and headed straight to her room; She didn't like sitting at the door with us. When she first arrived, she was beaten for it. Later, after she became famous, everyone let her stay alone in the room. Fortunately, when she came to visit her, she never went looking for anyone. After about the time it takes to finish a meal, the guest left and headed straight for that wood. We didn't notice anything, and no one went inside to see her. When Old Forkrod arrived to collect the bill with her, she finally saw a man lying naked inside, sleeping soundly. He was actually drunk. The tender meat stripped off the guest's clothes, put them on himself, and escaped. She really is clever. If it weren't for the darkness, she wouldn't have escaped even if it cost her life. When night fell, she disguised herself as a man again, leaving everyone stunned. Immediately, Old Fork sent people to look around. Hmph, as soon as she entered the woods, she was hanging there. After taking it off, she had already died, but her tongue barely slipped out, her face was not unpleasant, and even in death, she was still likable! It's been so many months, and nothing has happened in the woods until nightfall. She hasn't come out to scare people—how benevolent she is! ......" Before she could finish, Xiangzi staggered out. They arrived at a cemetery, where pine trees were planted in a square area, with more than a dozen graves among them. The sunlight was already very weak, and the pine forest was even dimmer. He sat on the ground, where there was some hay and pine flowers. There was no sound, only a few mountain magpies in the trees making long, mournful cries. This could never be Xiao Fuzi's grave. He knew it, but his tears kept falling in streams. There was nothing left—even little Fuzi was buried! He was strong-willed, and Xiao Fuzi was strong-willed. All he had left were useless tears—she had already become a hanging ghost! The leader is buried in a chaotic death post—this is the fate of a lifetime of hard work!

Back at the car factory, he slept for two days in frustration. He absolutely did not want to go to the Cao residence, and there was no need to send a message. Mr. Cao couldn't save Xiangzi's life. After sleeping for two days, he pulled the cart out, his mind completely blank. He no longer thought or hoped for anything, only suffering because his stomach was coming out. When his stomach was full, he went to sleep. What was there to think about, what was there to hope for? Watching a skinny, skinny dog waiting by the sweet potato handle, eating some skin and whiskers, he realized he was just like this dog, spending his whole day just to gather some sweet potato skins and whiskers. Making do with living is everything; there's nothing left to think about.

People have raised themselves from beasts, yet now they have driven their own kind into beasts. Xiangzi was still in that city of culture, but had become a beast. It wasn't his fault at all. He stopped thinking, so even if he killed someone, he bore no responsibility. He had no hope left, just drifting down in a daze, sinking into that bottomless pit. He eats, he drinks, he visits prostitutes, he gambles, he is lazy, he is cunning, because he has lost his heart, and his heart has been taken away by others. All he had left was that tall meat frame, waiting to rot, ready to go to the Death Pit.

Winter passed, and the spring sunlight was the natural gift of clothes to everyone. He sold all the cotton coats rolled up. He wants to eat good food and drink good food, without storing winter clothes, and never preparing to see winter again; Let's enjoy ourselves for a day today, or die tomorrow! Who cares if it's winter or not! Unfortunately, if winter arrives and you are still alive, then let's talk about it later. Originally, whenever he thought about it, he would think of things that would last a lifetime; Now, he only cared about the present. Experience taught him that tomorrow is just a continuation of today, and tomorrow inherits today's grievances. After selling the cotton clothes, he felt very satisfied. Why use cash on hand? Why wait for that winter that would choke with a gust of wind?

Gradually, he wanted to sell not only clothes but everything else, selling anything he didn't need for the time being. He likes to see his things turn into money and be spent by himself; If you spend it yourself, it won't end up in someone else's hands—that's the safest option. Sell things and buy them when they actually need them; If you can't afford it, then simply don't use it. No need to wash your face or brush your teeth—turns out it doesn't really matter—not only does it save money, but it's also easy to deal with. Who is it for showing dignity? Wearing tattered clothes, eating pancakes and braised pork in their stomachs—this is true! If there's something good in your belly, even if you die, there's still some fat left, so you don't look like a starving rat. Xiangzi, what a dignified Xiangzi, had become a thin, dirty, low-class rickshaw puller. He never washed his face, body, or clothes, and sometimes went over a month without shaving his hair.

His car isn't particular anymore—whether new or old, as long as the car is small, that's enough. If he pulled up a deal and made a little profit, he'd sell it halfway. If the passenger refused, he'd glare and start fighting, and staying at the police station for two days would be no big deal! Pulling the cart alone, he walked slowly, feeling sorry for his sweat. When he got into the carriage and was in a good mood, he would even run off in one breath, just to leave others behind. At times like this, he's also very good at breaking through—cutting across other cars, deliberately taking hard turns, stubbing the cars behind, or sneaking a punch to the car in front—he knows all of it. At first, he thought pulling the cart meant carrying a life, and that one careless move could kill someone. Now, he deliberately wants to be bad; It doesn't matter who dies from the fall; everyone deserves to die! He recovered, his silence and silence. Not a word is spoken; he eats, he drinks, he takes out the spoils.

Language is the way humans exchange opinions and convey feelings. Without opinion or hope, what is the point of speaking? Besides bargaining, he keeps his mouth shut all day; The mouth seems to be specially prepared for eating, drinking tea, and smoking. Even when drunk, he wouldn't make a sound; he'd sit in a secluded spot and cry. Almost every time he got drunk, he would go to the woods where little Fuzi hanged himself to weep; After crying, he settled into the White House. Sobering up, Qian washed his hands and fell ill. He does not regret it; If he had moments of regret, he regretted why he had been so assertive, so cautious, and so honest back then. All the regrets are now in the past; now there is nothing left to regret.

Now, how could he take advantage of something? That's what he did. Smoking an extra cigarette, spending fake coins on things, drinking douzhi and eating a few pieces of pickled vegetables, pulling a cart with less effort to earn one or two extra coins—all these made him feel satisfied. He took advantage, and others suffered losses. Yes, this is a form of revenge! Gradually, he expanded this further, learning to borrow money from friends, but even after borrowing, he still didn't want to repay it; If pushed too hard, he could act shamelessly. When Chu Yi arrived, no one suspected him at all. Everyone knew he was a person of dignity and trustworthiness, so as soon as he spoke, he lent him money. He used this remnant of his personality to borrow everywhere, borrowing from Rubai and then spending it casually. When someone asks for a debt, he puts on a pitiful face to beg for leniency; If that didn't work, he would borrow another twenty cents, pay off fifteen fen of the debt, and drink the remaining five cents first. After a while, he couldn't even lend out a single coin, so he started scamming people out of money. He visited every residence he had been to before, whether master or servant, and whenever they met, he would make up a lie to swindle a few coins; With no money, he begged for some torn clothes. As soon as the clothes were in hand, they turned into money, and money quickly turned into cigarettes and alcohol. He lowered his head and pondered, coming up with a bad idea. If he came up with one idea, he could earn more than a day's truck pull; It saves effort and pays in, which he finds very worthwhile. He even went to find Gao Ma from the Cao residence. From a distance, he waited for Aunt Gao to come out to buy things. When he saw her come out, he almost rushed over in a single step and charmingly called her Sister-in-law Gao. "Oh! You scared me to death! Who do I think it is? Xiangzi! How are you like this? "Aunt Gao's eyes widened as if she saw a monster.

"Don't even mention it!" Xiangzi lowered his head.

"Didn't you already tell the master? Why did you leave and never look back? I was even asking Old Cheng about you, and he said he didn't see you. Where exactly are you going? Both the husband and wife are worried! ”

"I've been sick for a long time, almost died!" Tell the teacher to help me out, and I'll come back to work once I'm ready!" Xiangzi spoke the words he had already made—simple, moving.

"The gentleman isn't home. Would you come in and see Madam?"

"Forget it! This is what I am! Go ahead and tell me! ”

Aunt Gao took out two yuan for him: "Madam gave it to you, and told you to take some medicine quickly!" ”

"That's right! Thank you, ma'am! Xiangzi took the money, thinking about where to develop it. As soon as Aunt Gao turned her face, he ran to the overpass and played all day.

Slowly, he had cleared all the doors of the residence, and this time it was no longer very effective. He realized this path couldn't rely on length; he had to come up with another idea, a way to make money more than pulling a rickshaw. Previously, his only hope was pulling the cart; Now, he hates pulling carts. Naturally, he couldn't completely sever ties with the car for the moment, but as long as he had a way to get through meals, he refused to touch the handlebars. His body was lazy, but his ears were sharp; whenever he heard news, he would run ahead. What about the civic group, what about the petition group? Whatever someone paid for, he did it all. Whether it's Sanmao or Ermao, he was happy to waving flags for a whole day and wandering with the crowd. He felt that no matter what, this was better than pulling a rickshaw. It didn't make much money, but he didn't need to work hard. Holding a small flag, he hung his head, cigarette dangling from his mouth, and followed everyone with a half-smile, not making a sound. When he had to shout a few times, he would open his mouth wide and remain completely silent, cherishing his voice. He didn't want to put effort into anything, because he had worked hard before but never gained anything. During this moment of flag raising and shouting, if he encountered any danger, he would be the first to run away, and he would run very fast. His life could be destroyed by his own hands, never sacrificing anything for anyone again. Those who strive for the individual also know how to destroy the individual; these are the two extremes of individualism.

[1]

 'Beng'er' means skill or future.

[2]

 Kiln tune, a ballad popular in brothels.

Learning Resource Sharing 24

It's the time to offer incense to the top again, and the weather is getting scorching.

It seemed as if all the paper fan sellers had suddenly appeared from somewhere, carrying boxes, with the ringing of bells on the boxes catching everyone's attention. By the roadside, green apricots are already being sold in piles, cherries are bright red, swarms of golden bees fall on rose and date pots, glass powder glistens in large porcelain basins, and the picks for cakes and liangfen are neatly prepared, with all kinds of colored seasonings arranged. People also change into light, flashy thin clothes, and the streets suddenly gain many colors, like countless rainbows scattered across the world. The scavengers worked intensely, constantly splashing clean water onto the road, but the dust still swirled up, making people irritable. Amid the light dust, there are long willow branches and lively, nimble swallows, making one feel a sense of refreshment. A kind of weather that makes people unsure what good weather is; everyone yawns lazily, feeling both tired and exhilarating.

Yangge, lion dance, road breakers, Five Tiger Staffs, and various other gatherings all gradually headed up the mountain. Beating gongs and drums, carrying baskets, waving apricot-yellow flags, one after another, stirring extraordinary excitement throughout the city, giving people faint yet intimate feelings, leaving some sounds and dust in the air. Both those attending and watching felt a sense of enthusiasm, devotion, and excitement. The bustle of chaotic times comes from superstition; fools' comfort is only self-deception. These colors, these sounds, the sky full of clear clouds, the dust on the streets, give people spirit and activity: those who go up the mountain go up, those who visit temples visit temples, those who admire flowers look at the flowers...... At worst, you can watch the lively scene by the street and chant a couple of Buddhist prayers.

When the weather is so hot, it seems to awaken the spring dreams of the ancient capital, making it possible to travel everywhere. Everyone thinks of something, and the warmth drives the flowers, grass, fruits, trees, and human pleasures to grow together. The green willows and fresh cattails in the North and South Seas attract young men playing harmonica. Men and women set their boats under the shade of willows, or drift among tender lotus leaves, singing love songs and kissing their eyes. Peonies and peonies in the park invite poets and refined scholars to stroll leisurely, waving precious paper fans; When tired from walking, he would sit by the red walls and under the green pines, sipping a few cups of tea that could dispel idle sorrow, sneaking glances at the noble ladies and famous beauties from north and south. Even that usually calm place is brought to visitors by gentle breezes and sunny days, just like butterflies are sent here. Peonies at Chongxiao Temple, green reeds at Taoran Pavilion, mulberry groves and rice fields at the Natural Museum all attract the voices and shadows of umbrellas; Even the Temple of Heaven, the Confucius Temple, and Yonghe Palace were somewhat lively despite their solemn atmosphere. He traveled long distances with his students, to the Western Hills, to the hot springs, to the Summer Palace, to travel, to run around, to gather, to scribble on the rocks. Even the poor have places to go: Huguo Temple, Longfu Temple, Baita Temple, Earth God Temple, and Hua'er Market are all livelier than ever: all kinds of grasses and flowers are brightly displayed by the roadside, and with just a couple of copper coins, you can bring 'beauty' right into your home. When the bean juice was spread, the pickled vegetables looked as fresh as a giant flower, with a charred chili on the tip. Eggs are cheap, fried egg cubes are golden and tender, making your mouth water. The overpass was even more intense. The tea pavilions built on the new banquet were lined up one after another, with pristine white tablecloths and enchanting singing girls, all facing the old pine trees atop the Temple of Heaven's wall. The sound of drums and gongs lasted for seven or eight hours, and the crisp weather made them especially crisp, disturbing everyone's hearts. Prostitutes could dress up easily; a floral wool jacket could be displayed beautifully, clearly showing off their curves. The once peaceful people now have places to go: in front of Jishuitan, outside Wanshou Temple, at Yaokeng in the eastern suburbs, and at Baishi Bridge in the western suburbs, fishing is possible, and small fish often touch tender reeds, stirring gently. After fishing, pig head meat from the wild teahouse, boiled tofu, white dry wine, and salted beans can also make people drunk and full; Then, carrying fishing rods and small fish, we walked along the willow bank, stepping into the sunset, and calmly entered the ancient city gate.

Everywhere is fun, lively everywhere, and vibrant everywhere. A sudden wave of summer heat was like a divine talisman, making the old city permeate with magical power. It doesn't care about death, disaster, or hardship; when the time comes, it unleashes its power, hypnotizing millions of hearts and singing its hymns as if in a dream. It is impure, beautiful, old, lively, chaotic, leisurely, lovely, and the great Beiping of early summer.

It is precisely at this time of year that people look forward to news that is enough to relieve boredom, news that can be read two or three times without getting bored, news that can be read and read in person. The sky is so long and clear!

Here comes the news! As soon as the tram left the factory, the newspaper vendor shouted at the top of his lungs, chasing after people everywhere: "News about Ruan Ming being executed, news about the nine o'clock parade!" "One copper coin, one copper coin, one copper coin, all caught by the little black hand. On the tram, in the shops, in the hands of passersby, every page was about Ruan Ming: photos of Ruan Ming, his history, his travel notes, large and small print, illustrated descriptions—the entire page was about Ruan Ming. On the tram, in the eyes of passersby, and in the words of those who spoke with Ruan Ming, it seemed there was no one else left in the old city—only Ruan Ming; Ruan Ming paraded through the streets today, and today he was executed by firing squad! Valuable news, ideal news—not only did they talk about Ruan Ming, but soon you could even see him. Women rush to dress up; The elderly leave early, afraid that if their feet are slow, they might fall behind; Even the kids in school want to skip half the day to learn and see for themselves. By 8:30, the streets were already packed—excited, hopeful, crowded, and noisy, waiting to see the news of the event. The coachmen forgot to arrange their business, the shops were in chaos, and the vendors were too lazy to shout, all eagerly awaiting the prison cart and Ruan Ming. In history, there were Huang Chao and Zhang Xianzhong, the people of the Taiping Heavenly Kingdom who were killed and loved watching killings. Execution seemed too simple; they loved hearing 'lingchi', beheading, skinning, and live burial, which sounded like eating ice cream—so thrilling it made them shiver slightly. But this time, besides the execution, they paraded through the streets, almost to thank the person who came up with such an idea, so they would see a half-dead man tied up in a cart, delighting their eyes; Even if he wasn't the execution inspector, it was pretty much enough. These people have no sense of good or evil in their hearts, do not understand good from evil, cannot distinguish right from wrong, and cling tightly to certain rites and morals, wishing to be called civilized people; They love watching their own kind cut into pieces, as cruel and thrilling as a child slaughtering a puppy. Once power was in hand, any of them would go massacre the city, cutting off women's breasts and feet and piling them into small hills—this was their swift move. If they haven't gained this authority, they might as well watch more pig slaughter and sheep slaughter and killing people to satisfy themselves. If they can't even touch this one, they'll curse the child with 'a thousand cuts to kill' or 'ten thousand cuts' to vent their anger.

The clear blue sky echoed, a tall red sun rose high in the east, a few gentle east winds stirred, and the willow branches by the roadside swayed gently. On the east side road, there was a large shadowy area packed with people: young and old, men and women, ugly, handsome, fat and thin. Some dressed beautifully, some wore only short jackets, all chatting and laughing, looking forward and constantly peeking south or north. As soon as one person poked their head out, everyone followed, their hearts racing faster. As the crowd moved closer and closer, the crowd gradually crowded to the roadside, forming a wall of flesh, with only heads moving chaotically at varying heights. Patrol officers came out in formations to maintain order, blocking and shouting, sometimes even grabbing a child as hard as mud and throwing a couple of punches, causing everyone to burst into laughter. Waiting, patiently waiting, my legs ache, yet I still won't leave empty-handed; Those in front refused to leave, while the newcomers behind crowded forward, leading to arguments. They didn't move, relying solely on words, cursing each other and shouting 'Good.' The child gets annoyed and gets slapped by the adult; Pickpockets cursed loudly when they got what they had stolen. The noise and clamor turned into a commotion, and no one was willing to move. The more people gathered, the less willing they were, all expressing their love for seeing the half-dead prisoner.

Suddenly, everyone fell silent, and from afar, a squad of armed police officers arrived. "Here they come!" Someone shouted. Immediately after, the noise erupted, and the whole crowd moved forward like machines, inch by inch, then inch after inch—here they come! Here it comes! Their eyes lit up, they muttered something, a chorus of voices, the streets stenched with sweat, and the people of this land of morality passionately loved watching people kill.

Ruan Ming was a short boy, hands tied behind his back, sitting in the car like a sick little monkey; With his head down, a white sword over two feet long was stuck in his back. The voices are like waves pushing the waves forward, and everyone curls their lips in criticism, a bit disappointed: 'This is just the little monkey!' Is it really that boring! With her head down, her face pale, she just didn't make a sound! Some people came up with an idea and wanted to tease him: "Bro, give him a good call!" Immediately, shouts echoed from all directions, "Great!" "Like cheering for Kunling on stage—contemptuous, malicious, annoying, shouting." Ruan Ming still didn't say a word, not even lifting his head. Some people were really anxious, looking down on such a soft prisoner, squeezing to the roadside and spitting at him a few times. Ruan Ming still didn't move, showing no sign of it. The more people look, the more bored they become, and the more reluctant they are to leave; What if he suddenly says, "In twenty years, he'll be a hero again"? What if he asked the hotel for two pots of baigan and a plate of braised pork? No one was willing to move, let's see what he was doing. The carriage passed, but still had to follow. He hadn't shown much now, so who would have thought he would come to the single archway without catching his breath and singing a few lines of "Silang Visits His Mother"? Follow along! Some followed all the way to the overpass; Although he never did anything admirable or satisfying, people watched him take a bullet, and it was clear that this trip was worthwhile.

During such a lively season, Xiangzi walked slowly with his head down at the foot of Desheng Gate. He walked to Jishuitan and looked around. With no one around, he slowly and tiptoed toward the lakeside. He walked to the lakeside, found an old tree, leaned against its trunk, and stood for a while. Hearing that there was no sound outside, he sat down quietly. The reed leaves moved slightly, or a small bird suddenly chirped, causing him to stand up in a hurry and sweat on his forehead. He listened, he looked, but there was no movement around, so he slowly sat down again. After several times, he grew used to the slight movement of the reed leaves and the birdsong, deciding not to panic anymore. Staring blankly at the ditch outside the lake, some small fish with eyes shining like tiny pearls, gathering and dispersing, coming and going; Sometimes a patch of tender duckweed topped his head, sometimes foam came from his mouth. By the edge of the ditch, some tadpoles with legs stretched out straight, shaking their large, black heads. The water suddenly sped up, washing away the small fish and tadpoles. Their tails swayed downstream, but with the water came another swarm, struggling to stop. A water scorpion dashed over at incredible speed. The water gradually stabilized, and the fish formed a group again, opening their mouths to nibble on a floating green leaf or a piece of grass. The slightly larger fish hide in the depths, occasionally poking their backs and quickly turning down, leaving a whirlpool and some ripples on the water's surface. The kingfisher swept across the water like an arrow, and the small and big fish disappeared, leaving only duckweed on the water. Xiangzi stared blankly at all this, seeming to see yet not see. Without thinking, he picked up a small stone, tossed it into the water, splashed some water, and scattered many duckweeds. He was startled and nearly stood up again.

After sitting for a long time, he secretly touched his waist with his large black hand. Nodding, his hand stopped there; After a while, he took out a stack of cash, counted them, and carefully hid them back where they were.

His mind was entirely focused on that little money: how to spend it, how not to teach others, how to enjoy it while safe. He no longer thinks for himself; he has become an appendage to money, subject to its control.

The origin of this amount of money has already determined its destination. Such money cannot be spent openly. This little money, even those holding it, dared not see the light of day. People watched Ruan Ming on the street, while Xiangzi hid at the quiet base of the city, trying to move to a quieter, darker place. He didn't dare walk the market anymore because he had sold Ruan Ming. Even alone, facing the quiet flowing water, with no one behind him, he didn't dare to look up, as if a ghostly shadow was always following him. Ruan Ming, who fell in bloodstains on the overpass, lived in Xiangzi's heart, lived in some cash at his waist. He didn't regret it, only fear—afraid of the ghost who followed him everywhere.

After Ruan Ming became an official, he enjoyed some things he had previously thought should be overthrown. Money leads people into a harsh society, abandons noble ideals, and willingly leads them into hell. He wore luxurious clothes, went to prostitutes, gambled, and even smoked opium. When his conscience was revealed, he thought it was a sinister social trap against him, not entirely his fault; He admitted his actions were wrong, but the temptation to blame society was too strong, and he couldn't resist. Over time, his money ran out, and he recalled those intense ideas, but he did not rally himself to carry them out; He wanted to use his thoughts to exchange for some money. Turning thoughts into money is like trying to get passing grades by associating with teachers while studying. The mindset of the lazy cannot coexist with character; everything that can be exchanged for money will sooner or later be sold. He received an allowance. Agencies eager to promote the revolution cannot be extremely cautious in selecting soldiers; those willing to join are comrades. However, those receiving allowances must have some achievements, no matter what means are used; The government office wants reports. Ruan Ming couldn't just take money and do nothing. He participated in organizing the work of foreign coachmen. As for Xiangzi, he was already a seasoned expert who waved flags and shouted loudly; Thus, Ruan Ming met Xiangzi.

Ruan Ming sold his thoughts for money; Xiangzi was about money, accepting ideas. Ruan Ming knew that when necessary, he could sacrifice Xiangzi. Xiangzi had never planned for this, but when the time came, he did—betraying Ruan Ming. Working for money means fearing encountering more money; Loyalty is not based on money. Ruan Minh believed in his own thoughts and forgave all his bad behavior with intense thought. Xiangzi found Ruan Ming's words very reasonable, but seeing how he enjoyed himself, he was also quite envious—"If I had more money, I would be happy for a few days too!" Just like the one named Ruan! "Money diminished Ruan Ming's character, and it dazzled Xiangzi's eyes. He sold Ruan Ming for sixty yuan. Ruan Ming wanted the power of the masses, while Xiangzi wanted more—like Ruan Ming—enjoyment. Ruan Ming's blood spilled onto the allowance, and Xiangzi stuffed the cash into his waist.

He sat until the sun was slightly westward, and the reeds and willows on the lake shimmered with golden-red light. Only then did Xiangzi stand up and walk west along the city base. He was used to scamming money; Selling human lives—this is the first time they've done it. Besides, what Ruan Ming said made a lot of sense! The emptiness of the city base and the towering walls made him increasingly fearful as he walked. Occasionally, when he saw a few crows on the trash heap, he wanted to walk away from them, afraid they would startle them and give him a few ominous calls. When he reached Xichenggen, he quickened his pace. Like a dog that had just stolen food, he slipped out of Xizhimen. Having someone to accompany him at night, to numb him and to make him fearless—that is the ideal destination; The White House is such an ideal place.

By autumn, Xiangzi's illness no longer allowed him to pull rickshaws; his credibility had been lost, and he could no longer rent a rickshaw. He became the caretaker of the small shop. At night, with just two copper coins, you can lie down in the shop. During the day, he did chores that could only make him drink a bowl of porridge. He couldn't beg on the streets; with his size, no one would show him kindness. He wouldn't embellish himself or go to temple fairs to beg for money, because he had never received any teachings and didn't know how to disguise his sores as a tragic misfortune. He doesn't have the skills to be a thief; thieves have their own groups and connections. Only he earns a living for himself, with no other support or dependence. He worked hard for himself, and also died for himself. He waited to take his last breath; he was a dead soul with breath left, and individualism was his soul. This soul will decay along with his body into the soil.

Since Beiping was designated as the ancient capital, its grandeur, craftsmanship, food, language, and patrol ...... It has gradually flowed outward, seeking out places with the same authority and wealth as the Son of Heaven to support him. The Westernized Qingdao also had Beiping's hotpot lamb; Even in the bustling Tianjin, you could hear the mournful 'hard noodles—steamed buns' even in the middle of the night; In Shanghai, Hankou, and Nanjing, there are also patrol officers and constables speaking Beijing dialect, eating sesame paste flatbreads; Xiangpian tea runs from south to north, passing through Shuangxun in Beiping before heading south; Even the handlers would sometimes take trains to Tianjin or Nanjing to carry the coffins of high-ranking officials.

Beiping itself is gradually losing its original grandeur. After the ninth day of the ninth lunar month, you can still buy flower cakes in pastry shops, and those selling yuanxiao might close the market in autumn. Those old shops that have been around for two or three hundred years suddenly think to hold their anniversary and distribute flyers for big discounts...... Economic pressure forces the show to seek other paths; dignity can't be enough to make a living. However, weddings and funerals generally retain the traditional rituals and grandeur; weddings and funerals seem to be worth paying attention to, and require some grandeur. The attendants of weddings and funerals, the instruments, wedding sedan chairs, and official canopies are far beyond what any city can match. Funeral pine cranes and pine lions, paper-carved figures and horses, wedding attendants and attendants, and twenty-four musical instruments—all still display official style in the marketplace, evoking the prosperity and grandeur of that peaceful era.

Xiangzi's life mostly relied on these remaining rituals and rules. If there are married, he holds the flag umbrella for them; For funerals, he holds wreaths and couplets for them; He was displeased and didn't cry; he only accompanied others through the streets for those dozen or so copper coins. Wearing the green or blue robe prepared by the gang house or wedding sedan chair shop, and the inappropriate black hat, he could temporarily cover his tattered cloth and look a bit more dignified. When he met that wealthy family handling affairs and taught everyone to shave their heads and wear boots, he had the chance to keep his head and feet clean and tidy. His illness made it hard for him to walk, so he was holding a banner or two couplets, slowly rubbing along the roadside.

But even doing something like this wasn't really a good thing. His golden age was over; he neither started a family nor built a career in foreign cars, and everything became "just like that" according to his wishes. He was so tall, yet he insisted on waving a Flying Tiger Banner or a pair of short, narrow mourning couplets; He refused to touch the heavier red umbrella and the silence sign, among other things. He would also compete with an elderly person, a child, or even a woman. He refused to suffer even the slightest loss.

Holding such a small thing, he lowered his head, bent his back, and held a cigarette he picked up from the road, weakly rubbing it slowly. When everyone stands still, he might still leave; Everyone else has left, and he stands for a while too; He seemed unable to hear the gong giving the command. He never cares about the distance between front and back, whether the lines are neat or uneven. He walks his way, lowering his head as if dreaming, or pondering some profound truths. The gong driver in red and the steward holding a silk flag almost cursed him in every village dialect: "You brat!" I said, 'What about you, camel!' You damn well follow your example! "He seemed not to have heard it yet. The gong player went over and struck him with a hammer. He rolled his eyes and looked around in a daze. Ignoring what the gong beater said, he carefully searched the ground for any cigarette butts worth picking up. Dignified, ambitious, dreaming, self-interested, individual, robust, great—Xiangzi accompanied others at funerals countless times; No one knows when or where he will bury himself—this degenerate, selfish, unfortunate, the child born in society's sickness, the ghost of individualism's end!
